# Life MDG ERP - Claude Code Configuration

## Project Overview

Life MDG ERP is a Laravel 12 + Vue 3 modular ERP, extracted and trimmed from the WideHalo ERP platform for Life MDG's own launch. It keeps WideHalo's seven founding principles, scoped to the modules Life MDG actually needs to start:

| Principle | Definition |
|---|---|
| **Africa First** | OHADA/SYSCOHADA, mobile money (Orange Money, MTN MoMo), CFA Franc, i18n |
| **Asia First** | Multi-currency/timezone, i18n (10 languages shipped in `lang/`) |
| **API First** | All features exposed as REST API before any UI |
| **Compliance First** | GDPR, PDPL, OHADA, OWASP by design |
| **Simplicity First** | Onboarding wizard (Setup module), smart defaults, AI-assisted import |
| **AI Assisted First** | Claude embedded at every friction point via `AiContextualAssistantService` |
| **Strategy First** | KPI/ratio cockpit, benchmarks, correlations, AI strategic recommendations |

## Scope: 28 modules

Extracted from the WideHalo ERP monorepo (`Widehalo-ERP`, 49 modules), 27 modules at launch. Everything not listed below (Manufacturing, POS, Ecommerce, Email, WhatsApp, Documents, Planning, Quality, PLM, Discussion, Assets, Contracts, SMS, SmartTable, Notes, CustomerService, MarketingAutomation, RealTime, MobileSync, and HR's advanced ATS/performance/training/succession features) was intentionally left out. **`Modules/Messaging` was added for real in Chantier 20** (see the changelog entry below) — real-time internal team messaging, not part of the original 27-module extraction, so it is called out separately here rather than folded into the launch groups above.

**Socle CORE / système (12)**: Core, AI, Security, AuditLog, API, Integration, Validation, Shared, Settings, Setup, Workflow, Calendar
**Compta et Finance**: Accounting
**Commercial et CRM**: CRM, Sales
**Stock et Logistique**: Inventory, Logistics, Achats
**Pilotage et Reporting**: BI, Analytics, Reporting, Strategy
**RH basique**: HR (trimmed — see below), Payroll, Timesheets, Projects
**Support**: Helpdesk (full, coupled to the other modules — see below)
**Communication**: Messaging (real-time internal team messaging — Chantier 20)

### HR: "basique" scope only

`Modules/HR` kept only: `Employee, Department, Position/JobPosition, Attendance*, ShiftSchedule, BiometricDevice, LeaveRequest, LeaveBalance, LeaveType, LeaveApprovalLog, TimeOffRequest, EmployeeDocument, EmployeeSkill/Skill, CompensationHistory, SalaryBand, EmployeeCompensation, Deduction` plus their controllers/services/policies/tests. ATS/recruitment, 360° performance reviews, training catalogue, succession/org chart, advanced benefits, and engagement surveys were removed (not needed for launch) — including a duplicate legacy Payroll implementation that lived inside HR (the dedicated `Payroll` module is the single source of truth for payroll).

### Helpdesk: coupled to every other module

Any record in `Accounting\Invoice`, `CRM\Contact`, `Inventory\Product`, `Sales\SalesOrder`, `Achats\PurchaseOrder`, `Projects\Project`, `Logistics\Shipment`, or `HR\Employee` can raise and list its own tickets via the `Modules\Helpdesk\Traits\HelpdeskLinkable` trait:

```php
$invoice->raiseTicket(['subject' => 'Facture erronée', 'priority' => 'high']);
$invoice->tickets; // tickets linked to this invoice
```

`POST /api/v1/helpdesk/tickets` accepts an optional `source_module`/`source_id` pair (validated against an allowlist morph-map registered in `HelpdeskServiceProvider` — never a raw class name from client input) to link a new ticket to any record. A global "Signaler un incident" button (`resources/js/Components/Helpdesk/QuickTicketButton.vue`) is embedded in `AppLayout.vue` so any authenticated user can raise a ticket from anywhere in the app. To make a new module's model linkable, add the `HelpdeskLinkable` trait and register its morph-map alias.

## Repository Structure

```
/
├── app/                    # Root Laravel app (real entry point — NOT apps/api or apps/webapp, which don't exist here)
├── Modules/                # 27 ERP modules (laravel-modules / nwidart)
├── resources/js/           # Vue 3 + Inertia frontend (real entry: resources/js/app.js)
├── database/
│   ├── migrations/         # Root migrations (schema shared across modules)
│   └── seeders/            # RolesAndPermissionsSeeder is the active RBAC seeder
├── lang/                   # i18n dictionaries: ar, en, es, fr, ha, hi, mg, pt, sw, zh
├── tests/                  # Root Unit/Feature/Integration tests
└── config/, routes/, bootstrap/   # Standard Laravel
```

Each module self-registers its own routes via its `RouteServiceProvider` — `routes/api.php` at the root only holds global routes (health, auth, dashboard, AI chat, admin). Removing a module is `rm -rf Modules/<Name>` plus an entry removed from `config/modules_statuses.json`; no manual route wiring needed elsewhere.

## Development Workflow

**Backend (Laravel API):**
```bash
composer install
cp .env.example .env && php artisan key:generate
php artisan migrate --seed

vendor/bin/pest                  # Run all tests
vendor/bin/pest --filter=TestName
vendor/bin/pint --test           # Code style
```

**Frontend (Vue 3):**
```bash
npm install
npm run dev                      # Start dev server
npm run type-check
npm run lint
npm run build
npm run test                     # Vitest unit tests
```

## Known gaps (not blocking launch, tracked here rather than silently left)

- **`composer.json`** intentionally drops the `widehalo/core`/`widehalo/modules` requires and their `.widehalo-core` path-repositories that Widehalo-ERP carried — nothing in the app code actually imports from that namespace (confirmed by grep across the whole tree before removing it), so it was dead weight.
- **`Dockerfile` now builds cleanly** — the `docker/{supervisor.conf,php.ini,php-fpm.conf,health-check.php}` gap tracked here previously has been closed: `docker/` now contains real infrastructure content (PHP-FPM pool tuned for the container's non-root `appuser`, OPcache/limits tuning, a supervisord fragment launching `php-fpm` in the foreground, and a TCP-connect health check against the exposed port 9000). Confirmed via a real `docker-build.yml` CI run, not just a local lint. The gitignored-lockfile and dead-`.widehalo-core` COPY lines were fixed earlier (matching the same fixes applied to `.github/workflows/`).
- **`composer.lock` / `package-lock.json` are gitignored** (matching Widehalo-ERP's own convention) — run `composer install` / `npm install` fresh (not `npm ci`, which requires a lockfile to already exist rather than generating one — this bit CI too, see the GitHub Actions workflows' commit history); `nunomaduro/larastan` (dev-only, static analysis) may need a working GitHub API connection to install depending on your network — it is not required to run the app or the test suite.
- **Most of what was originally flagged here as "incomplete in the source Widehalo-ERP repo" has since been built for real**, not just documented: multi-company Consolidation now lives behind a single unified `Modules\Accounting\Models\ConsolidationGroup` (the parallel `ConsolidationHierarchy` stack was kept too — it's a genuinely different concept, an ownership tree with periods/eliminations — and wired up rather than deleted); the Budget-variance models (`BudgetActual`, `BudgetAlert`, `BudgetForecast`, `GLEntry`) and Security's compliance-audit models (`ComplianceAudit`, `ComplianceViolation`, `EncryptedField`, `EncryptionKey`, `ServiceIdentity`, `TrustZone`) all reconnected cleanly once the root `App\Models\Company` foundation landed. **ASC606 Revenue Recognition (`RevenueRecognitionEvent`/`RevenueContract`) is the one exception, explicitly excluded rather than built**: it's the US-GAAP five-step revenue-recognition standard (performance-obligation allocation, variable-consideration constraint, contract-liability schedules) — Life MDG keeps its books under OHADA/SYSCOHADA, which has no equivalent concept, so there is nothing to port. `Modules/Accounting/tests/Feature/RevenueContractTest.php`'s 5 failures are this gap, not a regression.
- **Strategy module's `training_roi`/`time_to_fill` HR ratios** return static fallback values (145.0 / 28 days) since they depend on the Training/ATS features that are out of scope — this was already the design pattern used throughout `KPIRegistryService` for any data source that might not exist (every KPI query is wrapped in try/catch with a fallback), so it degrades the same way the rest of the app does when a data source is thin.
- **`Modules/Projects`' wiki feature was removed** (depended on the excluded `Notes` module) rather than decoupled with a stub — it was a self-contained side feature (`ProjectWikiService`/`ProjectWikiController`), not core project management.
- **A security audit surfaced several gaps, now fixed** (see `docs/09-RBAC-SECURITE/STANDARDS-SECURITE-MADAGASCAR.md` for the full state and traceability): `Modules\Core\Services\SecretsService`'s AES-256-CBC secrets vault is backed by a real `Modules\Core\Services\EncryptionService`/`KeyManagementService` pair; the federation endpoints (`/api/v1/federation/*`) are protected by a real `VerifyFederationSignature` middleware (HMAC-SHA256 + anti-replay); mandatory TOTP 2FA roles are config-driven (`config('security.mandatory_2fa_roles')`) rather than hard-coded to admin/super-admin; all controllers extend `App\Http\Controllers\Controller`; `session.security` middleware (device fingerprint/hijack detection) is rolled out across all 27 modules' route groups, not just HR.
- **GraphQL (Lighthouse) is now wired up as a single real schema** — `project/graphql/schema.graphql` (CRM contacts/accounts to start, with `@paginate`/`@guard`/explicit resolver classes) is what `config/lighthouse.php:76`'s `schema_path` actually points at. This replaced a second, parallel "GraphQL v2" stack (`Modules\API\Services\GraphQLSchemaBuilderService`/`GraphQLQueryOptimizerService`/`GraphQLSubscriptionManagerService`) that stored hand-rolled schema fragments in `Cache` and never served a real GraphQL endpoint — those services were kept (74 tests outside the GraphQL-specific file depend on them for non-GraphQL query-optimization/subscription bookkeeping) but no longer duplicate schema-building responsibility.
- **Chantier 9 — dead-weight cleanup: stub/excluded-module tables dropped, declared exclusions actually removed (not just documented)**: the 10-item "deliberately left unbuilt" list that used to live here was audited item-by-item against the real current code before any deletion — several turned out to have been quietly built for real by later chantiers without this list ever being corrected, so blind deletion would have destroyed working features. Final disposition:
  - **Removed entirely** (code + tests + tables, previously carried as documented-but-present dead weight): **Mobile device auth** (`Modules/Core` — `MobileAuthService`/`MobileAuthController`/`MobileAuthServiceTest`/`MobileAuth/*.vue`; confirmed no React Native/Expo app anywhere in this repo, `mobile_sessions`/`mobile_login_logs` tables dropped); **ASC606 Revenue Recognition** (`Modules/Accounting` — turned out to be a *larger* dead cluster than originally documented: beyond `RevenueContract`/`RevenueContractTest`, a second, previously-undocumented broken subtree existed — `RevenueRecognitionSchedule`, `ContractLiability`, `ASC606RevenueRecognitionService` (a real 340-line service, 4 recognition algorithms), 2 async jobs, all backed by real, non-stub tables (`revenue_contracts`, `revenue_recognition_schedules`, `contract_liabilities`) — but `RevenueContract` was bound to the wrong (`acc_`-stub) table, imported a `RevenueRecognitionEvent` class that doesn't exist anywhere in the repo, was missing 3 relations its own controller/policy called, and had zero routes registered anywhere; confirmed genuinely dead via two adjacent integration/edge-case test files already failing on this exact code pre-cleanup — the whole cluster deleted, both real and stub tables dropped); **Skill matrix / competency grid** (`Modules/HR` — `SkillMatrixService` queried nonexistent table names (`employee_skills`/`employees` instead of the real `hr_employee_skills`/`hr_employees`) from day one, zero routes, zero Vue page, zero table ever migrated for it — deleted along with its test; did **not** touch the real, separate, already-working `EmployeeSkill`/`Skill` models or `SkillController::employeeSkills()`); **`MinioService`** (`Modules/Integration` — confirmed zero real callers anywhere and unconfigured storage disks in `config/filesystems.php`; its tests only ever asserted method existence, never real usage — deleted along with its test block).
  - **Corrected as already resolved by a later chantier — nothing deleted, the old exclusion note was simply stale**: **Agent talent management** (`Modules/Helpdesk` — the real feature exists and is fully live: `AgentPerformanceAnalyticsService`/`AgentPerformanceController` (19 routed endpoints — metrics, trend, skills, benchmarking, ranking, coaching recommendations, SMART goals via `PerformanceGoal`, development plans, team comparison) plus `AgentCoachingRecommendation`, all tested and passing; the only genuinely dead thing was a `describe('AgentPerformanceService', ...)` block in `Modules/Helpdesk/tests/Unit/ServiceTests.php` referencing a phantom class that was never built — invisible to every prior test run because that file's name doesn't match Pest's `*Test.php` discovery suffix, so it silently never contributed to any documented failure count; removed just that dead block); **Onboarding funnel telemetry** (`Modules/Setup` — `OnboardingMetricsController`'s full route set is live, gated by `module:Setup`+`role:employee,admin,super-admin` since Chantier 8.5sv, and its test suite passes 25/25; the feature is real per-tenant funnel measurement, not WideHalo's own product-marketing telemetry as the old note framed it); **Pessimistic task locking** (`Modules/Projects` — `TaskConflictResolutionService::acquireLock()`/`releaseLock()`/`getLockInfo()` etc. are real, tested (16/16 passing), and genuinely self-contained (only imports `DB`/`Task`, zero dependency on the excluded RealTime/Discussion modules despite what the old note claimed) — real, working business logic that simply has no controller/route wired to it yet, a "needs wiring" gap for a future pass, not a "delete it" gap).
  - **Confirmed still genuinely excluded, nothing to remove because nothing was ever built**: **FMLA eligibility checking** (`Modules/HR` — the code that had it, `AbsenceManagementService`, was already deleted in Chantier 8.3hp-8a; zero FMLA code exists anywhere today); **Offer-letter generation** (`Modules/HR` — already dropped from `CompensationService`'s scope in the same earlier chantier); **Production-capacity forecasting** (`Modules/Analytics` — `ProductionForecastService::forecastProductionNeeds()` still calls `getDailyCapacity()`/`detectBottlenecks()` against the excluded Manufacturing module's schema (`work_centers`/`manufacturing_orders`/`bom_components`), which doesn't exist in this repo; its own test file carries an explicit "deliberately not tested here" comment — both domain fact and code state confirmed still accurate).
  - **Dead-weight table cleanup, same chantier**: dropped 179 confirmed-dead tables via `database/migrations/2026_09_02_000001_drop_excluded_module_and_collision_stub_tables.php` — tables scaffolded by the catch-all migration for modules that are entirely absent from this repo's disk (Ecommerce, Manufacturing, POS, Documents, WhatsApp, Email, Quality, Planning, MarketingAutomation), plus 7 generic name-collision stub tables with zero owning model (`suppliers`, `purchase_orders`, `product_changes`, `product_specifications`, `product_variants`, `product_versions`, `product_webhooks` — careful to NOT touch the real, differently-named in-scope tables `achats_suppliers`/`achats_purchase_orders`/`inventory_products`). Verification found several candidate tables that looked dead but were actually still live-read by in-scope code and were correctly **kept**: `customers` (real `App\Models\Customer`, used by Accounting), `products`/`purchase_order_lines` (raw-queried by `CostEngineService`/`ProductionForecastService`/`DashboardService`), `documents`/`quality_inspections` (written by registered `Modules\Workflow\Services\Actions\{Documents,Quality}ActionHandler` singletons), `mfg_production_orders` (still queried by `Modules\Strategy\Services\KPIRegistryService` for TRS/defect-rate ratios — this directly **contradicts** the Phase 32 changelog entry claiming "Manufacturing-only ratios were dropped"; flagged here as an unresolved doc/code discrepancy for a future pass, not silently fixed), `sync_queue`/`push_tokens` (wired into Core's real `SyncController`/`SyncService` and the GDPR export job), `workflow_executions` (read by Calendar's `ModuleEventAggregatorService`), `approval_overrides` (written by a different, unrelated `app/Services/AuditLog/ApprovalOverrideService.php`). One genuine post-drop regression was caught by the full test suite and fixed before commit: `Modules\BI\Http\Controllers\Api\AnalyticsController::topProducts()` raw-queried the now-dropped `pos_order_items` (missed by the pre-drop reference check because Laravel's `DB::table('pos_order_items as oi')` aliased-table syntax doesn't match a naive exact-quote grep) — repointed at the real, in-scope `sales_order_lines` instead of restoring the dead POS table.
  - **A third, fully dead parallel workflow/approval engine at the plain `app/` namespace was found and deleted**: `app/Models/{Workflow,Approval,ApprovalChain,WorkflowAuditLog,WorkflowExecution,WorkflowStepLog}.php` + `app/Services/WorkflowEngine.php` — distinct from both `Modules\Workflow` (n8n-like engine, real, routed) and `Modules\Validation` (approval engine, real, routed) — confirmed via exhaustive grep to only ever reference each other, never a route or controller. Its 8 backing tables (`workflows`, `approvals`, `approval_chains`, `workflow_step_logs`, `workflow_conditions`, `approval_requests`, `workflow_triggers`, `workflow_audit_logs`) were dropped in the same migration (`workflow_executions`/`approval_overrides` were kept — see above, both are still live-read by unrelated real code despite the name collision).
  - **Confirmed still-mock, deleted rather than left as a documented gap**: 8 Vue pages previously flagged across earlier chantiers as "100% hardcoded/no backend, left untouched pending a product decision" were re-verified against their current content (not the old note) and deleted along with any dead routes: `Logistics/AIRiskMonitor/Index.vue`, `Logistics/Returns/Index.vue` (the Logistics-specific one — distinct from and did not touch Inventory's real `Rma`-backed `Returns/Index.vue`), `Achats/SupplierRisk/Index.vue` (had a `fetch()` call, but to a nonexistent endpoint, wrapped in try/catch with a faked-success `setTimeout` regardless of outcome — still pure mock), `Sales/CPQ/Index.vue`, `Sales/Subscriptions/Index.vue`, `Workflow/AIWorkflowBuilder/Index.vue`, `Workflow/RPA/Index.vue`, `AI/Agents/Index.vue`. Left untouched: `AI/AiActionAdvisor.vue` (real, routed, just never mounted in nav — a discoverability gap, not mock).
  - **Chantier 9 round 2 — the remaining ~112 "pure stub" in-scope tables audited module-by-module against real models/controllers, not just the round-1 excluded-module/collision list**: 49 more confirmed-dead tables dropped across 3 new migrations (`2026_09_03_000001` Socle, `2026_09_03_000002` Finance+CRM, `2026_09_03_000005` HR+Support — Stock/Logistique and Pilotage groups had zero drop candidates, see below), following the same rule throughout: drop only a table with zero Eloquent model anywhere, or a model that's a confirmed dead duplicate of another real, working model in the same module (matching the session's established dead-parallel-subsystem pattern) — anything else, even a real model with zero current consumers, was deliberately **kept** and flagged as a Category-C future-chantier candidate rather than dropped, since Round 1 already proved several "looked dead" items were quietly-built real features.
    - **Socle group (Core, AI, Security, AuditLog, API, Integration, Validation, Shared, Settings, Setup, Workflow, Calendar)**: only Core and Workflow had any catch-all stub tables at all (AI/Security/AuditLog/API/Integration/Shared/Settings/Setup/Calendar have zero — every table comes from a dedicated real migration). Dropped 3 confirmed model-less tables: `core_encrypted_fields`, `core_key_rotations`, `core_validation_audits`. Kept `core_approval_decisions`/`core_import_rows`/`core_workflow_states` (mis-flagged as pure-stub by the inventory doc — actually already patched with real columns) and `workflow_chain_executions`/`workflow_execution_steps` (real models, real live consumers via `WorkflowChainController`/`WorkflowExecutionController`/`WorkflowEngineService`) — the latter two are flagged as an **active-breakage landmine, not fixed here**: the live code writes/reads columns (`workflow_definition_id`, `trigger_key`, `context_snapshot`, `step_index`, `duration_ms`, etc.) that don't exist on the actual bare stub schema, a guaranteed `QueryException` the moment either path is really exercised — a schema-patch gap for a future chantier, same shape as the many prior stub-column-patch fixes documented throughout this file.
    - **Finance+Commercial group (Accounting, CRM, Sales)**: dropped 33 Accounting tables with zero Eloquent model anywhere (24 never patched beyond the catch-all scaffold, plus 9 that were patched with real purpose-built columns by later `patch_wave*` migrations but still had zero model/controller/service/raw-query consumer at all — the schema investment was never wired to any code) — corrected the doc's `acc_fiscal_years` mis-flag (it's real, backing the live `FiscalYear` model, not dropped). Dropped 1 CRM table, `crm_email_sequence_steps` — a confirmed dead duplicate of the real, live `SequenceStep` model (`EmailSequence::steps()` itself points at `SequenceStep::class`; every real caller goes through that relation, never `EmailSequenceStep`) — its model and factory deleted alongside. Sales had zero catch-all stub tables (confirmed via grep, all 3 Sales tables are dedicated real schema). Kept 11 CRM tables with real, confirmed live consumers.
    - **Stock/Logistique group (Inventory, Logistics, Achats) — zero tables dropped.** All 16 candidate pure-stub tables trace to a real Eloquent model; 13 have confirmed live controller/service consumers, and 3 Achats tables (`achats_po_accounting_mappings`/`achats_po_budget_allocations`/`achats_po_inventory_mappings`) are real, internally-consistent models used by `PurchaseIntegrationService` — already documented elsewhere in this file as a real, unbuilt-controller gap ("8 real methods, zero controller consumers... deserves its own dedicated pass"), kept as Category C rather than dropped. `inventory_po_receipts` was investigated as a possible dead-duplicate of the real PO-receiving flow (`PurchaseOrderService::receive()`) but turned out to model a genuinely different concept (a per-event receiving audit trail) — kept as Category C, not a confirmed duplicate.
    - **Pilotage group (BI, Analytics, Reporting, Strategy) — zero tables dropped.** Analytics/Reporting/Strategy have zero catch-all stub involvement at all (every table from a dedicated migration). BI's 13 catch-all candidates are 12 already-patched-and-live (Chantier 8.2bi) plus one real discrepancy the inventory doc missed: `bi_kpi_history` has zero patches anywhere and a real live consumer (`Kpi::history()`, eager-loaded by the routed `KpiController::show()`) — kept, but flagged as **another active-breakage landmine**: the table's actual columns (`name`/`config`) never match what the model/controller actually read/write (`kpi_id`/`value`/`recorded_at`) — a guaranteed SQL error on `GET /api/v1/bi/kpis/{id}` the first time it's hit with real history data, for a future patch-migration pass.
    - **RH+Support group (HR, Payroll, Timesheets, Projects, Helpdesk)**: Payroll/Projects/Helpdesk had zero pure stubs left (the inventory doc was stale — every candidate in these 3 was already patched by an earlier chantier). Dropped 10 HR tables with zero model or reference anywhere, all matching the already-documented ATS/recruitment/360°-review/training-catalogue exclusion (`hr_appraisal_competencies`, `hr_appraisal_goals`, `hr_course_enrollments`, `hr_interview_schedules`, `hr_interviews`, `hr_job_applications`, `hr_learning_paths`, `hr_recruitment_applicants`, `hr_review_goals`, `hr_training_enrollments`). Dropped 2 Timesheets tables, `timesheets_sheets`/`timesheets_entries` — dangling remnants of the `Timesheet`/`TimeEntry` parallel subsystem already deleted as broken/redundant in Chantier 8.4; `timesheets_entries` had one dangling reader (`Modules\Calendar\Services\ModuleEventAggregatorService::importTimesheetEntries()`) that queried columns never present on the bare stub, already silently no-op'd by its own blanket try/catch, so the drop changes nothing observably. Kept `hr_performance_appraisals` as Category C (zero model, but read by root `app/Services/DashboardService.php`'s `team_performance_avg` widget via a raw query that's always thrown internally and always fallen back to 0 — functionally invisible either way, left alone since auditing root-level `DashboardService.php` is outside this HR/Payroll/Timesheets/Projects/Helpdesk chantier's module boundary).
    - Corrected two more stale inventory-doc entries surfaced during this pass, beyond the ones already listed above: `hr_positions`/`hr_courses`/`hr_recruitment_jobs`/`hr_performance_cycles`/`hr_appraisal_cycles` (mis-flagged as pure-stub, actually already-patched real tables backing live models).
- **Chantier 8.1 (Accounting cross-layer audit) rewiring/cleanup**: a full API/Controller/View/Data audit of `Modules/Accounting` (the largest module, 76+ models) found real end-to-end gaps and fixed the reachable ones — `BankAccount::reconciliationSessions()` relation was missing (would crash `ReconciliationSessionController`), `BankReconciliationService::autoMatchAccount()`/`completeReconciliation()` were missing (the Reconcile.vue "Auto-Match"/"Terminer" buttons had nowhere real to call), `acc_bank_accounts` was missing `gl_account_id`/`iban`/`bic` columns despite `StoreBankAccountRequest` validating them (silently dropped on every create/update, and `BankAccount::glAccount()` crashed `GET bank/{bank}` outright since the column didn't exist at all) — all now routed under `bank-accounts/{bankAccount}/...` and `bank/...`, with `Reconcile.vue`/`BankReconciliation/Index.vue` pointed at the real endpoints/field names (`entry_id` not `journal_entry_id`, `POST bank` not `POST bank-accounts`). Five genuinely redundant controllers (`BalanceSheetController`, `IncomeStatementController`, `CashFlowController`, `LedgerMatchingController`, `OpenBankingController` — all superseded by already-routed `ReportController`/`AccOpenBankingController` methods) and two duplicate dead Vue pages (`Modules/Accounting/resources/js/Pages/{Invoices,Expenses}/Index.vue`, masked by the real root-level copies) were deleted, plus `TransactionAIController`/`BalanceSheetAIController` (functionally duplicated already-routed `AccountingAIController::categorizeTransactions`/`summarizeBalanceSheet`).
- **Chantier 8.1b — the 7 previously-unrouted Accounting controllers were built out into full features, not just routed**: `AssetImpairmentController`, `BudgetVarianceController`, `CostEngineController`, `DepreciationPolicyController`, `DepreciationScheduleController`, `IntercompanyClearanceController`, `ScenarioPlanningController` (the 3 that live directly under `Http/Controllers/`, not `Http/Controllers/Api/`, also got a critical fix: none of them extended `App\Http\Controllers\Controller`, so every `$this->authorize()` call inside them was a fatal `Error: Call to undefined method` waiting to happen the moment they were ever hit). Also fixed: `AssetImpairmentController`/`DepreciationScheduleController` validated against bare table names (`fixed_assets`, `gl_accounts`, `journal_entries`) instead of the real `acc_`-prefixed ones; `IntercompanyClearanceController::index()` called `$q->user()` on a query `Builder` instead of `$request->user()` (fatal error); `DepreciationScheduleController::store()` seeded `book_value` from the annual depreciation amount instead of the asset's `acquisition_cost`; `BudgetVarianceController`'s 6 methods all called `BudgetVarianceService` methods that didn't exist (`analyzeVariance`, `drilldownVariance`, etc.) — rewired onto the service's real, already-tested methods (`calculateVariance`, `varianceReport`, `monthlyTrend`, `topVariances`), plus 2 new small aggregation methods (`varianceByDepartment`/`varianceByCategory`) on the real `Budget::department`/`BudgetLine::category` columns; `ScenarioPlanningService` was an explicit stub (every method returned `implemented:false`) — replaced with real delegation to `BudgetVarianceService::projectScenario()` (already real, used elsewhere), plus a `listScenarios()`/`index()` endpoint the controller was missing entirely. `BudgetPolicy`/`BudgetScenarioPolicy` (new) and the `accounting.asset_impairment.*`/`accounting.depreciation_policy.*`/`accounting.budget.*`/`accounting.budget_scenario.*` permissions (new, `RolesAndPermissionsSeeder`) close the gap where `AssetImpairmentPolicy`/`DepreciationPolicyPolicy` already existed but their permissions were never seeded, so every non-super-admin check silently failed. 7 new Web controllers + Vue pages (`Modules/Accounting/resources/js/Pages/{AssetImpairments,BudgetVariance,CostEngine,DepreciationPolicies,DepreciationSchedules,IntercompanyClearances,ScenarioPlanning}/Index.vue`) — reachable only by direct URL/route name for now (`/accounting/asset-impairments` etc.), matching the existing `consolidation-hierarchies` precedent: this repo has no per-page Accounting sub-navigation yet, and designing one is a separate UX chantier, not a side effect of a routing pass.
- **36 of the 42 `acc_` tables created by the catch-all scaffold migration (`database/migrations/2026_05_29_000003_create_all_missing_module_tables.php`) have no Eloquent model** (stub schema `id/tenant_id/data/timestamps`, never queried by any code) — confirmed by a full audit, correcting the earlier estimate above of "41 of 94". Left as-is: nothing references them, so they're inert scaffold debt rather than a functional gap, and building 36 speculative models with no driving requirement is out of scope.
- **Chantier 8.2 (CRM) rewiring/cleanup**: a full API/Controller/View/Data audit found and closed a real RBAC hole — `CampaignController`/`WorkflowBuilderController`/`RevenueIntelligenceController` lived directly under `Http/Controllers/` (not `Http/Controllers/Api/`), so none `extends App\Http\Controllers\Controller`; `CampaignController`/`WorkflowBuilderController` never called `authorize()` despite `CampaignPolicy`/`WorkflowPolicy` already existing and being correctly written — any authenticated user could create/launch/pause campaigns or workflows regardless of role. Fixed by moving both into `Http/Controllers/Api/`, adding `extends Controller` + the missing `authorize()` calls, and — since the policies checked permission strings (`crm.campaigns.*`/`crm.workflows.*`, non-standard `.edit` verb) that were never seeded by the real `RolesAndPermissionsSeeder` chain — adding a `CRM_EXTRA_PERMISSIONS` block there (mirroring `ACCOUNTING_EXTRA_PERMISSIONS`) so the fix is fail-closed-then-correctly-open rather than fail-closed-for-everyone. A duplicate `crm/opportunity-scores` route registration was removed (kept the one route, dropped the redundant alias). 4 real, already-built Vue pages with no route (`Quotes/Index+Show`, `Territories/Index`, `Forecast/Index`, `Opportunities/Scoring`) got thin Web controllers + routes, matching the Accounting `consolidation-hierarchies` URL-only-discoverability precedent. 5 dead duplicate Vue pages under `Modules/CRM/resources/js/Pages/` (masked by larger, real root-level copies — same pattern as the Chantier 6 cleanup) were deleted. A deeper read (beyond what the initial audit flagged) found `TerritoryManagementController`/`TerritoryManagementService`/`TerritoryQuota`/`TerritoryAlert` were an entire broken, fully redundant parallel subsystem: 6 of the controller's methods called service methods that didn't exist at all, 2 more wrote field names that didn't match the real models' `$fillable`, and one called a nonexistent `Territory::alerts()` relation — while the *real*, tested, already-routed `TerritoryController`+`TerritoryService`+`TerritoryForecastService` already cover every concept it was attempting (rebalance, forecast, at-risk, quotas) via `Territory`'s own native `ytdRevenue()`/`quotaAttainment()`/`quotaForecast()` methods. Deleted the entire broken subtree (controller, service, both models, both factories) rather than repairing it, and added the one genuinely new, non-duplicate concept — a `coverage()` gap-analysis endpoint — as a plain method on the real `TerritoryService`/`TerritoryController` instead (no `authorize()` call, matching the rest of that controller: this module gates by route-level `role:` middleware, not per-method policies — no `TerritoryPolicy` exists).
- **Chantier 8.2 (Helpdesk) rewiring/cleanup**: a full API/Controller/View/Data audit closed a real RBAC hole and a subtler routing bug, then built out one real unwired feature. `CustomerServiceAIPolicy` (46 well-written ability methods covering `CustomerServiceAIController`'s 21 cs-ai endpoints — sentiment/emotion/language analysis, routing rules, escalation predictions, response templates/suggestions, satisfaction/NPS predictions, agent performance/coaching/goals) existed but was never called from the controller and never registered with Laravel's Gate at all (it doesn't map to any single Eloquent model, so neither auto-discovery nor the `AppServiceProvider::$policies` array could find it) — any authenticated Helpdesk-module user could reach all 21 endpoints. Fixed by registering every one of the policy's public methods as its own Gate ability in `HelpdeskServiceProvider::boot()` (`Gate::define($ability, [CustomerServiceAIPolicy::class, $ability])`, looped via `get_class_methods()` rather than hand-listing 46 entries), adding `$this->authorize()` to all 21 controller methods, and seeding the 44 distinct `helpdesk.*` permission strings the policy checks (`HELPDESK_EXTRA_PERMISSIONS` in `RolesAndPermissionsSeeder`, same `_EXTRA_PERMISSIONS` pattern as Accounting/CRM) — `support-admin`/`customer-service`/`admin` already wildcard-match the `helpdesk.` prefix, so no other seeder change was needed. Separately, `Modules/Helpdesk/routes/api.php` registered Knowledge Base category/article CRUD *twice* — once against `KbCategoryController`/`KbArticleController`, again later in the file against `KnowledgeBaseController` — and Laravel's `RouteCollection` keys routes by method+URI in an associative array, so the *second* (later) registration silently wins at dispatch time, not the first; a pre-existing test (`HelpdeskScreensWebTest`) had already caught and documented this. `KbCategoryController` (100% redundant) was deleted outright; `KbArticleController` was trimmed to keep only `suggest()` (its one non-duplicate method); the now-visibly-dead route lines were removed. (This same first-vs-last correction was double-checked against the CRM `opportunity-scores` route fix earlier in this chantier — that one was safe, since `indexScores()` was a pure delegate to `getScores()`.) Also cleaned up: a fully dead scaffold `HelpdeskController` + its blade views (zero routes, zero references), 4 dead duplicate `ChatController` methods already superseded by `LiveChatController` per an existing code comment, an orphaned `HelpdeskAIController::kbChatbot` (never routed; `KbChatbotController::answer` already covers it), and an orphaned `LiveChatWidget.vue` component (genuinely dead — confirmed zero imports anywhere). `resources/js/Pages/Helpdesk/Bot/Widget.vue` (a real, self-contained 196-line standalone chat widget already calling the already-public `AnswerBotController::ask()`/`deflect()` endpoints) turned out to be the opposite case — real functionality never mounted, not dead weight — so it got a public route (`GET /helpdesk/bot/widget`, deliberately outside the `['auth', 'module:Helpdesk']` group since it's designed for anonymous external-site embedding) instead of being deleted. Built a `SlaAutomation/Index.vue` page (+ thin Web controller/route, URL-only discoverability like the Accounting Chantier 8.1b pages) for `SlaController`'s 11 real, migrated, already-live endpoints (`SlaPolicy`/`SlaBreach`/`SlaAutomationService` — `SlaService::apply()` runs on every ticket's creation hook) that had never had a page; confirmed this is a genuinely different concept from `Escalation/Index.vue`'s `HelpdeskSlaPolicy`/`EscalationRule` admin-config pair, not a duplicate, so both stay. Added a migration for 8 models with correct `$fillable`/`$casts` but no table at all (`ConversationAnalytics`, `EscalationHistory`, `EscalationWorkflow`, `ResponseCustomization`, `ResponsePerformance`, `SatisfactionFactor`, `SatisfactionHistory`, `SatisfactionModel`) — dormant today (nothing currently calls the relations that reference them) but a real "table not found" landmine the moment anything does; 3 of the 8 had been explicitly-but-narrowly excluded by the original `cs_*` migration ("not eager-loaded... at the time"), which is a weaker rationale than an intentional scope exclusion, so all 8 were built to match this session's dominant precedent (missing-table-for-existing-model gaps get built, not documented away).
- **Chantier 8.2 (BI) rewiring/cleanup**: a full API/Controller/View/Data audit found the module's active screens actively broken, plus 5 entire subsystems fully written but never reachable. Fixed the active breakage first: the catch-all scaffold migration (`2026_05_29_000003_create_all_missing_module_tables.php`) had left `bi_dashboards`/`bi_reports` and 10 of 13 other `bi_*` tables as bare `id/tenant_id/data/timestamps` stubs, silently crashing 5 real routed pages (`/bi` on `Dashboard::user_id`, `/bi/sql-editor`/`/bi/alerts`/`/bi/data-sources`/`/bi/dashboards/builder` on missing `BiQuery`/`BiAlert`/`BiDataSource`/`Widget` columns) — patched all of them additively (`2026_08_24_000001_patch_remaining_bi_stub_tables.php`) to match each model's real `$fillable`/`$casts`. `BIController` (dead scaffold, zero routes) + its blade views were deleted, matching the Helpdesk/`HelpdeskController` precedent. Given the scale of the remaining gap — 5 fully-written orphan subsystems (`AlertRule`, `DataStory`, `ExternalDataSource`, `ForecastModel`, `CustomVisualization`; each with a real Policy + Controller already calling `authorize()`, but no table, no route, and no seeded permission) — the user was checkpointed via `AskUserQuestion` and chose to build all 5 out fully rather than leave any as documented gaps. Each got its missing tables (7/5/6/7/3 tables respectively, matched to each model's real fields — the AlertRule and ExternalDataSource migrations' docblocks note 2-3 additional supporting tables/relations the agents found and added/documented along the way), a thin Web controller + `Index.vue` page (URL-only discoverability, same precedent as `consolidation-hierarchies`/`SlaAutomation` — except Visualization, where `BiWebController::visualizations()` already existed and rendered the right Inertia component, so only the migration + Vue page were needed, not a second controller), a `Gate::policy()` registration in `BIServiceProvider::registerPolicies()` (none of the 5 policies were auto-discoverable — each policy's class name doesn't match its model's name, e.g. `AlertRule` → `AlertPolicy` not `AlertRulePolicy`), and a `BI_EXTRA_PERMISSIONS` block in `RolesAndPermissionsSeeder` (45 permission strings across 5 `bi.<resource>.*` prefixes — `finance-manager`/`inventory-analyst` already wildcard-match `bi.*`, so no other seeder change was needed). One cross-subsystem migration bug from the parallel build surfaced during verification and was fixed before commit: the Visualization migration made `bi_custom_visualizations.company_id`/`bi_visualization_templates.company_id` `NOT NULL` while the other 4 sibling migrations (written independently, same batch) made the equivalent column `nullable()` — the established pattern in this codebase, since API users in tests/demo data often have no `company_id` — corrected to match.
- **Chantier 8.3 (Inventory + Logistics) rewiring/cleanup, part 1 — RBAC holes**: `Modules/Inventory/routes/api.php` had **zero** module/role gating on every one of its ~120 endpoints — only `auth:sanctum, session.security, tenancy.user` (any authenticated user of any tenant could read/write all inventory data) — fixed by adding `module:Inventory` + `role:employee,logistics-manager,warehouse-operator,purchasing-manager,inventory-analyst,manager,admin` to all 4 top-level route groups (`employee` included deliberately — it is this app's broad "every non-delete permission across every module" role by design, confirmed via the seeder, and excluding it would have broken the pre-existing "any employee can view/create products" test coverage). `Modules/Logistics`'s `CarrierPolicy`/`ShipmentPolicy`/`DeliveryRoundPolicy` were fully written but never invoked by `CarrierController`/`ShipmentController`/`DeliveryRoundController` and never registered with the Gate — fixed with `authorize()` calls throughout plus a `LogisticsServiceProvider::registerPolicies()` (same Modules-namespaced-policies-don't-auto-discover pattern as Core/BI) and a new `LOGISTICS_EXTRA_PERMISSIONS` block (8 `logistics.deliveryround.*` strings — `deliveryround` wasn't in the generic `MODULES['logistics']` resource list). Both fixes broke pre-existing tests for two different reasons, both fixed rather than worked around: 14 Inventory test files (delegated to a background agent, verified independently after) created bare unroled `User::factory()->create()` users that the new gate now rejects — added the seed-guard + `->assignRole('employee')` pattern; 6 Logistics test files called `Role::firstOrCreate(['name' => 'logistics-manager', ...])` directly without ever running `RolesAndPermissionsSeeder`, so the role existed with zero permissions attached — worked fine against route-only gating, broke the moment `authorize()`/Policy checks were added — fixed with the same seed-guard. `Modules/Logistics/tests/Feature/Chantier83LogisticsRbacTest.php` (new, 5 tests) locks in both fixes: a plain `sales-rep` (zero logistics permissions by design — it only gets `crm.*`) is denied creating a carrier/delivery round and deleting a shipment; `logistics-manager` can do all three.
- **Chantier 8.3 (Inventory + Logistics) rewiring/cleanup, part 2 — active breakage**: `/categories`, `/warehouses`, and `/stock-adjustments` 500'd on every visit under their `create`/`show`/`edit` actions (and `/stock-adjustments` on `index` too) — `Modules/Inventory/app/Http/Controllers/Web/{Category,Warehouse,StockAdjustment}Controller.php` rendered `Categories/{Form,Show}.vue`, `Warehouses/{Form,Show}.vue`, and `StockAdjustments/{Index,Form,Show}.vue`, none of which existed anywhere in the repo. `stock-adjustments` was deleted outright rather than built: `Stock/Movements.vue` (already real, already routed at `/stock/movements`) already covers manual stock adjustments end-to-end via `POST stock-movements` with `type: 'adjustment'` against the real `StockMovement` model — the separate resource was 100% redundant scaffolding with no API route behind it at all. `categories`/`warehouses` were real, reachable CRUD APIs with no working page — built `Categories/Index.vue` as a new self-contained list+modal-CRUD page on the exact pattern already used (and already working) by `Warehouses/Index.vue`, then trimmed both Web controllers down to their one real `index()` action, since the modal-based pages call the JSON API directly and never needed the separate create/store/show/edit/update/destroy web routes. Found and fixed two adjacent bugs while building this: `Warehouses/Index.vue` read `data.current_page`/`data.total`/`data.last_page` directly from the paginated API response instead of `data.meta.*` (`WarehouseResource::collection()` on a paginator wraps pagination info under `meta`, Laravel's default) — the page listed correctly but its pager silently never rendered; `CategoryResource`/`Store`/`UpdateCategoryRequest` referenced a `status` field that was never migrated onto `inventory_categories` at all — harmless (nullable, always resolved to `null`) but removed to match the real schema.
- **Chantier 8.3 (Inventory + Logistics) rewiring/cleanup, part 3 — stub-table columns**: the catch-all scaffold migration left 26 `inventory_*` and 12 `logistics_*` tables as bare `id/tenant_id/status/data/timestamps` stubs; most were patched to match their models by earlier passes, but a full model/controller cross-check (`Schema::getColumnListing()` vs each model's `$fillable`, run against a fresh migration) found 11 tables still missing columns their own already-routed controllers actively write — a genuine `QueryException` on every real request, not a hypothetical gap: `inventory_cycle_counts.assigned_to` (`CycleCountController::store`), `inventory_picking_orders.source_id` (`PickingOrderController::store`), `inventory_purchase_orders.expected_at`/`received_at` (`PurchaseOrderController::store`/`receive`), `logistics_carriers.{website,tracking_url_template,api_provider,api_credentials,notes}` and `logistics_carrier_rates.{origin_zone,destination_zone,valid_from,valid_until}` (`CarrierController`/`CarrierRateController`), `logistics_routes`' full geographic field set (`RouteController`), `logistics_shipments.{origin_warehouse_id,destination_warehouse_id,value_currency,estimated_cost,actual_cost,booked_at,picked_up_at,temperature_min,temperature_max,co2_kg}` (`ShipmentService`/`LogisticsAnalyticsService`'s `co2_kg` query), and 4 fully-bare Logistics stubs behind real, routed, real-model controllers (`logistics_freight_invoices`, `logistics_putaway_rules`, `logistics_shipment_lines`, `logistics_shipment_packages`). Two additive patch migrations (`Modules/Inventory/.../2026_08_27_000001_patch_inventory_stub_table_columns.php`, `Modules/Logistics/.../2026_08_27_000001_patch_logistics_stub_table_columns.php`) close all of them, matched field-for-field against real usage rather than guessed from the models alone — `PutawayRuleController` turned out to bypass the `PutawayRule` Eloquent model entirely and write via raw `DB::table()` with a completely different field set (`product_category`/`carrier_id`/`transport_mode`/`requires_cold_chain`/`has_hazmat` vs the model's unused `product_category_id`/`strategy`/`temperature_required`) — the migration matches the controller's real, validated fields, not the model's dead ones. `Lot.created_by`/`LotMovement.created_by`/`PoReceiptLine`'s full field set/`ReorderRule`'s full field set were patched too even though currently orphaned (no controller reaches them yet — `PoReceiptLine`/`ReorderRule` in particular have zero routes), on the same "close the landmine before the next chantier trips it" precedent as the Chantier 8.2 (Helpdesk) 8-model migration. A second, deeper bug surfaced while writing the regression test for shipment lifecycle transitions: `ShipmentController::book()`/`dispatch()`/`deliver()`/`cancel()` had `ShipmentService` injected but never called it — they set `status` directly instead, so `booked_at`/`picked_up_at`/the shipment's tracking-event trail were silently never written even after the schema fix, and `dispatch()`'s hardcoded `status: 'dispatched'` was actually the wrong vocabulary for this model (`'dispatched'` belongs to the separate, unrelated `LgxShipment` — this module's real `ShipmentFactory`/`TrackingEventController`/`TrackingEventFactory` all use `'picked_up'`). Fixed by delegating all four methods to the already-written, already-correct `ShipmentService` methods.
- **Chantier 8.3 (Inventory + Logistics) rewiring/cleanup, part 4 — 3 missing Logistics tables**: `DeliveryRoute`/`RouteStop`/`CarrierRateCard` are real, fully-written models (relations, business logic, `HasAuditLog`) already used by live, routed code — `RouteOptimizationService` behind `CustomsRouteController`'s `optimize`/`start`/`complete`/`routeStopComplete` endpoints, and `CarrierIntegrationService::getRate()` behind the carrier rate-lookup endpoint — but `lgx_delivery_routes`/`lgx_route_stops`/`lgx_carrier_rate_cards` had no migration at all, so every one of those endpoints 500'd with "table not found" the moment a route/rate-card record existed. `CustomsRouteController::routesIndex()`/`routesStore()` (the only endpoints that would create a `DeliveryRoute`) are already documented as deliberately unrouted elsewhere in this file (collide with the real `RouteController::index`/`store` on the same path) — the fix here is scoped to the schema gap behind the endpoints that ARE reachable, not to un-collide the dead create path. New migration (`2026_08_27_000002_create_lgx_delivery_routes_and_carrier_rate_cards.php`) follows the same plain-`id()`/`company_id` convention as the sibling `lgx_vehicles` table rather than the generic `tenant_id`/`status`/`data` scaffold used elsewhere in this module. Writing the regression test surfaced a second, unrelated bug: `DeliveryRouteFactory`/`RouteStopFactory`/`CarrierRateCardFactory` were boilerplate scaffold output — generic filler fields (`title`, `slug`, `email`, `phone`, `amount`, `cost`, …) that don't exist on any of these 3 models, plus `fake()->word()` used for date/numeric fields (producing garbage like `'ipsa'` for a `date` column) — silently dead until the tables above existed to expose them. Rewrote all three to match their real `$fillable`/`$casts`.
- **Chantier 8.3 (Inventory + Logistics) rewiring/cleanup, part 5 — dead routes**: 7 Inventory routes pointed at controller methods that didn't exist — a fatal "call to undefined method" on every request, not a hypothetical gap. `demand-forecasts/expiring` (deleted — `DemandForecast` has no expiry concept at all, a copy-paste artifact of the real, working `lots/expiring`), `picking-orders/{id}/record` and `cycle-counts/{id}/record` (both deleted — 100% redundant with the already-real, already-routed `picking-orders/{id}/lines/{lineId}/pick`/`cycle-counts/{id}/lines/{lineId}/count`), `barcodes/lookup` (deleted — redundant with the already-routed `barcode/product/{barcode}`/`barcode/location/{barcode}`), `seasonal-factors/upsert` (deleted — `SeasonalFactorController::store()` already does `updateOrCreate()`, i.e. it's already an upsert), and — found while fixing the barcode route — `Route::apiResource('barcodes', BarcodeController::class)` registering `index`/`show`/`store`/`update`/`destroy`, none of which exist on that controller and none of which make sense (a barcode isn't a standalone CRUD resource in this design, it's an attribute of a product/location; also deleted). `picking-orders/next` was the one genuine gap — no "get the next order to pick" queue-pop existed anywhere — added a small `PickingOrderController::next()` (plain `where(status: pending, assigned_to: null)->orderBy(priority)->first()`, no invented business logic beyond a query already following this controller's own `index()` filtering style).
- **Chantier 8.3 (Inventory + Logistics) rewiring/cleanup, part 6 — dead code deletion**: `Modules\Inventory\Http\Controllers\InventoryController` (dead scaffold, zero routes, 4 `view('inventory::*')` calls to blade templates that were never a real UI in this Inertia-based app — deleted alongside its 2 blade views, matching the `HelpdeskController`/`CoreController`/`BIController` precedent) and `Modules\Inventory\Http\Controllers\Web\ProductWebController` (100% redundant — the real, routed `ProductController::index()` already renders the same `Inventory/Products/Index` page with the same `products`/`filters` props) were deleted. On the Logistics side, confirmed and removed the entire dead `wh_*`/`lgx_*` parallel subsystem flagged since Chantier 8.3's own regression fix (`WarehouseShipmentControllerTest.php`'s docblock already documented this, written by an earlier pass): `WarehouseShipmentController`, `WarehouseService`, `LgxShipmentService`, and the `Warehouse`/`WarehouseZone`/`WarehouseMovement`/`WarehouseLocation`/`WarehousePutAwayRule`/`LgxShipment`/`LgxCarrier`/`LgxTrackingEvent`/`LgxShipmentItem` models (+ their factories) — a fully self-contained, zero-route, zero-external-reference subtree that collides on URL with the real, routed `ShipmentController`/`CarrierController`. Also deleted `Route.php` (a byte-for-byte duplicate `logistics_routes`-table model of the real, used `LogisticsRoute`, zero references anywhere) and `CustomsItem.php` (orphaned, backed by a table — `lgx_customs_items` — that was never migrated, zero references). 6 Logistics module Vue pages (`Analytics`, `Carriers`, `Customs`, `DeliveryRounds`, `FreightInvoices`, `Shipments`) were masked by root-level `resources/js/Pages/Logistics/<Name>/Index.vue` copies that `LogisticsWebController` actually renders (`resolve()`'s root-first priority, per the Chantier 6 `app.js` fix) — confirmed dead rather than requiring a Helpdesk-`Tickets/Show`-style merge by checking that the root pages already make real `fetch()` calls to real endpoints while the module pages made none at all (relied on Inertia props the real controller never fully supplies) and spot-checking functional parity (both have approve/dispute on FreightInvoices, etc.) before deleting. `CustomsRouteController::routesIndex`/`routesStore` (also flagged as "dead" by an earlier pass's code comment) turned out on closer read to operate on the real `DeliveryRoute` model this chantier just gave a table (see the missing-tables entry above) — kept rather than deleted, since deleting it would remove the only way to ever create a `DeliveryRoute` via the API; left for a future pass to resolve its URL collision with `RouteController::store`.
- **Chantier 8.3 (Inventory + Logistics) rewiring/cleanup, part 7 — orphaned features wired**: `InventoryWebController`'s 8 methods (`suppliers`, `purchaseOrders`, `picking`, `cycleCounts`, `shipments`, `returns`, `crossdock`, `waves`) were real — correct props matching each target page's `defineProps` exactly — but had zero web routes anywhere in `Modules/Inventory/routes/web.php`. 4 of the 8 (`Shipments`, `Returns`, `WMS/Crossdock`, `WMS/Waves`) turned out to be fully self-contained axios-fetch pages that never read server props at all, so those were routed via a plain `Inertia::render()` closure (matching the existing `stock/movements` precedent) rather than running the controller's real-but-wasted queries against them; the other 4 (`Suppliers`, `PurchaseOrders`, `WMS/Picking`, `CycleCounts`) were routed directly to their `InventoryWebController` methods. `ChannelController` (`MarketplaceChannel`/`marketplace_channels`, Amazon/eBay connect+sync+status — a genuinely distinct concept from the already-routed `EcommerceSyncController`, which pushes products to an already-connected storefront rather than managing the connection itself) and `UnitController` (units-of-measure CRUD) were both real, fully-written API controllers with zero routes; wired up at `channels*`/`units*` under the same `role:employee,...` group as the rest of `Modules/Inventory/routes/api.php`. A new self-contained `Channels/Index.vue` page (list + "Connect Channel" modal + per-row sync button, same axios-fetch pattern as `Categories/Index.vue`) was built for `ChannelController`, since no page existed for it at all — reachable only by direct URL/route name (`/inventory/channels`), matching the `consolidation-hierarchies`-style discoverability precedent used throughout this chantier. `BarcodeController::lookupProduct`/`lookupLocation`/`stockMovement` (real, distinct from the dead `barcodes/lookup` deleted in part 6) were also given their real routes (`barcode/product/{barcode}`, `barcode/location/{barcode}`, `barcode/stock-movement`). On the Logistics side, `RouteOptimizationController::optimize()`/`result()` were already real and API-routed, and `RouteOptimization/Index.vue` is a real self-fetch page calling them — it only needed a web route, added as a thin `Inertia::render()` closure the same way as Inventory's self-fetch pages. **Two Logistics pages were investigated and deliberately left unrouted**: `AIRiskMonitor/Index.vue` (AI supply-chain risk scoring — port-strike/supplier/currency alerts) and `Returns/Index.vue` (a Logistics-specific reverse-logistics/RMA workflow, distinct from and redundant-looking with Inventory's own real `Rma`-backed `Returns/Index.vue`) are both **100% hardcoded mock data** — no `defineProps`, no `axios`/`fetch` call anywhere in either file — with no backing service, model, or table of any kind behind them. Unlike every other Category-C item in this chantier, wiring these up would mean inventing an entire AI risk-scoring engine and a parallel RMA subsystem from scratch rather than connecting an existing one, which falls outside this chantier's "build real features, don't invent business logic" mandate — left as a documented gap pending a product decision (build for real, or delete as speculative scaffolding — Inventory's `Rma`-backed Returns page already covers reverse logistics for the one warehouse-level concept that has a real backend).
- **Chantier 8.3 (HR) rewiring/cleanup, part 1 — RBAC hole, PII leak, LeaveRequest authz bug, active breakage, dead code, orphaned features (parts 1-6)**: a full API/Controller/View/Data audit of `Modules/HR` found a live PII leak — `EmployeeSelfServiceController::me()`/`updateMe()` and `EmployeePortalController::profile()` returned raw `response()->json($employee)`, auto-decrypting and serializing `bank_details_encrypted`/`national_id`/`passport_number` in full — fixed with a new `SelfServiceEmployeeResource` exposing only `masked_bank_details` (via the already-existing `BankDetailsMaskingService` accessor) and omitting the two ID fields entirely. `Modules/HR/routes/api.php` had zero `module:HR`/`role:` gating on its ~50 endpoints (same hole as Inventory/Logistics) — fixed with the same `module:HR` + `role:employee,hr-manager,payroll-officer,manager,admin` pattern, plus new `DepartmentPolicy`/`JobPositionPolicy`/`LeaveTypePolicy`/`SalaryBandPolicy`/`SkillPolicy` (none of the 5 controllers had a Policy class at all) registered in `AppServiceProvider`. `LeaveRequestPolicy::update()` had the same `$model->employee_id` (an `hr_employees.id`) vs `$user->id` (a `users.id`) ID-space bug documented elsewhere in this file for Security's `company_id` mismatch — an employee could never pass `authorize('update', ...)` on their own leave request; fixed by comparing `$model->employee?->user_id` instead. `LeaveRequestController::approve()`/`reject()` had zero `authorize()` calls at all — any authenticated employee could approve/reject anyone's leave — fixed with `authorize('approve', ...)` (role-gated: manager/hr-manager/admin only). Active breakage fixed: the HR dashboard rendered a nonexistent `hr::dashboard` Blade view (404 on every visit) — now `Inertia::render('HR/Dashboard')`; `Leave/Analytics.vue` was 100% hardcoded/`Math.random()` mock data with no backing endpoint — built a real `HrDashboardService::getLeaveAnalytics()` + `GET dashboard/leave-analytics` off the same days-per-year-minus-taken formula already used by the self-service leave-balance endpoint. Dead scaffold code deleted (Category B): `HRController` + its blade views, dead duplicate `Employees/{Index,Show}.vue`/`Leaves/RequestForm.vue` masked or superseded by root-level pages, an orphaned `EmployeeNode.vue` component, and a dead duplicate root-level `EmployeePolicy` stub (the real one is `Modules\HR\Policies\EmployeePolicy`, already correctly registered). Orphaned real features wired: `SalaryBandController::equityAnalysis()`/`SkillController::employeeSkills()` had no route despite `Compensation/Index.vue` already calling the former by URL; `EmployeeWebController::create()`/`edit()`/`portal()` and self-fetching `departments`/`leaves`/`attendance/manage` pages had no web route; the real admin `Attendance/Index.vue` (CRUD) was masked by the root-level personal-clock-in page of the same component name — renamed to `Attendance/Manage.vue` to resolve the collision. Fixed two bugs found while wiring: `SalaryBandController::equityAnalysis()` passed a string where `AIService::ask()` requires an array `$context` (a fatal TypeError the first time this never-before-routed method was ever hit) and had no try/catch fallback, breaking the "AI features degrade gracefully" principle — both fixed. `Departments/Index.vue` was rewritten off orphaned `parent_id`/`is_active` columns (the raw columns exist in the DB from an early migration but were never in `Department`'s `$fillable`/relations — no `parent()` relation exists at all) to use the model's real `status` field and flat list instead of a tree hierarchy the backend doesn't support. `EmployeeSkillFactory` was scaffold boilerplate (`fake()->word()` for FK/date/enum columns) — rewritten to match `EmployeeSkill`'s real `$fillable`; a missing `hr_employee_skills.expires_at` column (referenced by the model/controller, never migrated) was added.
- **Chantier 8.3 (HR) rewiring/cleanup, part 2 — AttendanceBiometricController (13 methods) wired for the first time**: a distinct, additive subsystem from the already-working `AttendanceController` (simple self clock-in/out) — biometric device management, attendance verification workflow, exception handling, shift scheduling, time-off requests, and analytics — was fully written (controller + `AttendancePolicy`, 13 abilities) but had zero routes, 6 missing tables (`BiometricDevice`, `TimeOffRequest`, `AttendanceException`, `AttendanceAnalytics`, `CompensationHistory`, `Deduction` — all real models, no table at all), and an unregistered policy (Modules-namespaced policies don't auto-discover; `AttendancePolicy` backs 5 different models so it needed 5 explicit `Gate::policy()` calls in a new `HRServiceProvider::registerPolicies()`, not just one). New migration adds all 6 tables plus patches `hr_attendance_records` with columns the controller actively writes but the model never declared (`device_id`, `clock_in_method`, `device_name`, `verification_status`, `break_minutes`, `notes`, `location_lat`, `location_lng`) — `AttendanceRecord` also had no `clockOut()`/`verify()` methods at all despite the controller calling both (`BadMethodCallException` waiting to happen) and no `device()` relation despite `listAttendance()` eager-loading one; all added. Fixed a real bug found while wiring: `clockIn()`'s `$validated` array used keys `latitude`/`longitude` but the model's real columns are `location_lat`/`location_lng` — silently dropped on every clock-in even after the schema fix, since the key names never matched; fixed by mapping explicitly. Routed under new `biometric-devices`/`attendance-records`/`attendance-exceptions`/`shifts`/`time-off-requests`/`attendance-analytics` prefixes (not `attendance/*`, already used by the unrelated `AttendanceController`) with `authorize()` calls added to `clockIn`/`clockOut`/`createShift`/`requestTimeOff` (previously uncovered) and a new `HR_EXTRA_PERMISSIONS`-block addition (13 `hr.attendance.*` strings). 5 factories (`BiometricDeviceFactory`, `TimeOffRequestFactory`, `AttendanceExceptionFactory`, `ShiftScheduleFactory`, `AttendanceRecordFactory`) were scaffold boilerplate (`fake()->word()` on FK/date/enum columns, dozens of dead fields not on any of these models) — rewritten to match each model's real `$fillable`/`$casts`.
- **Chantier 8.3 (HR) rewiring/cleanup, part 8 — the 4 large orphaned HR services checkpoint**: `CompensationService`, `AbsenceManagementService`, `AdvancedAttendanceService`, and `EmployeeManagementService` (~1,571 lines combined, zero controller consumers) were audited individually rather than treated as one batch, since a closer read found they fell into genuinely different categories — the user was checkpointed via `AskUserQuestion` on the split below and confirmed all three decisions.
  - **`AbsenceManagementService`/`AdvancedAttendanceService` were deleted outright, not built out** — both turned out to be broken/redundant parallel subsystems, the same pattern as the CRM `TerritoryManagementController`/Logistics `wh_*`/`lgx_*` dead subtrees found earlier in this session. `AbsenceManagementService` duplicated the already-live, already-tested `LeaveController`/`LeaveRequestController`/`EmployeeSelfServiceController::leaveBalance()` leave-management flow with a hardcoded 7-type US-style `LEAVE_TYPES` array (vacation/sick/unpaid/maternity/paternity/bereavement/jury) and an explicit FMLA-eligibility check — the same US-federal-employment-law concept already documented above as excluded — instead of the real, configurable `LeaveType` model. `AdvancedAttendanceService` was worse: full of literal `// Placeholder — would get from...` comments and hardcoded fake values (`$isEnrolled = true`, `$isMatched = true`, a hardcoded 09:00–17:00 schedule ignoring the real `ShiftSchedule` model, a hardcoded $50/hour rate ignoring real compensation), and its `AttendanceRecord`/`LeaveRequest` writes used column names (`clock_in_time`, `leave_start_date`) that don't exist on either model at all — a `QueryException` waiting to happen the moment either method was ever called, not a hypothetical gap. Its isolated unit test (`AbsenceManagementTest.php`) was deleted alongside the service; the `LeaveRequest::booted()` hook the service depended on (auto-resolving a `leave_type_id` FK from a plain `leave_type` string) was left in place as harmless, still-real defensive coverage for any future string-only caller, with its comment updated to no longer reference the deleted service. The `hr_leave_balances` table (real, migrated, still queried directly by `Modules\Analytics\Services\Forecasting\HrForecastService`) is left orphaned with nothing populating it now that its only writer is gone — a real, if minor, latent gap, tracked here rather than silently patched over, since fixing Analytics' assumption is outside this HR chantier's scope.
  - **`EmployeeManagementService` and `CompensationService` were built out, with the offer-letter generation method dropped from the former's decision (`generateOfferLetterData()`/`getBenefitsDetails()` on `CompensationService`, not `EmployeeManagementService`)** — a recruitment artefact issued before someone is an employee, the same ATS/recruitment exclusion already documented above for HR's "basique" scope; its own isolated test in `CompensationServiceTest.php` was removed accordingly. `EmployeeManagementService::onboardEmployee()`/`getEmployeeProfile()`/`updateEmployee()`/`getEmployeesByDepartment()` referenced `Employee` columns that don't exist (`department`/`position`/`salary` — real columns are `department_id`/`job_position_id` FKs, and salary lives on `EmployeeCompensation`, not `Employee`) — fixed the former two; `updateEmployee()`/`getEmployeesByDepartment()` were dropped entirely as 100% redundant with the already-live, already-routed `EmployeeController::update()`/`byDepartment()`. A pre-existing isolated test (`HRServicesTest.php`) that exercised the broken field names was fixed to use real `Department`/`JobPosition` factories rather than reverted. New `EmployeeManagementController` routes the onboarding/completion/profile/termination workflow (`employees/onboard`, `employees/{employee}/complete-onboarding`, `/profile`, `/terminate`) — `terminate()` reuses the `update` ability rather than `EmployeePolicy::archive()`, since `hr.employee.archive` was never seeded as a real permission (matching the identical, already-documented workaround in `EmployeeController::export()`'s own code comment). `CompensationService` (base salary/bonus/benefits/equity-vesting tracking) was already real and schema-correct — just unrouted; new `CompensationController` routes it under `employees/{employee}/compensation/*` + `compensation/audit`, distinct from `SalaryBandController`'s band-level equity analysis. `EmployeeCompensationFactory` was scaffold boilerplate (`fake()->word()` on every typed column) — rewritten to match the model's real `$fillable`/`$casts`.
  - **`Shifts/Schedule.vue` backend-concept mismatch resolved by adapting the frontend, not inventing new business logic** (the third checkpointed decision): the page wanted a per-date monthly shift-assignment calendar, but the only real backend (`ShiftSchedule`, behind the `shifts` endpoints wired in part 2 above) models recurring weekly templates (`days_of_week` + `effective_from`/`effective_to`), with no per-date instance concept at all — building a template-to-calendar-instance expansion engine would have been inventing new business logic, the one thing this chantier's standing policy defers to the user rather than building silently. Rewrote the page as a template list + assign-shift dialog matching `ShiftSchedule`'s real shape instead. While wiring it, found and fixed two adjacent real bugs: `AttendanceBiometricController::createShift()`'s validation was missing `is_flexible`/`effective_to`/`notes` — all real `ShiftSchedule` `$fillable` columns the controller's own `ShiftSchedule::create($validated)` call would have silently dropped on every request; and the model's `$casts` had no `boolean` cast for `is_night_shift`/`is_flexible` at all, so both round-tripped as raw `0`/`1` instead of real booleans — both fixed.
- **Chantier 8.3 (Payroll) rewiring/cleanup, part 1 — RBAC gaps**: `Modules/Payroll/routes/api.php`'s route group listed `hr-manager,accountant,finance-manager,manager,admin` but not `payroll-officer` — the role literally named for and seeded with full `payroll.*` permissions was locked out of the entire module at the outer route gate, before any of its permissions were ever checked. `PayrollController::statistics()`/`taxesByCountry()` had no `authorize()` call at all, unlike every other method on the controller (`index`/`generate`/`approveBatch`/`processPayment`, all gated on `payroll.payslip.view`/`.generate`/`.approve`) — `accountant`/`finance-manager` (accounting/bi/strategy permissions only, no `payroll.*`) could read payroll aggregates and tax breakdowns by passing only the route-level role gate. Fixed by adding `payroll-officer` to the route group and the same `abort_unless($request->user()->can('payroll.payslip.view'), 403)` check already used elsewhere in the controller to both methods.
- **Chantier 8.3 (Payroll) rewiring/cleanup, part 2 — the headline silent-zero-salary bug**: `PayrollIntegrationService::calculateSalaryComponents()`/`calculatePerformanceAllowance()`/`calculateOvertime()` all read `base_salary`/`monthly_salary` straight off `Employee`, and `generatePayslip()`/`generatePayslips()` read `Employee::tenant_id`/`salary_currency` the same way — but none of these are real `Employee` `$fillable` fields (confirmed via `Schema::getColumnListing('hr_employees')`: the raw columns physically exist in the DB, left over from an earlier schema, but are absent from the model's `$fillable`, so mass-assignment silently drops them on every real write). No real write path in this app (`EmployeeController::store()`, `EmployeeManagementService::onboardEmployee()`) has ever populated them, so every payslip generated through the real UI/API silently computed to a near-zero salary — the existing `PayrollIntegrationServiceTest.php` only passed because Laravel factories bypass `$fillable`/`$guarded` entirely via `Model::unguarded()`, exercising a code path production code can never reach. The real salary source is `EmployeeCompensation` (already used by `Modules\HR\Services\CompensationService`, built in part 8 above) — added a `getCurrentCompensation(Employee, Carbon $asOf)` helper (mirroring `CompensationService`'s own query shape, but resolved against the payslip period's start date rather than `now()`, correct for retroactive generation) and rewired `base_salary`/`currency`/the performance-allowance and overtime hourly-rate calculations onto it. The real tenant source is `users.tenant_id` (a genuinely live column, read by `App\Http\Middleware\InitializeTenancyFromAuthenticatedUser` to initialize `stancl/tenancy` — confirmed via that middleware's own docblock: this app uses a shared-DB + `tenant_id` scoping model) — `hr_employees.tenant_id` is a third, phantom column, referenced nowhere in live HR code (only in the already-documented, pre-existing `SkillMatrixServiceTest` failure cluster); `generatePayslips()`'s employee query no longer filters by it at all (matching `EmployeeController::index()`, the real employee-listing endpoint, which has no tenant filter either), and `generatePayslip()`'s tenant id now comes from the caller or falls back to the employee's linked `User::tenant_id`. The granular allowance/bonus sub-categories (`housing_allowance`, `transport_allowance`, `family_allowance`, `monthly_bonus`) are left reading the same phantom `Employee` fields, deliberately — unlike `base_salary`, they have no real per-employee data source anywhere in this app (only a single aggregate `bonus_amount`/`benefits_annual_value` exists on `EmployeeCompensation`) — left at their existing `?? 0` fallback rather than guessing a split, the same fallback-first pattern used elsewhere (e.g. Strategy's `training_roi` ratio). Fixed the pre-existing `PayrollIntegrationServiceTest.php` to build a real `EmployeeCompensation` fixture and a `User` with a real `tenant_id` instead of relying on the factory-unguarded bypass; added `Chantier83PayrollZeroSalaryTest.php` locking in the real behavior (non-zero payslip from a real compensation record, correct tenant-independent employee lookup, graceful zero-not-crash for an employee with no compensation record at all).
- **Chantier 8.3 (Payroll) rewiring/cleanup, part 3 — overtime data source + PayrollPolicy**: `calculateOvertime()` read `hr_timesheets.overtime_hours` and `calculateLoanRepayment()` read `hr_employee_loans.monthly_installment` — both dead stub tables from an early scaffold migration with zero writers anywhere in this app, so both always silently returned 0. Overtime now derives from real, live time-tracking data (`Modules\Timesheets\TimesheetEntry.hours_worked`, approved entries only) — hours beyond the 160h/month standard already implied elsewhere in this same service's hourly-rate divisor count as overtime, since `TimesheetEntry` has no distinct overtime flag of its own to read directly. Loan repayment has no real per-employee data source anywhere in this app at all (confirmed via a repo-wide grep — no model, controller, or UI ever writes a loan record) — building a full loan-management feature from scratch is out of scope for this bug-fix chantier, so it's left at its existing safe 0 fallback, documented rather than silently left. Separately, `PayrollPolicy` (an "employee can view their own payslip" ability, among others) had two real bugs and was never registered with the Gate or called from any controller at all: it checked a `payroll-manager` role that doesn't exist anywhere in `RolesAndPermissionsSeeder` (the real role is `payroll-officer`), and its own-payslip check compared `$model->employee_id` (an `hr_employees.id`) directly against `$user->id` (a `users.id`) — the same ID-space mismatch bug pattern already fixed in `LeaveRequestPolicy` and Security's policies elsewhere in this file, meaning an employee could never actually pass the check on their own payslip. Fixed both bugs, registered `Gate::policy(Payslip::class, PayrollPolicy::class)` in a new `PayrollServiceProvider::registerPolicies()` (Modules-namespaced policies don't auto-discover, same precedent as Core/BI/HR), and — since the "own payslip" ability had no controller consumer anywhere, not even a frontend call site waiting for it — added the small, real self-service endpoints it was clearly written for: `GET payroll/me/payslips` (an employee's own payslip list) and `GET payroll/payslips/{payslip}` (single-payslip view, `authorize('view', $payslip)`), both under a plain `auth:sanctum` group (not the payroll-staff `role:` group, since any employee should reach their own payslips).
- **Chantier 8.3 (Payroll) rewiring/cleanup, part 4 — StatutorySchemes wired into live tax/social-security calculation**: `Modules\Payroll\Data\StatutorySchemes` (8 real African statutory schedules — SN/CI/CM/MG/BJ/TG/BF/ML, real progressive income-tax brackets/abatements/surcharges/minimums and real social-scheme employee/employer rates + monthly ceilings, sourced from the actual national tax codes) existed fully written but had zero consumers anywhere in this app — `calculateIncomeTax()`/`calculateSocialSecurity()`/`calculatePensionContribution()` used crude flat-rate/single-threshold approximations for only 4 countries (SN/CI/CM/NG) instead. Wired all three onto the real dataset: `calculateIncomeTax()` now runs a real progressive-bracket calculation (`calculateProgressiveIncomeTax()`, new) — abatement (rate, capped where the country defines a cap) reduces the taxable base, marginal brackets apply, then any flat surtax/CAC-style surcharge/statutory minimum (MG's real IRSA `minimum_tax` floor, CM's real 10% CAC surcharge) — for any of the 8 real countries; NG (not in the real dataset) and any other unsupported code keep the old flat-rate approximation as an explicit fallback rather than guessing new rates. `calculateSocialSecurity()`/`calculatePensionContribution()` sum real scheme employee-rates (respecting each scheme's own real ceiling) via a new `sumSchemeContributions()` helper — since the underlying data models contribution schemes as separately-named entries per country (not a clean universal pension-vs-social-security split: SN/CI/CM/MG each split into 2 named schemes, BJ/TG/BF/ML each have one combined scheme covering everything), added a `category` field (`pension`/`social_security`/`health`/`combined`) to every scheme entry in `StatutorySchemes.php` itself — categorizing already-real data, not inventing new rates — so the two public methods can split without double-counting: `calculatePensionContribution()` sums `pension`-category schemes only (0 for combined-scheme countries, since there's no real split to report), `calculateSocialSecurity()` sums everything else including `combined` (so a combined-scheme country's full contribution is still reported through this bucket, matching the old code's own convention of treating social_security as the general figure). Fixed the pre-existing `PayrollIntegrationServiceTest.php`'s SN/CI/CM/NG tax assertions, which asserted the old crude approximation's values (in some cases even the opposite comparison direction — SN's real progressive brackets now tax more than CI's at 500k gross, the reverse of the old flat-rate ordering) — replaced with values confirmed against the service's own live calculation. Added `Chantier83PayrollStatutorySchemesTest.php` (5 tests: MG's real minimum-tax floor, BF's real 7-bracket schedule reconstructed independently and compared, the NG/unsupported-country fallback, no double-counting on a combined-scheme country, and a real ceiling capping the contribution base).
