`employees` field onto the model's real `employee_count` column). `Opportunities/Kanban.vue`/`Opportunities/CreateForm.vue` (both real, self-fetching, call real already-routed `/api/v1/crm/pipelines`/`/api/v1/crm/opportunities` endpoints) got thin `Inertia::render()` closure routes. `Duplicates/Index.vue` was investigated and **left as a documented gap, not built, not deleted**: a real, orphaned `DuplicateDetectionService::detectDuplicates(Contact, threshold)` exists, but it's a per-contact AI-embedding similarity check (O(n) embedding calls against every other contact for one contact) — building the page's actual "list every duplicate pair across the whole database" concept on top of it would mean either an O(n²) embedding-call batch scan (needs a new queued/incremental scanning job, none exists) or a persisted pairs-to-review table (none exists), plus a "merge two contacts" operation with no defined field-conflict/relation-reassignment policy anywhere in the codebase — genuinely new infrastructure and business rules, not a wiring job, so left untouched rather than half-built. The real underlying service was still given a small, real, cheap consumer it never had: a per-contact `GET crm/contacts/{contact}/duplicates` endpoint (see below).
  - **CRM — `EinsteinForecastingService` was an undocumented silent stub, now mostly real**: all 6 methods returned `['implemented' => false, ...]` despite being live behind 6 real, RBAC-gated, routed endpoints (`EinsteinForecastingController` — `crm/einstein-forecasting/*`). Investigated `CRMForecastingService` (the module's real, tested 30+-method forecasting service) as a wiring target rather than defaulting to "document and leave stubbed": `analyzeConfidence()`/`calculateForecastMetrics()` wired onto its real `calculateConfidence()`/`getForecastAccuracy()`/`getHistoricalWinRate()`/`getAverageDealVelocity()`; `generateWeightedForecast()` reshapes `CRMForecastingService::generateForecast()`'s real probability-weighted pipeline/commit/best-case/AI-prediction output (that method already **is** a weighted forecast, just under a different name); `forecastByRepresentative()` built for real on the same weighted-pipeline formula, parameterized by the real `owner_id` dimension `Opportunity` already has (returns the full per-rep breakdown when no single `rep_id` is given); `adjustForecast()` built as a real, non-persisted what-if computation (scales the real current forecast by the requested factor, reports a confidence delta) — mirrors how Accounting's `ScenarioPlanningService::simulate()` already does non-persisted scenario previews rather than inventing a new persistence layer for one. Only `forecastByProduct()` stays an honest documented stub: confirmed via `Opportunity::$fillable` that this module has no product/line-item dimension anywhere to group by — building one would mean designing new schema, not wiring an existing one.
  - **A severe, previously-undocumented active-breakage bug was found and fixed while wiring `EinsteinForecastingService`**: `CRMForecastingService`'s 30+ methods all filter `Opportunity` queries by `->where('tenant_id', $tenantId)` — but `crm_opportunities` has never had a `tenant_id` column at all (confirmed via `Schema::hasColumn`), unlike its siblings `crm_contacts`/`crm_leads`, which already carry one. Every one of these pre-existing forecasting methods has been a guaranteed "Unknown column" SQL error the moment anyone ever called them with a real, non-null/non-zero tenant id — dormant only because nothing in the app passed a real one through before this chantier wired `EinsteinForecastingController`'s endpoints onto real `company_id` values. Fixed with an additive migration (`crm_opportunities.tenant_id`, nullable, matching `crm_contacts`/`crm_leads`'s existing shape) plus adding it to `Opportunity::$fillable` and populating it from `company_id` in `OpportunityController::store()` (previously silently never set).
  - **A much larger, separate cross-tenant gap was found but NOT fixed in this pass — flagged for a dedicated follow-up chantier**: `ContactController`/`AccountController`/`LeadController`/`OpportunityController` (CRM's 4 core entity controllers) have **zero tenant/company scoping of any kind** on any of their `index`/`show`/`update`/`destroy` methods — confirmed via grep, not a single `company_id`/`tenant_id` filter exists anywhere across all 4. Any authenticated user of any company can currently read, edit, or delete any other company's contacts, accounts, leads, and opportunities. This is more severe than the `CallRecordingController`/`RevenueIntelligenceController` leak fixed above, but retrofitting real tenant isolation across 4 large controllers (and auditing the likely dozen+ other CRM controllers that reference these same models — `CampaignController` enrollments, `PipelineController`, `ActivityController`, `OpportunityScoringController`, etc.) is a full chantier-sized effort in its own right, not a targeted fix; left undone rather than attempted half-complete under this session's time budget, and documented here with the explicit severity it deserves rather than silently left for someone to rediscover.
  - **Sales — a severe, previously-undocumented RBAC/tenant gap on quotations, found during the systematic audit**: Chantier 8.5-light's changelog entry claims tenant-ownership scoping was added to "all 4 of `showOrder`/`updateOrder`/`confirmOrder`/`cancelOrder`'s lookups" — true for orders, but every one of the 6 quotation endpoints (`storeQuotation`/`showQuotation`/`updateQuotation`/`sendQuotation`/`convertQuotation`) plus `updateOrderStatus` had **zero** `abort_unless($user->can(...))` permission check and **zero** `SalesOrder::forTenant()`/`SalesQuotation::forTenant()` scoping at all — any authenticated user of any tenant, with no `sales.*` permission whatsoever, could view/edit/send/convert any other tenant's quotation by id, and confirm/cancel/ship/deliver any other tenant's order by id. Fixed to match the sibling order-endpoints' already-correct pattern (`abort_unless(...->can('sales.read'/'create'/'update'))` + `forTenant($this->tenantId($request))`) — no new permission strings needed, since `sales.read`/`sales.create`/`sales.update` were already real, seeded, and already used correctly elsewhere on this same controller.
- **Chantier 10 (HR + Payroll + Timesheets + Projects + Helpdesk) re-verification**: a second, cross-layer audit pass (route → controller → model → API → view → docs → security/RBAC) across these 5 modules — prompted by the same-session discovery that Accounting/CRM, both already audited in Chantier 8, still had a critical cross-tenant leak on re-check — found real, still-live gaps despite this exact group having already been through Chantiers 8.3hp/8.3pt/8.2h.
  - **Payroll cross-tenant leak, the assigned headline finding**: `PayrollController::tenantId()` read `$request->user()->tenant_id` — the well-documented phantom column (real, migrated, never in `User::$fillable`, never populated by the real registration flow) already fixed repeatedly this session (Reporting, Strategy, AI, Sales, Achats, Integration, Workflow) — despite this exact module's own Chantier 8.3 changelog entry describing `users.tenant_id` as "a genuinely live column" (that claim was itself the bug: a leftover from a chantier that hadn't yet discovered the phantom-column pattern elsewhere). Every company's payslips were silently collapsing into one shared `tenant_id=0` bucket — a payroll-officer/hr-manager/admin from Company A could list, approve, and pay Company B's payslips. Fixed `PayrollController`/`PayrollIntegrationService::generatePayslip()`'s fallback to read `company_id` instead; also added the `module:Payroll` route-group gate every sibling module already has (harmless on its own, but a `module:` gate is how the app disables a module per tenant, and Payroll's absence of it was the one asymmetry left after the tenant-id fix). Locked in with `Modules/Payroll/tests/Feature/Chantier10PayrollTenantIsolationTest.php` (2 real companies, 2 real payslips, over the real HTTP path: list/approve-batch/statistics all confirmed isolated) and a correction to the pre-existing `Chantier83PayrollZeroSalaryTest.php`'s fixtures (real `Company` records + `company_id`, not a bare `tenant_id` literal).
  - **Setup's parallel `tenant_id` usage investigated and corrected too, not left as a documented exception**: Setup's own Chantier 8.5sv entry explicitly justified reading `users.tenant_id` by citing `App\Http\Middleware\InitializeTenancyFromAuthenticatedUser`'s docblock ("this app uses a shared-DB + tenant_id scoping model"). Re-investigated end to end: that middleware's docblock describes design *intent* and its own safe-degradation behavior when `tenant_id` is absent — it is not evidence the column is ever actually populated. A repo-wide grep of every real user-creation path (`AuthController::register()`, `DemoSeeder`, every factory) confirms `users.tenant_id` is written nowhere in production code; `tenancy.user` middleware is attached to literally every module's routes (including Payroll's) and is a no-op in the overwhelmingly common case where `tenant_id` is null, so its presence there proves nothing about which column is "real". Concluded this was the exact same phantom-column bug as everywhere else, just not yet caught — fixed `SetupController`/`OnboardingMetricsController`'s shared `tenantId()` helper to `company_id`, keeping the original 8.5sv fix's "no client-controlled header fallback" property intact (so the original `X-Company-ID` IDOR this helper was written to close stays closed). All of Setup's own tests already set `company_id`/`tenant_id` to the same literal value on their test users, so this required no test changes.
  - **Timesheets — `TimesheetEntryPolicy` never registered with the Gate, active breakage**: `TimesheetsServiceProvider::boot()` only loaded migrations — no `registerPolicies()`/`Gate::policy()` call anywhere (Modules-namespaced policies don't auto-discover, same precedent as Core/BI/HR/Payroll). `TimesheetEntryController` calls `$this->authorize()` on nearly every method including `index()`'s `viewAny` — with no policy bound to `TimesheetEntry`, Laravel's Gate denies by default, so **every timesheet entry list/update/delete/approve request 403'd for every user, including admins**, not a hypothetical gap. Fixed with a new `registerPolicies()`.
  - **Timesheets — `TimesheetAdvancedController` phantom-column leak + a worse client-controlled IDOR**: `storeSheet()`/`submitPeriod()`/`utilization()` read `$request->user()?->tenant_id` (⁣`?? 1`) — same phantom-column bucket-collapse as Payroll. `revenueRecognition()` was worse: `?company_id=` was a fully client-controlled query param, letting any authenticated user read another company's revenue-recognition data just by asking for it. All four fixed to `$request->user()?->company_id ?? 0` with no client-supplied override.
  - **Projects — `prj_projects` never had any tenant/company column at all, and 3 of `ProjectAdvancedController`'s 6 *routed* endpoints were guaranteed-fatal on every call**: `portfolioKpis()` filtered `prj_projects.tenant_id`, a column that had never been migrated — a guaranteed `QueryException` on every real call, not a hypothetical gap. `portfolioTimeline()`/`portfolioResources()` called `GanttService::getTimelineOverview()`/`getResourceHeatmap()` — two methods that did not exist on that class at all — guaranteed fatal `Error` on every call. Fixed by adding `company_id` to `prj_projects` (new migration, nullable/indexed, matching this session's established pattern for this exact class of gap), rewiring `ProjectKpiService`'s tenant queries onto it, and building both missing `GanttService` methods for real (company-scoped active-project timeline; per-member logged-vs-capacity resource heatmap over `prj_team_members`, with a defensive fallback if `ts_timesheets` is absent). The 6 *un*routed `ProjectAdvancedController` methods (`index`/`store`/`show`/`gantt`/`storeTask`/`updateTask` — already documented elsewhere as deliberately never routed, since they collide with the real `ProjectController`/`TaskController`/`GanttController`) turned out to be worse than merely redundant: `store()` accepted a client-controlled `X-Tenant-ID` header fallback (the same IDOR pattern already fixed for Setup), `index()`'s tenant filter was entirely client-supplied, and `storeTask()`/`updateTask()` referenced `ProjectTask`, a model Chantier 8.4 already deleted — a guaranteed class-not-found, not just dead code. Deleted all 6 rather than patch unreachable, vulnerable code, matching this session's established dead-parallel-subsystem-deletion precedent.
  - **Projects — `ProjectController`/`TaskController` given real company-based scoping now that the data exists to scope by**: neither controller had ever had any per-company isolation concept — `index()` listed every company's projects, and `show()`/`update()`/`destroy()` had no ownership check beyond `BaseErpPolicy` (deliberately permissive by design, since it assumes some other layer already scopes "within the tenant" — which nothing did here). Since this is exactly the "underlying data/column already exists (once the migration above lands), just needs real wiring" case rather than inventing new business rules, wired it rather than leaving it as a documented gap: `index()`/`show()`/`update()`/`destroy()` now scope/check by the caller's `company_id`, `store()` populates it, and `TaskController` applies the same check via its parent project's `company_id`. Guarded with `when()`/null-safe comparisons throughout — the scoping is a no-op whenever either side lacks a real `company_id` (pre-chantier data, or a not-yet-provisioned user) — so none of Projects' existing ~200 tests needed changes; confirmed via a full `Modules/Projects/tests` run (195 passed, 1 pre-existing skip, unchanged).
  - **Dead code deleted**: `Modules/Projects/app/Http/Requests/StoreRequest.php` and `Modules/Helpdesk/app/Http/Requests/StoreRequest.php` (both zero-consumer scaffold FormRequests, matching the identical `Payroll`/`Analytics`/`Security` `StoreRequest.php` deletions from earlier chantiers). `Modules/Timesheets/resources/js/Pages/Entries/Index.vue` (a smaller Pinia-store duplicate of the richer, already-routed `TimeEntries/Index.vue`) deleted; its sibling `Projects/Index.vue` in the same directory turned out to be the opposite case — a real page (same Pinia store, real `useTimesheets()` composable, calling the real, already-tested `GET /api/v1/timesheets/projects`) that `Dashboard.vue`'s own quick-action link already expected at `/timesheets/projects`, but had no route at all — wired up instead of deleted. 5 HR Vue pages (`Performance`/`Succession`/`Recruitment`/`Learning`/`Training`) confirmed calling endpoints that have never existed in `Modules/HR/routes/api.php` (`perf-cycles`, `succession-v2`, `jobs`, `courses`, `enrollments`, `learning-paths`, …) and reachable via zero `render()` call anywhere — deleted rather than built, since ATS/recruitment/training/succession/360°-review is this module's own already-documented deliberate scope exclusion, not a wiring gap.
  - **Full 7-layer sweep otherwise came back clean for this group**: a route/controller cross-check across all 5 modules' `routes/api.php`+`routes/web.php` found zero remaining dead `[Controller::class, 'method']` references and zero controllers with no route referencing them; HR/Payroll/Helpdesk's existing policy registrations (`AppServiceProvider::$policies`, `HRServiceProvider`/`PayrollServiceProvider`/`HelpdeskServiceProvider`'s `registerPolicies()`) were re-confirmed correct, not just re-asserted. No further orphaned Vue pages (module- or root-level) were found across the 5 modules after the fixes above. Permission-string coverage was spot-checked (not modified, per this chantier's explicit "don't touch `RolesAndPermissionsSeeder.php`" scope) — no new mismatches found in these 5 modules beyond what earlier chantiers already documented/seeded; `App\Policies\LeaveRequestPolicy`/`PayrollPolicy`/`TimesheetEntryPolicy` all correctly use role checks rather than permission strings for their ID-space-sensitive abilities, so they carry no seeding-mismatch risk. `Modules/Payroll/app/Services/PayrollService.php` (zero consumers anywhere, not even a test — a second, unrelated dead scaffold service distinct from the real, live `PayrollIntegrationService`) was noticed but deliberately left alone rather than deleted, since it doesn't read the tenant_id column this chantier was scoped to fix and its own removal wasn't explicitly asked for.
- **Chantier 10 (Socle: Core + AI + Security + AuditLog + API + Integration + Validation + Shared + Settings + Setup + Workflow + Calendar) re-verification**: the third parallel group of this session's cross-layer re-audit, run alongside the Finance+Commercial and RH+Support groups above — found the same phantom-`users.tenant_id` bug family alive in 6 more modules despite it already being documented (and supposedly closed) repeatedly earlier in this file.
  - **AI — `AiUsageBudgetService`/`AiActionAdvisorController` budget-tracking bug**: every budget-check/set-limit site read `$request->user()->tenant_id ?? $request->user()->id` — since `tenant_id` is never populated, this fell through to the acting user's own `users.id`, meaning every user got their own private per-user "tenant" budget bucket instead of sharing their real company's admin-set `AiUsageLimit`. Not a cross-tenant leak (no shared/guessable fallback, so no data crossed real company boundaries) but a real functional bug: an admin's `setLimit()` call for a company never actually constrained the users who were supposed to be budget-capped. Fixed to `company_id ?? 0`, matching the fix pattern used everywhere else this session. Also fixed the separate, already-documented `requireAdmin()` phantom-`role`-column bug's sibling: `AiActionAdvisorController`'s guidance-tone helper still read `$user->role ?? 'user'` instead of `hasAnyRole()`.
  - **API — cross-tenant leak on request logs and webhooks**: `RequestLogController`/`WebhookController`'s `tenantId()` helpers both resolved to the acting user's own `id`, not `company_id` — the same "private per-user bucket" bug as AI above, applied to API request-log history and webhook configuration (not, in this case, an actual cross-company leak, since nothing fell back to a shared/attacker-controlled value — but real data isolation was accidental, one user-id collision away from breaking, and every admin's real per-company request-log view was silently empty since logs never landed under the company's own bucket). Fixed to `company_id ?? 0`. Separately, `ApiKeyController`'s main route group had `role:` gating from Chantier 8.5-light's `ApiKeyPolicy`/`WebhookPolicy` fix but no `module:API` gate at all — added, matching the standard pattern.
  - **Core — Secrets vault had 2 real bugs, one an active-authorization bypass**: `SecretsService::getTenantId()` resolved `$user->tenant_id ?? 'default'` — the phantom column, so **every tenant's secrets vault silently collapsed into one shared `'default'` bucket**, meaning any user of any company could read/list/rotate any other company's stored secrets by name collision (a real cross-tenant leak, unlike the AI/API instances above, since `'default'` is a real shared fallback every tenant hits). Fixed to `(string) ($user->company_id ?? '0')` (cast to string — `core_secrets.tenant_id` is `string(36)`), mirrored into `SecretAccessControl`/`SecretRotationManager` and `routes/secrets.php`'s inline closures, which all had the identical bug independently (no single shared helper). Separately, `SecretsController::show()`'s single-secret-read endpoint had `SecretAccessControl::canAccessSecret()` fully written (admin/super-admin bypass, else an explicit per-user `SecretAccessGrant` with the `'read'` scope) but never called — any authenticated user of the (now-correctly-scoped) tenant could read any secret in the vault regardless of grant. Fixed by wiring the check in.
  - **Achats (Stock+Logistique group, not Socle, but resolved by the same reviewer pass) — the headline finding: silent purchase-order/RFQ/receipt line-item data loss on every real save**: `PurchaseOrders/Form.vue`/`RFQs/Form.vue`/`PurchaseReceipts/Form.vue` all submit the whole record (header fields + a `lines` array) as one request body — but `StorePurchaseOrderRequest`/`UpdatePurchaseOrderRequest` had no validation rule for `lines` at all, and neither `PurchaseOrderService::createPurchaseOrder()` nor `PurchaseOrderController::store()`/`update()` ever read it, so **every purchase order ever created through the real UI silently ended up with zero line items** — the same bug independently for RFQs and, worse, `PurchaseReceiptController::index()`/`store()`/`recordQualityIssue()`, which were literal `// Implementation to follow` stubs, and `update()`/`destroy()`, which were routed against methods that didn't exist on the class at all (fatal "call to undefined method" on every PUT/DELETE). Fixed by validating `lines` against each line model's real `$fillable` and wiring `store()`/`update()` onto the already-real, already-tested `PurchaseOrderService::addLineItem()`/`RFQService::addLineToRFQ()`/`removeLineFromRFQ()` (update uses a delete-and-recreate strategy, matching the frontend's own no-per-line-id-tracking edit shape), and by rebuilding `PurchaseReceiptController` end-to-end against the real, already-built `PurchaseReceipts/{Index,Form,Show}.vue` pages. A second, repo-wide-pattern bug surfaced alongside: `PurchaseOrders/Form.vue`/`Suppliers/Form.vue`/`RFQs/Form.vue`/`PurchaseReceipts/Form.vue` all send `PATCH` on edit, but `routes/api.php` only ever registered `PUT` for each — a 405 on every real edit submission, undetected since no pre-existing test exercised the edit path at all; fixed with `Route::match(['put','patch'], ...)` throughout. `PurchaseOrderResource`/`RFQResource` were also missing the `lines` array entirely in their JSON shape (only a `lines_count`), so even a correctly-saved PO's edit form always started from a blank line list — added. `SupplierQuoteController::index()`/`store()` were also literal stubs (`GET supplier-quotes` returned nothing; the quote-recording endpoint authorized then did nothing) — wired onto `RFQService::recordSupplierQuote()`. `RFQs/Show.vue`'s "Compare quotes" and `Index.vue`'s "Evaluate" navigation both linked to `/rfqs/{id}/compare`, a route that never existed (404 on every click) — added as a thin closure to the real, already-self-fetching `Compare.vue` page, matching the `stock/movements`/`spend-analytics` precedent. `Compare.vue` itself called a fictional `rfqs/{id}/accept-quote`/`reject-quote` scheme — repointed to the real `SupplierQuoteController::accept()`/`reject()`. Both Achats route groups (main + the separate `v1/achats` AI-assist group) had gaps in their `module:`/`role:` gate — the main group had `role:` but no `module:Achats`, the AI-assist group had neither at all — both fixed to match the standard pattern.
  - **Settings/Shared — 2 route groups with literally zero middleware, not just a missing role tier**: `Modules/Shared/routes/api.php`'s whole group had no `auth:sanctum` at all (any unauthenticated caller could hit it), and `Modules/Settings/routes/api.php`'s had `auth:sanctum` but no `module:`/`role:` gate — both fixed with the standard `module:`+`role:employee,admin,super-admin` pattern. Both modules' existing AI-assist/settings tests created bare `User::factory()->create(['role' => 'employee'])` — the phantom `role` column, never read by the new gate — so both suites 403'd until fixed to seed `RolesAndPermissionsSeeder` and `assignRole()` a real Spatie role, the same fix pattern already applied to 20 other test files earlier this session for the identical reason.
  - **Setup — `AdminCompanyController`/`AdminModulesController` had the same phantom-`tenant_id ?? 'default'` shared-bucket bug as Core's Secrets vault**: every admin's company profile and per-tenant module-toggle state silently landed in one shared `'default'` bucket regardless of the admin's real company — fixed to `(string) ($request->user()->company_id ?? 0)`, consistent with `SetupController::tenantId()`'s already-fixed (Chantier 10, HR+Payroll group) rationale.
  - **Workflow — 2 real bugs, one silently defeating the module's own retry/dry-run features**: `WorkflowChainController`'s dry-run and retry-step endpoints both hardcoded `app(HrPayrollActionHandler::class)` as the executor regardless of the action's real module prefix, so dry-running or retrying any non-HR/Payroll/IT step in a chain (`crm.*`, `achats.*`, `notify.*`, …) silently returned `unknown_action` for every one of those steps — fixed by delegating to the real, already-used, module-aware `WorkflowEngineService::executeAction()` dispatcher every other execution path in this module already goes through. `WorkflowController`/`WorkflowScheduleController`'s scheduled-flow tenant resolution used the phantom `tenant_id ?? 1` fallback (collapsing every tenant's scheduled automation flows into one shared bucket) — fixed to `company_id ?? 0`. `HrPayrollActionHandler` also had 2 smaller correctness issues found while touching it: a disciplinary-record action claimed to persist to a `hr_disciplinary_records` table that doesn't exist anywhere in this app's HR "basique" scope (now honestly reports `persisted: false` instead of silently no-op'ing while claiming success) and a role-provisioning action's `role_permissions` mapping referenced an `'it_admin'` template with no matching seeded Spatie role — mapped to the closest real administrative role, `system-admin`.
  - **Calendar/Integration/AuditLog/Validation came back clean on re-check** — all already correctly fixed by Chantier 8.3cs/8.5-light and re-confirmed still correct; no new findings in this pass beyond the modules listed above.
- **Chantier 10 (Stock+Logistique: Inventory + Logistics) re-verification**: found the one in-scope module with **zero** `registerPolicies()` call and **zero** `authorize()` calls anywhere at all, plus 2 route-registration bugs that were guaranteed-fatal on the exact verbs they claimed to support.
  - **Inventory — a module-wide RBAC gap deeper than "missing seed", the headline finding**: `WarehousePolicy`/`StockMovementPolicy`/`StockPolicy` were all fully written (matching the established `view-any/view/create/update/delete/approve/export/archive` shape used throughout this app) but Inventory was the one in-scope module with no `registerPolicies()` call in its service provider and no `authorize()` call in any controller — every warehouse/stock-movement/stock endpoint relied solely on the route-level `role:` gate added back in Chantier 8.3il, with zero per-record check underneath it. Fixed by registering all 3 policies and wiring `authorize()` calls into `WarehouseController`/`StockMovementController`.
  - **`stock-movements` apiResource registered `update`/`destroy` against controller methods that didn't exist at all** — a fatal "call to undefined method" on every real PUT/DELETE to a stock movement, not a hypothetical gap. Since a stock movement is designed as an immutable audit-trail fact in this module (`Stock/Movements.vue` only ever `GET`s and `POST`s one, never edits/deletes), the fix was to drop those two verbs from the route registration rather than stub out edit/delete semantics that don't belong on an audit record — `GET stock-movements/{id}` (also apiResource-registered but likewise missing) was added for real, since reading a single movement's detail is a legitimate, currently-unserved need.
  - **Logistics — 2 route groups (`ai/assist` and `ShipmentVisibilityController`'s group) had zero `module:`/`role:` gate at all**, unlike every other Logistics route group (already fixed in Chantier 8.3il) — any authenticated user of any module/role could trigger AI-assist guidance or read/refresh shipment GPS visibility data regardless of role. Fixed to match the standard `module:Logistics`+`role:logistics-manager,warehouse-operator,manager,admin` pattern already used elsewhere in this module.
  - **Achats' findings from this same reviewer pass are documented above, under the Socle entry** (the Achats agent was paired with Socle for scheduling reasons, not because Achats belongs to Socle — see that entry for the purchase-order/RFQ/receipt line-item data-loss bug, the largest single finding of this whole re-verification round).
- **Chantier 10 (Pilotage: BI + Analytics + Reporting + Strategy) re-verification — corrects a stale claim from Chantier 8.6/8.7 above**: this file's own Chantier 8.6/8.7 entry (and, further back, 8.5ars) claimed Strategy's tenant-scoping was already fixed for real. **It was not, or not completely**: a Chantier 8.6 pass had correctly removed the client-controlled `X-Tenant-Id` header/query-param fallback (a real improvement) but replaced it with `$request->user()?->tenant_id ?? 'default'` across all 9 API controllers plus `Web/StrategyPageController` and `CascadeController` — and, despite that pass's own code comment explicitly asserting `users.tenant_id` was "the correct, non-client-controlled column", it is the exact same phantom column documented and fixed repeatedly elsewhere in this file (real DB column, never in `User::$fillable`, never populated by any real registration/onboarding path). The practical effect: every company's strategic plans, KPIs, ratios, alerts, signals, rituals, scenarios, OKRs, and cascade map continued silently collapsing into one shared `'default'` bucket after the 8.6 "fix" — a live cross-tenant leak that survived one full remediation pass undetected, because the pass fixed the visible symptom (client-controlled header) without checking whether its own replacement value was real. Fixed by rewiring every `tenantId()` helper across all touched files to the real `company_id` boundary column, matching `StrategyPlanController::tenantId()`'s already-correct precedent elsewhere in the module. Also found and fixed while touching this: `StrategyPlanController` had full CRUD (`index`/`store`/`show`/`update`/`destroy`/`tree`/`duplicate`/`health`) with **zero Policy class at all** — unlike `RatioController` (read-only, so its always-true `RatioPolicy` abilities are a deliberate no-op) or `KpiController` (already policy-backed) — meaning `employee` (this app's broad by-design role) could delete any company's strategic plan with no per-record check beyond the outer route gate; new `StrategyPlanPolicy` (mirroring `StrategyKpiPolicy`/`RatioPolicy`'s existing convention) closes it, registered in `StrategyServiceProvider::registerPolicies()`.
  - **BI — a second, independently-discovered active-authorization-bypass in `EmbedController::createToken()`, plus the dead-code-elimination find behind it**: the dashboard-ownership guard read `isset($dashboard->tenant_id) && $dashboard->tenant_id !== ($user->tenant_id ?? $dashboard->tenant_id)` — two compounding bugs that together made the whole check permanently vacuous: `bi_dashboards.tenant_id` is a dead scaffold column never in `Dashboard::$fillable` (so `isset()` was always false, short-circuiting the whole condition to `false`/"allow"), and even if it weren't, the `?? $dashboard->tenant_id` fallback made the comparison `$dashboard->tenant_id !== $dashboard->tenant_id`, permanently false regardless. Net effect: any authenticated manager/admin could mint an embed token for **any** dashboard by id, regardless of real ownership. Fixed with a real `company_id`-based ownership check (the actual live tenant-boundary column on `Dashboard`) and locked in with a new assertion in `EmbedTokenTest.php`. Separately, `AiBiController` had `link-objective`/`link-forecast` routes pointing at `linkObjective()`/`linkForecast()` methods that never existed on the class (fatal on every call) with zero Vue page or test anywhere ever calling either URL — deleted the routes rather than build the underlying link-storage schema and alignment-scoring logic, which was never specified anywhere; documented as a gap, not silently guessed at.
  - **Analytics/Reporting came back clean on re-check** — both already correctly tenant-scoped and RBAC-gated by Chantier 8.5ars, re-confirmed still correct; no new findings in this pass.

- **Chantier 18 — simulation financière (façon upmetrics) + états financiers Madagascar/OHADA réellement en place**: two linked requests, each with the user's explicit "7 périmètres" requirement (route, contrôleur, vue, modèle, format de données, sécurité, RBAC).
  - **Partie A — nouvelle fonctionnalité de simulation**: investigation first ruled out reusing any of the 4 pre-existing "scenario" systems already in the app — `BudgetScenario`+`ScenarioPlanningService`/`BudgetVarianceService::projectScenario()` (top-down % scaling of an existing budget, one aggregate snapshot, no period breakdown), `TreasuryScenario`/`TreasuryForecast` (a flat adjustment-factor on cash totals), `Modules\Strategy\Models\StrategyScenario` (a generic `variable_name`/`base_value`/`adjusted_value` hypothesis for KPI impact, not transactions), and `Modules\Analytics\Models\ForecastScenario`+`ForecastingEngineService` (a real, reusable time-series math library — `linearRegression`/`exponentialSmoothing`/`movingAverage` — but operating on historical numeric arrays, not hand-entered future sale/purchase assumptions with a realize action) — none matched "enter future sales/purchases, see them roll forward period-by-period, then convert some into real transactions," so a new `FinancialSimulation`/`FinancialSimulationLine` pair in Accounting was built rather than reusing/duplicating.
    - New `acc_financial_simulations`/`acc_financial_simulation_lines` tables + models. A line carries `type` (sale/purchase), an optional `product_id` (defaults price from `Product.selling_price`/`cost_price`, counterpart chart-of-account code from the product's `Category.default_sale_account_code`/`default_purchase_account_code` — Chantier 17's account-mapping columns), `recurrence` (once/weekly/monthly) + `growth_rate_percent` (compounded per cycle), and a `status` (simulated/realized) + `realized_at`/`realized_type`/`realized_id` morph — the same distinguishing-shape precedent already used by `CashFlowForecastItem.is_actual`/`reference_type`/`reference_id`.
    - **`FinancialSimulationService::project()`**: expands every line into dated occurrences across the simulation's horizon (week or month buckets, independent of the line's own recurrence granularity), anchors on today's real `FinancialReportService::balanceSheet()` (opening cash from real `BankAccount` balances, or a user-supplied override), and rolls trésorerie + capitaux propres forward period by period while holding every other Bilan rubrique flat at today's real value — a documented simplification (no AR/AP timing lag, no fixed/variable cost split), matching the same honesty precedent `ScenarioPlanningService` already carries for its own limitations. The simplified Bilan is guaranteed to balance by construction (proven algebraically, not just tested): since the real anchor already balances and both sides shift by the identical cumulative net result, Actif = Trésorerie roulante + actifs plats always equals Passif = capitaux propres roulants + passifs plats.
    - **`FinancialSimulationService::realizeLine()`**: turns "considérer une valeur simulée comme réelle" into an actual transaction — for a sale line, calls the real `SalesService::createOrder()`+`confirmOrder()`; for a purchase line, `PurchaseOrderService::createPurchaseOrder()`+`addLineItem()` (confirmed `Modules\Achats\Services\PurchaseIntegrationService` — the module's own PO↔Accounting bridge — is dead code: `generateInvoiceFromPO()` is a literal `return 0` stub and its event has zero listeners, so nothing was built on top of it). Then posts a real, balanced 2-line journal entry (debit/credit 411 Clients or 401 Fournisseurs against the resolved counterpart account), reusing the resolve-`account_code`-then-post pattern already established by `JournalEntryApiController::store()`/Chantier 15's `TreasuryImportService` — HT-only in v1, no VAT split, a documented simplification. The realized line is then excluded from `project()`'s "simulated" bucket for every period (its real effect is presumed already captured by the real ledger going forward), so nothing double-counts.
    - Purchase realization creates a real, **unapproved draft** `PurchaseOrder` (following the module's own real approval-chain workflow via `Modules\Validation\Services\ApprovalRequestService` rather than auto-bypassing it) — sale realization confirms immediately, since `SalesOrder::confirmOrder()` has no approval gate of its own to respect.
    - New `FinancialSimulationController` (CRUD + `project`/`realize`), `FinancialSimulationPolicy` (shared across both models, including a non-standard `realize` ability seeded via `ACCOUNTING_EXTRA_PERMISSIONS`), routed under the same `role:accountant,finance-manager,manager,admin` gate the rest of `Modules/Accounting/routes/api.php` already uses (this module still has no `module:` gate anywhere, an established, unchanged convention). New `Accounting/FinancialSimulation/{Index,Show}.vue` — assumption-line editor, a 3-tab (Compte de résultat / Trésorerie / Bilan simplifié) projection view with an inline SVG line chart (same no-external-library pattern as `Strategy/Ratios/Index.vue`'s sparklines), and a "Réaliser" button per line — linked from `BalanceSheet.vue`'s page actions (no central Accounting sub-nav exists in this app, matching the long-established `consolidation-hierarchies`/`SlaAutomation` URL-discoverability precedent).
  - **Partie B — les états financiers Madagascar/OHADA, three real, previously-undocumented bugs found and fixed, deeper than "just add the right presentation"**:
    1. **`FinancialReportService::balanceSheet()`/`incomeStatement()`/`cashFlow()` — the actual Accounting-module code the real `BalanceSheet.vue`/`IncomeStatement.vue` pages were *supposed* to call — was 100% broken, confirmed by actually running it**: its SQL selected `coa.sub_type`, a column that has never existed on `acc_chart_of_accounts` (confirmed via `Schema::getColumnListing`) — a guaranteed "no such column" SQL error on every real call. Fixed by dropping it from the query (`buildSection()` already tolerates its absence via `?? null`).
    2. **A second, more severe layer**: those two Vue pages don't actually call `FinancialReportService` at all — they call `GET reports/{balance-sheet,income-statement}` (`ReportController`, a *third*, separate code path), which delegates to `AccountingService::getBalanceSheet()`/`getIncomeStatement()`. `getBalanceSheet()` reads `GLAccount` — the already-documented, never-seeded dead `acc_gl_accounts` table — always returning three empty collections. `getIncomeStatement()` reads `JournalEntry.entry_type`/`.amount`, deprecated flat-entry fields the real posting path (`JournalEntryApiController::store()`, Chantier 15) never populates (it writes `debit`/`credit` on each `JournalEntryLine` instead). **The real user-facing Bilan/Compte de Résultat pages have never shown real data, regardless of any of this chantier's other fixes** — not a presentation gap, active breakage since before this session started tracking Accounting bugs.
    3. **`Modules\Reporting\Services\OhadaReportService`** (1126 lines, already SYSCOHADA-structured by account-class prefix — Actif immobilisé/Stocks/Créances/Trésorerie-actif; Capitaux propres/Dettes financières/Passif circulant/Trésorerie-passif; CA→marge→résultat d'exploitation→financier→net — exactly the presentation this chantier was asked to ensure) turned out to be **also fully broken**: `getAccountBalances()`/`getPeriodMovements()` queried `accounting_journal_lines`/`accounting_journal_entries`, a table pair with **no migration anywhere in the repo** (confirmed via `Schema::hasTable`) — every real call silently degraded to empty data via a surrounding try/catch, invisible because the service never threw. (The same nonexistent table pair is also written to by `Modules\Workflow\Services\Actions\InventoryAccountingActionHandler` — a second, separate broken code path, left undocumented-but-unfixed here since it's outside this chantier's Accounting-reports scope.) Fixed by repointing both to the real, live `acc_journal_entry_lines`/`acc_journal_entries`/`acc_chart_of_accounts` ledger (the same tables `FinancialReportService`/`JournalEntryApiController` actually write to) — `acc_journal_entries` has no tenant/company column at all (this app posts to one shared ledger, matching `FinancialReportService`'s own behavior), so the `$tenantId` parameter is kept for signature compatibility but no longer used to filter.
    4. **Fixing the phantom-table bug surfaced a second, independent bug that had been masked by the first**: `extractAccounts()`/`sumAccounts()`/`sumMovements()` iterate `foreach ($balances as $code => $amounts)` and call `str_starts_with($code, $prefix)` — but PHP auto-casts purely-numeric string array keys (`'310'`, `'401'`, …) to `int`, so the moment real (non-empty) data reached these methods for the first time, `str_starts_with()` fataled on an `int` argument. Fixed by casting `$code` back to string in all three.
    5. **Fixing *that* surfaced a third, genuine accounting-completeness gap**: `generateBalanceSheet()` only ever covered classes 1-5 — it never folded the period's net result (classes 6/7, "Résultat de l'exercice") into capitaux propres, so the Bilan could structurally never balance except in a company with zero transactions (empirically confirmed: any real posted purchase/sale immediately produced `equilibre: false`). Fixed by computing the cumulative résultat (produits classe 7 − charges classe 6, since inception through the report date) live and folding it into capitaux propres as an explicit "Résultat de l'exercice (non clôturé)" line — the same live/no-formal-closing-entry presentation used by real accounting software, since this app has no period-end closing-entry mechanism. Provably balances by construction: double-entry bookkeeping guarantees the sum of every account balance across the *whole* chart (classes 1-7) is always 0 for a balanced ledger, so Actif(2-5 debit) − Passif(1,4,5 credit) always equals exactly produits − charges.
    - **`Modules\Accounting\Http\Controllers\Api\ReportingController`** (Accounting's own, not Reporting's) gained `ohadaBalanceSheet()`/`ohadaIncomeStatement()`, pure delegation to `app(OhadaReportService::class)` — reused, not duplicated — routed at `POST accounting/financial-reports/ohada/{balance-sheet,income-statement}` under the module's existing gate (deliberately avoiding any change to Reporting's own RBAC gate). `BalanceSheet.vue`/`IncomeStatement.vue` were rewritten to call these instead of the broken `ReportController`/`AccountingService` path, render the real rubrique structure, and fix a hardcoded `EUR` currency display bug (→ `Ar`/`MGA`).
    - **A fourth, unrelated active-breakage bug found while wiring the Vue pages**: `AccountingWebController::balanceSheet()`/`incomeStatement()` existed, rendered real Vue components, but had **zero web route anywhere** — both pages were 100% unreachable from the UI regardless of any backend fix. Added `GET accounting/reports/{balance-sheet,income-statement}`.
    - `ReportsIndex.vue`'s "Bilan OHADA" quick-report tile called the generic `POST reporting/reports/{slug}/execute` executor — but the OHADA row's seeded `query_template` is literally the SQL comment `-- Handled by OhadaReportService::generateBalanceSheet()`, never real SQL, so this one tile always silently no-op'd while its 3 siblings (real SQL templates) worked. Fixed by special-casing that one tile to navigate to Accounting's own, now-real Bilan page instead of routing through the broken generic path.
    - **Left as a documented gap, not fixed**: `VatDeclaration/{Index,Show}.vue` also displays `€` instead of `Ar`, and its shape isn't confirmed to match Madagascar's real CA20 declaration form — no official reference available in this sandbox to build against, same "adaptation, not a certified reference" caveat already established for the chart of accounts and IRSA/CNaPS/OSTIE labels.
  - **Verification**: `php artisan migrate:fresh --seed` clean; full `Modules/Accounting` suite green (418 tests, no regressions) and full `Modules/Reporting` suite green (97 tests, no regressions); new `FinancialSimulationTest.php` (growth-rate compounding + per-period Bilan equilibrium proven numerically, sale/purchase realize end-to-end including the real created `SalesOrder`/`PurchaseOrder`/balanced `JournalEntry`, double-realize rejected, realized-line-excluded-from-projection, RBAC denial) and `OhadaFinancialStatementsTest.php` (delegation + real rubrique structure + balance); `npm run type-check`/`npm run build` clean; `php -l` swept across every changed PHP file.

- **Chantier 19 (Lot 1 — Finance + Commercial: Accounting, CRM, Sales) — deuxième passe de re-vérification des 7 couches, méthodologie renforcée par exécution réelle**: motivated by the user's own observation that new bugs keep surfacing every time work advances on a feature (directly evidenced by Chantier 18 finding 3 stacked bugs in Accounting/Reporting despite 3 prior code-reading-only audits — Chantiers 8.1/8.1b/10). The methodological fix: **code reading alone cannot detect a SQL query referencing a nonexistent column, a table never migrated, or a service reading a table the real write path never populates** — these only surface via empirical execution (`php artisan tinker` or a real HTTP test request against realistic seeded data), exactly how the Chantier 18 bugs were actually found. This lot re-audited Accounting (excluding the pages Chantier 18 already verified empirically), and ran CRM/Sales through empirical verification for the first time ever in this session. Executed via 3 parallel agents scoped to disjoint files, merged with zero conflicts (confirmed via `git status --porcelain=v1` across all 24 changed/new files), then re-verified together in the final merged state (not just trusted per-agent).
  - **Accounting — 9 real bugs found and fixed by actually calling the endpoints, not just reading them**:
    1. Four fully-written `AccountingWebController` methods (`journalEntries`/`currency`/`openBanking`/`lettrage`), each rendering a real Vue page, had **zero web route anywhere** — the same "real page, zero route" bug class Chantier 18 already found twice on this exact controller (`balanceSheet`/`incomeStatement`) — wired up now (`Modules/Accounting/routes/web.php`), plus the missing `open-banking/feeds/{connection}` API route.
    2. `StoreInvoiceRequest` required `total` unless `journal_id` was present (`required_without:journal_id`) — but the real `Invoices/Form.vue` never sends either `journal_id` or a top-level `total`, it sends `lines`; every real invoice creation through the actual UI 422'd. Fixed to `required_without_all:journal_id,lines`.
    3. **Line items were computed for totals but never persisted**: `InvoiceController::store()`/`update()` calculated the invoice total from the submitted `lines` array but never wrote a single `InvoiceLine` row — confirmed empirically (create an invoice with lines via the real endpoint, `line_items` relation comes back empty). Fixed with a new `syncInvoiceLines()` helper, and `InvoiceResource` now actually exposes `line_items` in its JSON shape (it never did before, so even a correctly-saved invoice's edit form always started blank).
    4. `InvoiceExportController`'s PDF export and its own blade view (`accounting/invoices/pdf.blade.php`) both referenced `$invoice->lines` — a relation that doesn't exist on `Invoice` (the real one is `lineItems()`) — a fatal error on every PDF export click, confirmed by actually triggering the export.
    5. `ReportController::ledgerMatching()`/`ledgerMatch()`/`ledgerUnmatch()` were literal stubs returning canned fake data regardless of input — rewritten against `InvoiceLine`'s real `match_ref`/`matched_by`/`matched_at` columns.
    6. `BankReconciliationService::rankCandidateMatches()` filtered a raw `DB::table('acc_journal_entries')->amount` column — the real posting flow (Chantier 15's `JournalEntryApiController`) never populates a total on the header row, only `debit`/`credit` on each `JournalEntryLine` — every candidate-match ranking silently scored against a column that's always null. Fixed to sum `lines.debit`. A second bug in the same method compared raw encrypted ciphertext for description-similarity scoring (always a mismatch since AES-CBC ciphertext differs per encryption even for identical plaintext) — fixed to compare decrypted values.
    7. `OpenBankingService::syncTransactions()` called `BankReconciliationService::importTransactions()` — a method that doesn't exist on that class at all, a guaranteed fatal error on every real sync, confirmed by calling it. Rewired to the real `importStatement()`.
    8. `OpenBankingConnectionFactory` was scaffold boilerplate mismatched to the model's real schema — rewritten.
    9. **`Modules\Accounting\Models\Company` (the multi-company consolidation-group model) had zero Policy class registered at all** — `ConsolidationController`'s every mutating action (`create`/`update`/`generateReport`/`recordTransaction`/`eliminateIntercompany`) calls `$this->authorize()`, and with no policy bound, Laravel's Gate denies by default — **an unconditional 403 for every user including `accountant`/`finance-manager`, confirmed empirically**, fully blocking the consolidation feature for its own intended users despite `ConsolidationController` being otherwise correctly written. Fixed with a new `CompanyPolicy` + `Gate::policy()` registration in `AccountingServiceProvider`, plus 3 new non-standard-verb permission strings seeded (`accounting.consolidation.{generate-report,record-transaction,eliminate-intercompany}`).
    - **Documented, not fixed**: `CurrencyController::gainLosses()` reads a real `CurrencyGainLoss` table whose only writer (`MultiCurrencyService::recordGainLoss()`) has zero callers anywhere and hardcodes `base_currency = 'EUR'` (inconsistent with this app's MGA-first design) — a business-logic decision (what should trigger a gain/loss recording, and in which base currency) rather than a routing fix, left for a future chantier.
    - New `Chantier19AccountingReauditTest.php`; full `Modules/Accounting` suite green, 428 passed.
  - **CRM — a cross-tenant isolation gap more severe than previously documented, found and fixed across two independent code paths**: `ContactController`/`AccountController`/`LeadController`/`OpportunityController`'s `index`/`show`/`update`/`destroy` had **zero company/tenant scoping of any kind** — this exact gap was already flagged in CLAUDE.md's own Chantier 10 entry ("more severe than the CallRecordingController/RevenueIntelligenceController leak... left undone... documented here with the explicit severity it deserves") but never fixed. Confirmed empirically via a real HTTP request as company B that any authenticated user of any company could read/edit/delete any other company's contacts, accounts, leads, and opportunities. Fixed by adding real scoping to all 4 controllers' `index/show/update/destroy` (plus `OpportunityController::kanban()`/`pipeline()`), populating `company_id`/`tenant_id` on `store()`.
    - **`Modules\CRM\Http\Controllers\Web\ContactWebController` had the identical gap independently** — a second, separate Inertia-rendered code path (`index`/`show`/`leads`/`accounts`/`editAccount`/`updateAccount`) with zero scoping and zero `authorize()` calls at all, confirmed via a real request as a cross-company user. Fixed the same way.
    - **`CrmAccountPolicy` was found to be a vacuous no-op, not merely "missing scoping" as CLAUDE.md's Chantier 10 note characterized it**: it compared `users.tenant_id` (the well-documented phantom column, never populated) against `crm_accounts.tenant_id` — a column that has **never existed on that table at all**, confirmed via `Schema::hasColumn`. The comparison was therefore permanently vacuous, and cross-company view/update both succeeded before the fix, confirmed empirically. `ContactPolicy`/`LeadPolicy`/`OpportunityPolicy` were also missing a real same-company check. All 4 rewritten to compare `(int)($user->company_id ?? 0) === (int)($model->company_id ?? 0)`. New migration adds `company_id` (nullable, indexed) to `crm_accounts`, which had neither `company_id` nor a working `tenant_id` before this.
    - **Documented, not fixed — a genuinely separate, chantier-sized effort, matching the established pattern of not silently expanding scope mid-fix**: `PipelineService`, `CRMForecastingService`, `EinsteinForecastingService`, `ForecastService`, `OpportunityScoringService`, `CampaignOrchestrationService` all run tenant-unfiltered `Opportunity`/`Contact` aggregates, and the `Activity`/`Pipeline` tables have no tenant column at all — auditing and fixing every one of these is out of this lot's scope, flagged here with the same severity Chantier 10's note already carried.

Fixed by carrying the quotation's total forward as a synthetic order line.

Fixed by computing and merging real tenant-scoped KPI aggregates into `meta`.

Same discipline as Lot 1: every substantial feature actually exercised via `tinker`/real HTTP requests, not re-read; every real bug fixed immediately and locked in with a Pest test calling the real route; 5 agents scoped one-per-module to avoid file conflicts, merged with zero overlap (confirmed via `git status --porcelain=v1`), then re-verified together in the final merged state.
  - **Projects — the heaviest finding of the lot: a module-wide cross-tenant data leak far more pervasive than the one Chantier 10 had already fixed**. Chantier 10 only added company-scoping to `ProjectController`/`TaskController`; every *other* Projects sub-resource controller resolved a route-bound `Project`/`Task`/`ProjectTimeLog`/`TimeEntry` with **zero ownership check anywhere**, confirmed empirically via real cross-company HTTP requests: `ProjectAdvancedController` (budget/kpis/risks — the very endpoints Lot 2's task description named), `MilestoneController`, `ProjectMemberController`, `ProjectReportController` (`authorize('view', $project)` was vacuous since `ProjectPolicy::view()` always returns `true` by design), `ProjectTeamController`, `ProjectViewsController`, `GanttController`, `EpicController`, `SprintController`, `AutomationController`, `TimeEntryController`, `TimeTrackingController`, `ResourceCapacityController::getProjectDemand`. The identical gap existed independently at the web/Inertia layer (`ProjectWebController::show/calendar/gantt/kanban/automation/epics/sprints` server-rendered another company's real project data into props with zero check). Fixed with a new shared trait, `Modules\Projects\Http\Controllers\Api\Concerns\ScopesToProjectCompany`, applied everywhere above (and `ProjectController`/`TaskController` refactored onto it too, deduplicating the original Chantier 10 fix) — same 404-not-403, null-safe degrade contract already established. Second bug: `GanttService::getResourceHeatmap()` queried a `ts_timesheets` table that has **never existed anywhere in the repo** — silently caught, so `logged_hours` has been a guaranteed 0 for every team member since Chantier 10 first wrote this method; fixed to read the real `Modules\Timesheets\Models\TimesheetEntry` (approved entries only, matching the convention `PayrollIntegrationService::calculateOvertime()` already established for this table). Third: `ProjectsAiAssistController::assist()` read the phantom `users.role` column — same bug class fixed repeatedly elsewhere. Fourth: `Projects/Index.vue`'s "Nouveau projet"/"Voir" buttons had **zero click handlers at all** — no way to reach project creation or detail from the UI — fixed with real navigation/a create modal against the already-live API. **Documented, not fixed**: `ResourceCapacityController`'s standalone `allocations` CRUD (no `company_id` on `prj_resource_allocations` at all — a genuine design question, not a routing fix); two live parallel time-tracking model stacks within Projects itself (`TimeTrackingController`→`TimeEntry` vs `ProjectTeamController`/`TimeEntryController`→`ProjectTimeLog`, both independently routed for the same real-world action, time logged via one invisible to the other) — reconciling this needs a data/product decision, flagged with full severity. 220 passed (+25 new), 1 pre-existing skip.

Two independent Carbon-3 sign-convention bugs (`diffInDays()`/`diffInMonths()` default flipped from Carbon 2's `true` to `false`) made `calculateTenure()` and the dashboard's `avg_tenure_months` KPI return **negative** values for every real employee — fixed both, plus clamped a third always-negative `open_positions` figure (driven by the confirmed-dead `hr_positions` table) to 0 rather than leaving nonsense. `EmployeeWebController::payroll()` had a real cross-tenant leak (every company's payslips, zero scoping) on the real `/hr/payroll` page — fixed to `company_id`. The `leaves` API resource's route-model binding was silently broken on every dynamic route: Laravel derived the wildcard as `Str::singular('leaves')` = **"leaf"**, which can never bind to `LeaveController`'s `$leaveRequest`-typed params — PUT/DELETE/approve/reject all "succeeded" (HTTP 200) while silently touching nothing, confirmed empirically; `approve`/`reject` also had no route registered at all, and `show` was apiResource-registered against a method that doesn't exist on the controller. Fixed with an explicit `parameters()` override + the missing routes. That fix then exposed a second, previously-masked bug: `LeaveController::index/approve/update` referenced a nonexistent `approvedBy` relation (real name `approver()`) — fixed. Also fixed: a missing `me/leave-requests` route the self-service Attendance page's quick action actually calls, and the phantom-`role` AI-assist bug. **Documented, not fixed**: HR has **zero company-based tenant isolation anywhere** — `hr_employees`/`hr_departments` have no `company_id` column at all, and `EmployeeController`/`DepartmentController`/`JobPositionController`/`LeaveRequestController`/`LeaveController`/`LeaveTypeController`/`SalaryBandController`/`SkillController` have zero scoping, confirmed empirically that a Company A hr-manager sees every company's employees — matching CRM's Lot 1 gap in scale (a module-wide retrofit: new migration + populate `company_id` on every write path + scope ~8 controllers + review dozens of tests), locked in as a standing regression fixture rather than half-fixed, flagged with the same severity as the CRM precedent. This is also what the Payroll agent (below) independently flagged from its own side. 119 passed (+14 new).
  - **Payroll — 8 real bugs, mostly downstream of the Carbon-3/tenant issues surfacing elsewhere**: `calculateProgressiveIncomeTax()` produced a **negative net salary** for an employee with no compensation record — a country's flat `fixed_tax`/`minimum_tax` floor applied unconditionally even to a correctly-zeroed gross salary (confirmed via tinker: `calculateIncomeTax(0,'SN')===300.0` before the fix); the pre-existing zero-salary regression test only ever asserted `gross_salary`, never `net_salary`, so this went undetected since Chantier 8.3hp. Fixed with a `grossSalary <= 0` guard. `generatePayslip()`'s idempotency check ignored `tenant_id` — confirmed via 2 real companies that once Company A generated a payslip for a shared employee, Company B's later call silently returned A's payslip instead of creating its own; fixed to scope the lookup by tenant too (does **not** fully close the underlying gap — see HR's documented finding above, since `Employee` itself has no real company column to scope by at the source). `postPayslipsToAccounting()` never wrote to the real ledger — bare `JournalEntry` header rows on the deprecated flat `entry_type`/`amount` fields, zero lines, invisible to the Chantier 18-fixed financial statements; rewritten onto the real header+lines scheme (debit 641, credit 421 net + 447 withholding), verified balanced. `taxesByCountry()` 500'd on every real request with data (Eloquent `Collection::groupBy()`+`only()` assumes model items, not sub-collections) — a pre-existing bug invisible only because the one test exercising this path always ran against an empty table; fixed with `->toBase()`. `Dashboard/Index.vue` had 3 stacked frontend/backend shape mismatches (paginator `.data` never unwrapped, statistics field names that never matched the real backend response, `payment_date` vs the real `paid_at` column) — every statistics card and the "Paid on" badge silently blank since the page was built; all fixed. `PayrollService::createRun()` — CLAUDE.md's own Chantier 10 note claimed "zero consumers," which the agent found stale via grep: `Modules\Workflow`'s `Phase52ActionHandler::generatePayrollRun()` genuinely calls it, with no idempotency check unlike every sibling `PayrollRun`-creating path (fixed) and a nullable-into-non-nullable `tenant_id` TypeError risk on the Workflow side (documented, out of scope). Phantom-role AI-assist bug fixed. 75 passed (+11 new).
  - **Timesheets — 12 real bugs, the module's own RBAC hole plus a fully broken create/edit flow**: `TimesheetPeriod` ("sheets") had **zero authorization anywhere** and no Policy class at all (only `TimesheetEntry` had one) — confirmed via real HTTP test that any "employee"-role user could approve/reject/submit/update any other employee's weekly timesheet; fixed with a new `TimesheetPeriodPolicy` registered in `TimesheetsServiceProvider`, wired into every affected method including the previously-unrouted-but-real `weeklyView`/`teamView` (cross-employee hours exposure). Separately, `TimeEntries/Form.vue` posted field names (`work_date`/`task_description`/`hours`) that matched **none** of `StoreTimesheetEntryRequest`'s real fields (`entry_date`/`description`/`hours_worked`) and never sent the required `employee_id` — every real submission through the UI 422'd; `project_id`/`billable_hours`/`hourly_rate` also had no validated write path at all, starving every downstream billing/utilization report of real data — fixed the whole chain (request validation, service, controller default, resource exposure, and the Vue form/list). Also fixed: broken pagination (same `meta`-vs-flat-`total` bug class as the already-documented `Warehouses/Index.vue` precedent); frontend `user.id`-vs-`employee_id` ID-space checks hiding a real owner's own Edit/Delete/Submit buttons (simplified to trust the now-real backend enforcement instead); `ProjectBillingService::getRevenueRecognition()` filtering `prj_projects` by a `tenant_id` column that was never migrated on that table (real column `company_id`, per Chantier 10) — silently degrading to 2 hardcoded demo rows on every real call; `TimesheetEntry.tenant_id` never populated on creation, silently starving `utilization()`; the browser-timer's `stop()` writing entries under the caller's raw `users.id` instead of their linked `hr_employees.id` (real collision risk with a different employee's record); a wrong duplicated `/api/v1/projects/projects` URL in 2 files; 3 dead-end navigation links (`Dashboard.vue`'s "New Entry"/"Pending Approvals", `Projects/Index.vue`'s New/View/Edit with no route or page at all — the last fixed via an in-page modal rather than building 3 unneeded pages); `department_id` validated against a nonexistent `departments` table (real: `hr_departments`); an ID-space mismatch in `MetricsController::summary()`'s department filter (`TimesheetEntry.employee_id` compared against `User.department_id` — never matched); the phantom-role AI-assist bug. 143 passed (+10 new).
  - **Helpdesk — 7 real bugs, headlined by the `HelpdeskLinkable` trait's own documented contract silently never working**: `$model->tickets` (CLAUDE.md's own worked example, `$invoice->tickets`) always returned empty for every linkable model — `TicketService::createFromSource()` wrote the raw FQCN into `source_type`, but once a morph map is registered, `MorphMany::getMorphClass()` resolves to the short alias, so the reverse relation never matched what was actually written (`raiseTicket()`'s forward relation tolerated either form, masking the bug). Confirmed and fixed end-to-end via a real `Employee::raiseTicket()` call. Every real click on a public KB Portal article 404'd — `KbPortalArticle` had no `resolveRouteBinding()` override, so implicit binding only matched numeric `id` while the real frontend always sends the slug; fixed. `AgentPerformanceController::benchmarking()`/`coaching()` 500'd on every real call via a `$ticket->messages()` relation that has never existed on `Ticket` (real: `comments()`) — the identical bug already fixed in 2 sibling files per their own code comments, just missed here; fixed. `/helpdesk/portal` fatally errored on every visit — `PortalWebController::index()` called a `KnowledgeBaseService` method that never existed and targeted the wrong KB model pair entirely; fixed. `helpdesk:check-sla-breaches` was **never actually autoloadable** — lived at `Modules/Helpdesk/Console/Commands/` while its namespace declared `app/Console/Commands` (the PSR-4 mismatch class CLAUDE.md's own Testing Requirements section warns about), and `HelpdeskServiceProvider::registerCommands()`/`registerCommandSchedules()` were literal no-op stubs — moved the file, registered it, and scheduled it via the one proven-working pattern already used by `Modules\Analytics`, confirmed via `php artisan schedule:list`. Dead `TicketResource` (zero consumers, referenced a nonexistent `$this->title` field) deleted. **Severe, previously-undocumented app-wide finding, flagged not fixed**: the root `app/Console/Kernel.php` schedule (daily backups, materialized-view refresh, sandbox expiry — everything documented as live in Chantier 14) **never actually runs at all** — `bootstrap/app.php` never binds Laravel's console kernel contract to `App\Console\Kernel`, so the framework's own empty default kernel is what's really wired; confirmed via `php artisan schedule:list` showing only the one Analytics job that uses the different, working `callAfterResolving(Schedule::class, ...)` pattern. This means **the documented daily `backup:database --s3` job has likely never actually run**. Left untouched (out of scope for a Helpdesk-only lot, and could affect the other 4 parallel agents) — escalated here for a dedicated follow-up chantier. 282 passed (+10 new).
  - **Verification (combined, merged state)**: `git status --porcelain=v1` confirmed zero file overlap across the 5 agents (each stayed entirely within its own module, only Projects touched a root-level file and that was explicitly in scope); `php -l` clean on every changed file; `php artisan migrate:fresh --seed` clean; `vendor/bin/pest Modules/{HR,Payroll,Timesheets,Projects,Helpdesk} --parallel` all re-run in the final merged codebase, matching each agent's own reported figures exactly (119/75/143/220+1skip/282); `npm run type-check`/`npm run build` clean.
  - **Not yet started**: Lots 3-5 (Socle+Achats; Stock+Logistique; Pilotage) of the same renforced-methodology re-verification, per the approved Chantier 19 plan — checkpointed with the user after this lot before proceeding. The `bootstrap/app.php` console-kernel-binding gap found by the Helpdesk agent (above) is flagged as its own priority follow-up, independent of the remaining lots.

- **Chantier 19 (follow-up — root scheduler fix)**: closes the severe app-wide finding the Helpdesk agent (above) surfaced but correctly left untouched. Root cause confirmed: this app kept a Laravel 10-style `App\Console\Kernel` (with a real, non-trivial `schedule()` — materialized-view refresh, tenant-sandbox expiry, daily `backup:database --s3`, daily `backup:cleanup`), but `bootstrap/app.php` (this app's Laravel 11+-style fluent bootstrap) never wired it up — `Illuminate\Foundation\Application::configure()` only binds a custom console kernel class if explicitly told to, which this app's `bootstrap/app.php` never did, so Laravel silently fell back to its own empty default kernel. Confirmed via `php artisan schedule:list` showing only the 2 jobs registered through the different, already-working `callAfterResolving(Schedule::class, ...)` pattern (`Modules\Analytics`, and Helpdesk's own newly-fixed job) — none of `Kernel.php`'s 4 real jobs ever appeared. Practical impact: **the documented daily backup job (Chantier 14) has likely never actually run in this app's history**, along with the materialized-view refresh and sandbox expiry. Separately confirmed (read the framework source, not just guessed) that `App\Console\Kernel::commands()` was *not* actually load-bearing for command discovery — `Application::configure()` already auto-registers `app/Console/Commands` via its own default `withCommands()` call, independent of the dead Kernel class, which is why `php artisan list` always correctly showed `backup:database`/`materialized-views:refresh`/etc. even though none of them were ever scheduled. Fixed by porting `Kernel::schedule()`'s content into a `->withSchedule(function (Schedule $schedule) {...})` call in `bootstrap/app.php` itself — the correct, idiomatic Laravel 11+ mechanism, and now the one place a schedule is registered directly (module-level jobs still use their own `callAfterResolving()` pattern, unaffected) — and deleting the now-fully-dead `app/Console/Kernel.php` (its `commands()` method was already redundant with the framework's own auto-discovery, confirmed via `vendor/laravel/framework/.../ApplicationBuilder.php`, and its `schedule()` method now lives in `bootstrap/app.php`). Verified: `php artisan schedule:list` now shows all 6 real jobs (the 2 already-working ones plus the 4 previously-dead ones); `php artisan list` still shows every command unchanged (confirming command discovery was never actually dependent on the deleted class); the existing `tests/Feature/BackupRestoreTest.php` (a real subprocess-based backup→restore round trip, Chantier 14) still passes unchanged, confirming the backup commands themselves are unaffected — only their scheduling was ever broken; `Modules/Analytics` suite re-run clean (125 passed) confirming the sibling `callAfterResolving()` scheduling pattern still coexists correctly. No test anywhere referenced `App\Console\Kernel` directly (confirmed via repo-wide grep), so nothing else needed updating.

- **Chantier 19 (follow-up — CRM Pipeline/Forecasting/Scoring/Campaign tenant isolation)**: closes the second item deliberately left out of Chantier 19 Lot 1's CRM fix ("`PipelineService`, `CRMForecastingService`, `EinsteinForecastingService`, `ForecastService`, `OpportunityScoringService`, `CampaignOrchestrationService` all run tenant-unfiltered `Opportunity`/`Contact` aggregates, and the `Activity`/`Pipeline` tables have no tenant column at all"), now explicitly requested by the user as its own chantier. Confirmed each of the 6 with real cross-company data, not just by re-reading the note.
  - **`PipelineService`**: confirmed broken (fully tenant-unfiltered `Opportunity`/`LeadStatusLog` aggregates) but also confirmed to have **zero callers anywhere in the app** — the module's real "pipeline" endpoints are served by the entirely separate `PipelineController`/`PipelineAnalyticsController`/`PipelineAnalyticsService`. Fixed rather than deleted (closing the landmine before a future caller trips it, matching this session's established precedent) — every public method now takes an optional `$companyId` and filters through it, `getPipelineVelocity()` scoping `LeadStatusLog` via its `opportunity()` relation since the log table itself has no tenant column.
  - **`CRMForecastingService`**: the real bug was one level deeper than "no filter" — `crm_forecasts.tenant_id` is a real, migrated column that was **never in `Forecast::$fillable`**, so every `generateForecast()` write silently dropped it, making `getForecastAccuracy()`'s `tenant_id` read permanently vacuous rather than a crash. Fixed by adding it to `$fillable` and writing the real `$tenantId` param instead of falling back to the phantom `auth()->user()->tenant_id`. Also found and removed `forecastByTerritory()` (zero callers) filtering `Territory.tenant_id`, a column that has **never existed on `crm_territories`** — a guaranteed SQL error if ever invoked; `crm_territories` has no tenant concept anywhere in this codebase, documented as out of scope rather than built.
  - **`EinsteinForecastingService`**: already correctly threading `company_id` through its one real caller — no code change needed once `CRMForecastingService`'s underlying `Forecast::$fillable` bug (above) was fixed, since that's what made its read paths return real per-company data instead of always-vacuous ones. Verified via real HTTP calls.
  - **`ForecastService`**: confirmed broken and more severe than "unfiltered read" — `generateForecast()`'s `updateOrCreate` match-key had no tenant dimension at all, so the common manager/"all users" case (`$userId === null`) collided across every company sharing one row: Company A generating a forecast could silently overwrite Company B's. Fixed by threading `$companyId` end-to-end (Opportunity filter, match-key, write via the now-fillable `tenant_id`); `ForecastController` now passes the caller's real `company_id`. Verified the collision no longer happens via a real cross-company HTTP test.
  - **`OpportunityScoringService`**: confirmed broken — `scoreAll()`/`leaderboard()`/`pipelineForecast()` ran fully tenant-unfiltered aggregates (single-opportunity endpoints were already safe via `OpportunityPolicy`'s existing per-record check). Fixed by scoping the three aggregate methods through `OpportunityScore`'s `opportunity()` relation against `Opportunity.tenant_id` — no new column needed, scores are 1:1 derived from an already-tenant-scoped opportunity. `ScoringRule` (shared scoring-criteria config) deliberately left global, matching the same shared-config-resource category as `acc_operation_templates`/`ProductTemplate` elsewhere in this app — not a data leak in itself.
  - **`CampaignOrchestrationService` — deleted, not fixed**: zero callers anywhere (confirmed via grep, not even a test) and, independent of tenant scoping, entirely broken against the real schema — wrote fields to `CampaignEnrollment`/`CampaignAnalytic` that were never in either model's `$fillable`, with the real migration's own docblock confirming it was built to match the real, live `CampaignController`/`CampaignPolicy` instead, explicitly not this service. Same dead-broken-parallel-subsystem pattern already documented repeatedly this session (`TerritoryManagementController`, `AbsenceManagementService`, `wh_*`/`lgx_*`). **Investigating it surfaced a real, more severe, live gap instead**: `Campaign`/`CampaignController`/`CampaignPolicy` had **zero** company/tenant scoping at all — `crm_campaigns` had no company column, and `CampaignPolicy`'s owner-or-`hasRole('admin')` check let any admin-role user (Spatie roles are global, not per-company) view/update/delete/launch/pause any other company's campaign. Fixed for real: added `company_id` to `crm_campaigns` (populated on `store()`, scoped `index()`), and a real `sameCompany()` check in `CampaignPolicy`.

New `ActivityPolicy`/`PipelinePolicy` (registered in `AppServiceProvider`).
  - **Documented, not fixed**: `Pipeline.name`'s unique validation stays global (two companies still can't both name a pipeline "Sales") — a minor pre-existing UX limitation, not a security issue, left out of scope; `ActivityTimelineService` (a second, separate Activity-related service) confirmed to have zero callers anywhere, left as genuinely dead code outside this chantier's named scope.

## Testing Requirements

- `php artisan migrate:fresh --seed` — clean run required before merging
- `vendor/bin/pest` — backend tests
- `npm run build && npm run type-check` — frontend compiles
- `php -l` across changed files — this codebase has a history of PSR-4 namespace/filename mismatches that silently drop classes from autoload (see git history for examples fixed during extraction); a broad `php -l` sweep is cheap insurance

## Méthodologie d'audit approfondi (14 couches, à partir du Chantier 32)

Le Chantier 31 a confirmé un motif récurrent à travers cette session : une fonctionnalité peut être « réelle » (code écrit, testé unitairement, parfois même routée) sans jamais être réellement **vivante** — soit parce qu'aucun code métier ne la déclenche jamais (« sans producteur », ex. `Modules\Core\Services\ApprovalService`), soit parce qu'elle est carrément morte/factice (identifiants `uniqid()`, aucune persistance réelle, ex. `Modules\Workflow\Services\ApprovalWorkflowService`). Les 7 couches historiques (route/contrôleur/vue/modèle/format de données/sécurité/RBAC) ne suffisent pas à détecter systématiquement cette classe de problème — elles confirment qu'un chemin de code *pourrait* fonctionner, pas qu'il est réellement *appelé* par autre chose que lui-même ou ses propres tests. À partir du Chantier 32, chaque module traversé par un audit profond doit passer par les **14 couches** suivantes, dans cet ordre, module par module (jamais plusieurs modules en parallèle — un seul agent/fil actif à la fois, pour garder chaque audit exhaustif plutôt que superficiel) :

1. **Route** — la route existe-t-elle réellement, avec le bon verbe HTTP, et est-elle atteinte par au moins un vrai appelant (page Vue, job planifié, webhook, CLI) ?
2. **Contrôleur** — appelle-t-il le service qu'on croit, ou un chemin mort/dupliqué ?
3. **Vue** — la page/le composant existe, est monté, et est réellement atteignable (route web + lien de navigation, pas seulement une URL directe théorique) ?
4. **Modèle/service** — exécution réelle (tinker ou requête HTTP de test), jamais une simple lecture de code.
5. **Format de données** — `Schema::getColumnListing()` comparé aux `$fillable`/requêtes réelles ; les migrations existent-elles vraiment pour chaque table qu'un modèle déclare ?
6. **Sécurité (approfondie)** — au-delà de la validation serveur de base : cloisonnement multi-tenant réel (`company_id`, jamais un header/paramètre client), IDOR sur chaque accès par id, secrets/PII jamais exposés en clair, injection (SQL brut, XSS sur contenu affiché), CSRF sur toute requête mutante.
7. **RBAC** — policy écrite **et** enregistrée sur le Gate (`registerPolicies()`/`Gate::policy()` — les policies namespacées `Modules\*` ne s'auto-découvrent jamais), `authorize()` réellement appelé à chaque point de mutation, permission réellement seedée, et le rôle vérifié correspond au bon **palier** dans une chaîne à paliers (pas seulement une permission plate).
8. **Validation métier** — au-delà de la validation de requête HTTP : les règles métier réelles (seuils, transitions d'état, contraintes d'unicité/cohérence) sont-elles appliquées côté serveur de façon non contournable, ou seulement suggérées côté client ?
9. **Fake/Dead** — recherche explicite et systématique (grep des appelants, pas seulement lecture du fichier) de code orphelin : un service/contrôleur/modèle sans aucun appelant réel en dehors de lui-même et de ses propres tests ; un identifiant `uniqid()`/donnée en mémoire jamais persistée ; une classe entièrement dupliquée par une autre qui, elle, est réellement utilisée. Chaque trouvaille est classée **soit** « à activer » (brancher un vrai producteur — le motif déjà établi cette session pour Achats/Core/Workflow) **soit** « mort confirmé, à supprimer » (avec le même soin que les nettoyages Chantier 8.x/9) — jamais laissée en l'état sans décision explicite.
