10. **Relationnel (tables et FK)** — chaque `belongsTo`/`hasMany`/`morphOne` correspond-il à une vraie contrainte FK en base (ou à une absence de contrainte délibérée et documentée) ? Colonnes orphelines, morph-map incohérent avec la donnée réellement stockée (motif déjà trouvé sur `PurchaseOrder::approval()` au Chantier 31), cascades de suppression correctes (`cascadeOnDelete`/`nullOnDelete`/`restrictOnDelete` — le bon choix pour chaque relation, pas un défaut copié-collé).
11. **CORE** — le module s'appuie-t-il correctement sur les services transverses de `Modules\Core` (`AuditService`/`RecordsActivity`, `ParticipantNotificationService`, `AiContextualAssistantService`, secrets vault, tenancy) plutôt que de réinventer sa propre version divergente (le motif déjà documenté plusieurs fois : plusieurs sous-systèmes parallèles pour le même concept) ?
12. **API** — la forme de réponse (Resource/JSON) est-elle stable et cohérente avec ce que le frontend attend réellement (grep le fichier `.vue`, pas supposé) ? Versionnement, pagination, codes d'erreur cohérents avec le reste de l'app.
13. **IA** — le module a-t-il une entrée dans `AiContextualAssistantService::supportedModules()` pour chacune de ses actions substantielles, avec un vrai texte de repli fr+en (motif Chantier 30), et tout service IA propre au module (ex. `StrategyAIService`, `AiDataImportService`) dégrade-t-il vraiment de façon fallback-first plutôt que de planter/retourner vide ?
14. **Import/Export, sauvegarde/restauration, génération de rapport, OKR, documentation, performance** — six vérifications complémentaires, appliquées quand pertinentes au module : (a) tout flux d'import/export en masse suit le patron preview→commit déjà établi (Chantiers 15/16) et ne perd/duplique jamais silencieusement de la donnée ; (b) toute nouvelle table du module est couverte par `SchemaSnapshotService`/`backup:restore` (générique par conception, mais à re-confirmer si un modèle a une colonne à défaut non trivial) ; (c) le module propose-t-il un export PDF/Excel réel là où un rapport de pilotage aurait du sens (suivant le patron Chantier 29) ; (d) le module alimente-t-il correctement `Modules\Strategy\Services\KPIRegistryService`/les objectifs OKR quand c'est pertinent, ou dégrade-t-il proprement (valeur de repli documentée) sinon ; (e) `docs/03-MODULES/<Module>.md` reflète-t-il l'état réel du module après correctifs, pas l'état d'avant ce chantier ; (f) contre-vérification de performance — chronométrer les endpoints réellement substantiels (agrégats, rapports, exports) contre un volume de données réaliste, et signaler (sans forcément corriger dans le même chantier, sauf si trivial) tout endpoint anormalement lent — requête N+1 non éagerload, boucle PHP sur une collection déjà chargeable en une seule requête, etc.

**Discipline d'exécution** : un seul module à la fois, jamais plusieurs en parallèle (contrairement aux lots multi-agents des Chantiers 8-20, dont l'objectif était la couverture large — celui-ci vise la profondeur). Chaque bug réel trouvé est corrigé immédiatement avec un test Pest appelant la vraie route HTTP, jamais juste documenté sans décision. Chaque module terminé reçoit sa propre entrée `CLAUDE.md` (numérotée « Chantier 32.<n> — <Module> ») et son propre commit+push avant de passer au module suivant — pas un unique commit géant en fin de série.

## Security & Compliance

RBAC via `spatie/laravel-permission` (22 roles across the 27-module scope — see `database/seeders/RolesAndPermissionsSeeder.php`). **`docs/09-RBAC-SECURITE/STANDARDS-SECURITE-MADAGASCAR.md` is the source of truth** for the actual state of every security control (encryption, audit logging, webhooks, RBAC enforcement, session/auth hardening) and for Madagascar's legal/compliance framework (Loi 2014-038, Décret 2023-1541, CMIL, Convention de Malabo/Loi 2024-004, ANSSI-Madagascar) — do not restate "AES-256-GCM" (the real cipher is AES-256-CBC) or an unqualified "GDPR/PDPL/OHADA/OWASP" compliance line here or anywhere else; link to that document instead.

## AI Assisted First

`Modules\AI\Services\AiContextualAssistantService::getGuidance()` — same contract as WideHalo (see WideHalo's docs for the full call signature). Static fallback guidance already covers CRM, Accounting, HR, Inventory, Sales for this scope. `getGuidance()` delegates to `Modules\Core\Services\AI\AIService::forModule('AI')`, which resolves whichever provider is active (`AI_DEFAULT_PROVIDER`, default `anthropic`); when that provider isn't configured or the call throws, `enabled: false` is returned with static fallback text — never an error.

**Self-hosted DeepSeek provider**: in addition to Anthropic/OpenAI, a self-hosted DeepSeek provider (served via Ollama, `docker-compose.deepseek.yml`) is available and selectable via `AI_DEFAULT_PROVIDER=deepseek` or a targeted `module_providers` override in `config/ai.php` — additive only, zero behavior change when unset. Covers both `Modules\Core\Services\AI\AIService` (used by the `<Module>AIService` business services) and `AiContextualAssistantService`. See `docs/07-DEPLOIEMENT/IA-AUTOHEBERGEE.md`.

## Africa First / Asia First

- **Accounting Standards**: OHADA/SYSCOHADA (Accounting module)
- **Mobile Money**: Orange Money, MTN MoMo connectors (`Modules/Integration/app/Services/Connectors/`)
- **Currencies**: XOF/XAF/MGA and others via `Modules/Core/app/Services/SmartDefaultsService`
- **i18n**: `lang/{ar,en,es,fr,ha,hi,mg,pt,sw,zh}.json` — `resources/js/app.js` currently wires `en, fr, pt, es` into vue-i18n; the other six are present and ready to be added to that import list when needed

## Strategy First

Ratio definitions kept for Accounting, CRM, Inventory, Sales, Helpdesk, and (basic) HR — see `Modules/Strategy/app/Services/KPIRegistryService.php`. Manufacturing-only ratios were dropped along with the module.

- **Chantier 11 — déploiement simple et documenté (clone → DNS → SSL → base de données → images Docker → application accessible)**: un audit demandé explicitly ("tout cela est-il en place via un script simple ?") confirmed nothing coherent existed — the root `Dockerfile` built cleanly in CI but `docs/07-DEPLOIEMENT/README.md` explicitly documented it as "not used in production, CI validation only"; the one real deploy path (`deploy.yml`) was a tar+SSH flow assuming an already-provisioned bare server with zero DNS/reverse-proxy/SSL step of any kind, and referenced `scripts/smoke-tests.js`, which didn't exist; `Makefile` and `setup/` (`docker-compose.yml`, `install.sh`, `nginx/default.conf`, `README.md`) were confirmed-stale WideHalo-ERP split-repo artifacts referencing `apps/api`/`webapp/`/`infrastructure/scripts/*`, none of which exist in this monolithic repo — actively misleading rather than merely unused. User picked Docker Compose + Caddy on a single VPS (generic, no hardcoded provider/domain) after an `AskUserQuestion` checkpoint, specifically because Caddy obtains/renews a Let's Encrypt SSL certificate automatically the moment DNS points at the server — zero manual certbot step.
  - **New production stack**: `docker-compose.prod.yml` (root) — 6 services, all from the existing, CI-validated root `Dockerfile` except Caddy/MySQL/Redis: `app` (php-fpm), `queue` (`queue:work`), `scheduler` (`schedule:run` loop, no system cron needed), `mysql`/`redis` (deliberately **no host port published** — only reachable by other containers on the compose network, unlike the existing dev/CI-only `docker-compose.redis.yml`, which does publish 6379 and is left as-is for that different use case), and `caddy` (the only service publishing 80/443, obtains the SSL cert for `${APP_DOMAIN}`). A small `app-publish` init container (runs once, as root, `depends_on: condition: service_completed_successfully`) copies the image's already-built `public/` into a shared named volume before `app`/`caddy` start, since the image declares no volume for it — this is what lets Caddy serve static assets directly while proxying only `*.php` requests to `app:9000` via `Caddyfile`'s `php_fastcgi` directive (with an explicit nested `root /app/public` override, since Caddy's own filesystem view of the shared volume — `/srv/public` — differs from php-fpm's, `/app/public`, a documented Caddy pattern for this exact split-container shape).
  - **`scripts/deploy.sh`** (new): the "simple script" requested — idempotent, run from a fresh clone on the target server. Checks Docker/Compose-v2 present; copies `.env.example` → `.env` and exits with the exact required variables listed if absent (no dangerous defaults silently assumed); prints an explicit DNS reminder before touching anything, since pointing the domain's A record at the server is the one prerequisite step this script cannot itself perform; brings up the full stack, generates `APP_KEY` on first run only, waits for the `app` container's healthcheck (reusing the already-real `docker/health-check.php`), runs `migrate --seed` on first install or plain `migrate --force` on redeploys (detected via `migrate:status`), then verifies `https://$APP_DOMAIN/api/health` actually responds — confirming both that Caddy obtained its certificate and that the app is serving.
  - **`scripts/smoke-tests.js`** (new — the file `deploy.yml` referenced but never had): 3 checks (`/api/health`, `/`, authenticated `/api/metrics`), non-zero exit on any failure so `deploy.yml`'s existing rollback trigger actually fires on a real post-deploy regression instead of never running at all.
  - **`deploy.yml` rewritten** to use the real Docker image instead of the tar+SSH+native-PHP path it had before (which bypassed the Dockerfile entirely, per the stale doc's own admission): a `build-and-push` job now builds and actually pushes to `ghcr.io` (the `REGISTRY`/`IMAGE_NAME` env vars were already declared in the old workflow but never once used in any step — genuinely vestigial until now), then `deploy` SSHes into the already-`scripts/deploy.sh`-initialized target server to `git pull` + `docker pull` the new image + `docker compose -f docker-compose.prod.yml up -d --no-build` + `migrate --force`; `rollback` now retags and redeploys the previous image (tagged `previous` by the deploy step) instead of symlink-swapping a tar release directory that no longer exists in this design.
  - **`docker-build.yml`** extended to also validate `docker-compose.prod.yml`/`Caddyfile` syntax on every relevant change (`docker compose config`, same pattern already used for the 2 pre-existing compose files) — confirmed passing locally before commit.
  - **Deleted `setup/` and its 5 files entirely** (not just documented as stale) — confirmed non-functional in this repo (referenced directories that don't exist here) and, once `docker-compose.prod.yml` existed, strictly redundant with it. **`Makefile` rewritten from scratch** with real targets matching this repo's actual flat layout (`make install`/`dev`/`build`/`test`/`migrate`/`seed` for local dev, `make deploy`/`up`/`down`/`restart`/`logs`/`sh` as thin wrappers around `scripts/deploy.sh`/`docker-compose.prod.yml`) — the old one's ~40 targets referenced `apps/api`, `apps/webapp-business`, `apps/webapp-ecommerce`, `apps/mobile`, `infrastructure/scripts/*.sh`, none of which have ever existed in life-mdg-erp.
  - **Docs**: new `docs/07-DEPLOIEMENT/GUIDE-DEPLOIEMENT-SIMPLE.md` is the single-page clone-to-HTTPS walkthrough this chantier was asked to produce (prerequisites, DNS A-record step, `.env` variables, the one `scripts/deploy.sh` command, verification, troubleshooting table) — linked first from `docs/INDEX.md`'s Déploiement section. `docs/07-DEPLOIEMENT/README.md` corrected on two stale claims found during the initial inventory: it said the Docker build "cannot succeed as-is" (false — already fixed and CI-confirmed, this doc just never caught up) and cited `php:8.4-fpm` (the real Dockerfile has used `8.5-fpm` since that earlier fix); its deployment section now describes the real Compose+Caddy path instead of the abandoned tar+SSH one. `ENV-PRODUCTION.md`/`CHECKLIST-GO-LIVE.md` updated with the 2 new required variables (`APP_DOMAIN`, `DB_ROOT_PASSWORD` — the latter only read by the `mysql` service, never by Laravel itself) and the now-real `scripts/smoke-tests.js` checkbox. Fixed two stale in-repo comments (`docker-compose.deepseek.yml`/`docker-compose.redis.yml`) that referenced the now-deleted `setup/docker-compose.yml` as "the only compose file that exists."
  - **Not addressed, deliberately out of scope**: provisioning the VPS itself and registering the domain are manual, non-scriptable prerequisites documented in the new guide, not automated — the user confirmed they already have both but preferred the script stay generic (no hardcoded provider/domain) rather than have either baked in.
- **Chantier 12 — bootstrap defaults for fast bulk import (chart of accounts, admin, default company/customer/supplier)**: the user asked for a fresh install to ship with realistic Madagascar-flavored defaults rather than a blank slate, so an existing tenant's real data (Excel/CSV) can be imported and worked with immediately instead of manually creating every base referential first.
  - **A real 76-account SYSCOHADA-style chart of accounts existed but was never actually seeded** — `Modules/Accounting/database/seeders/AccountingDatabaseSeeder.php` was only reachable via `TenantDefaultSeeder`/`ProvisionTenantJob`, a path already documented elsewhere in this file as broken (imports 6 excluded-module namespaces, fatals at autoload). Fixed by calling `AccountingDatabaseSeeder` directly from `database/seeders/DatabaseSeeder.php` — the seeder was already idempotent (`if (DB::table('acc_chart_of_accounts')->exists()) return;`), so this was a pure wiring fix, not new code. Adapted a handful of accounts for Madagascar specifically rather than leaving them generic-French: `431`/`437` relabelled to CNaPS/OSTIE (Madagascar's real social-security/occupational-health bodies), a new `447` account for IRSA (the Malagasy payroll withholding tax), and two new class-5 treasury accounts (`531`/`532`) for Mvola/Airtel Money — the mobile-money payment methods `SmartDefaultsService::COUNTRIES['MG']` already lists. **This is a pragmatic adaptation of the existing SYSCOHADA base, not a certified official PCG 2005 malgache reference** — flagged explicitly in the seeder's own docblock; an accountant should review before real production bookkeeping (the user was checkpointed on this exact tradeoff via `AskUserQuestion` and chose to adapt the existing base rather than rebuild from an official PCG 2005 source, which this session has no access to).
  - **Default admin account, on user request, deliberately predictable**: `DatabaseSeeder` previously seeded `admin@widehalo.com`/`Admin#Wh2025!` with only the `super-admin` role — wrong branding (WideHalo, not Life MDG) and, per the user's explicit ask, not enough roles (several views/menus branch on a specific role name via `hasRole()`, not just the Gate-level `super-admin` bypass). Changed to `admin@life-mdg.com`/`admin`, `syncRoles()`'d against every seeded role (`Role::where('guard_name','web')->pluck('name')`, so it stays in sync if `RolesAndPermissionsSeeder` gains a role later rather than needing a hardcoded list). `DemoSeeder.php`'s own duplicate `admin@widehalo.com` bootstrap (a no-op `firstOrCreate` once `DatabaseSeeder` already created the real admin) was updated to the same email/password so it doesn't spawn a second, orphaned WideHalo-branded account. Confirmed via repo-wide grep that no test hardcoded the old email/password before changing it. **This is seeded unconditionally, in every environment including production** — a deliberate tradeoff the user chose explicitly over gating it to non-production (which would leave no guaranteed first-login account at all) — `docs/07-DEPLOIEMENT/CHECKLIST-GO-LIVE.md` now has an explicit checklist item to change this password before any real production go-live.
  - **New `database/seeders/DefaultDataSeeder.php`**: a default `App\Models\Company` ("Ma Société", `MGA`, `Indian/Antananarivo`), a default `Modules\Setup\Models\CompanyProfile` (the onboarding-wizard's own distinct model — confirmed via its migration/model during this chantier's investigation — carrying `country_code`/`currency_code`, tenant-keyed off the bootstrap admin's own id matching `DatabaseSeeder`'s existing `tenant_modules` convention), a default `App\Models\Customer` ("Client par défaut"), and a default `Modules\Achats\Models\Supplier` ("Fournisseur par défaut", `MG`/`MGA`). Deliberately kept separate from `DemoSeeder.php` (French/EUR illustrative sample data, a showcase dataset, not a real starting point) — this is the actual minimal referential a real tenant about to import its own data needs to exist.
  - **Fixed a confirmed, previously-documented gap directly blocking the stated goal**: `Modules/Setup/app/Jobs/ImportDataJob.php`'s CSV/Excel import mapping targets a `full_name` field for `contacts`/`employees`, but neither `crm_contacts` nor `hr_employees` has ever had that column (both use `first_name`/`last_name`) — `bulkInsert()`'s column-intersection filter (added in an earlier chantier specifically to tolerate target-table schema mismatches) silently dropped the name on every import with no error surfaced, a real blocker for "import a mass of pre-existing contacts/employees and move fast." Fixed by splitting `full_name` on the first whitespace into `first_name`/`last_name` inside `transformRow()`'s per-field loop (new `splitFullName()` helper) rather than writing the phantom `full_name` key — a contained fix at the exact point the mapping happens, no change needed to `ENTITY_SCHEMAS` or `entityToTable()`. The deeper, adjacent field-mapping mismatch this chantier's own earlier note already flagged (`AiDataImportService::ENTITY_SCHEMAS`'s other target field names vs. real table columns beyond just names) is **not** addressed here — still an open gap, scoped narrowly to the one concrete blocker (lost names) rather than expanding into a full schema-remapping rewrite.
  - New `tests/Feature/DefaultSeedDataTest.php` locks in all of the above against a real `$this->seed()` run: admin has every seeded role, the chart of accounts + adapted accounts + 5 journals exist, the 4 new default records exist, and `splitFullName()` handles a two-word name, a single-word name, and an empty string correctly.

- **Chantier 13 — produits/matières premières par défaut, comptes de compensation, régime TVA d'une SARL non assujettie**: extends Chantier 12's bootstrap-defaults work per explicit user follow-up request, after confirming via investigation that `Modules\Inventory\Models\Product` has no raw-material/finished-good distinction of its own (`type` only differentiates `storable`/`consumable`/`service`, a stock-tracking concept) — the existing convention throughout this module is to differentiate by `Category` instead, so that's what the new defaults follow.
  - **`DefaultDataSeeder::seedInventoryDefaults()`** (new, called from the same seeder as Chantier 12's Company/Customer/Supplier defaults): 5 default `Unit`s (Pièce/Kilogramme/Litre/Mètre/Heure), 4 default `Category`s (Matières premières/Produits finis/Marchandises/Services), and one `Product` per category ("Matière première par défaut", "Produit fini par défaut", "Marchandise par défaut", "Service par défaut" — SKUs `MP-DEFAUT`/`PF-DEFAUT`/`MD-DEFAUT`/`SV-DEFAUT`, all `MGA`) — enough real referential data for a bulk Excel/CSV import to have somewhere to map "type de produit" columns onto, without inventing fake business-specific inventory the user hasn't asked for.
  - **Compensation/"variation de stock" accounts added to `AccountingDatabaseSeeder`**: confirmed via investigation that the seeded chart of accounts had balance-sheet stock accounts (310/355/370) and purchase-expense accounts (601/607) but **no compensation accounts to reconcile them at period-end** — a real, confirmed gap, not a hypothetical one. Added `6031` (Variation des stocks de matières premières), `6037` (Variation des stocks de marchandises), `7135` (Variation des stocks de produits finis), using standard SYSCOHADA/PCG numbering, directly tied to the new default raw-material/finished-good/merchandise products above.
  - **VAT-exempt SARL regime**: confirmed via investigation that **no concept of a company being VAT-exempt existed anywhere in the codebase** — `Modules\Setup\Models\CompanyProfile` had a free-text `vat_number` field but nothing recording whether the company is subject to VAT at all, and `SmartDefaultsService::COUNTRIES['MG']` returns a flat `tax_rate: 20.0`/`tax_label: 'TVA'` regardless of any company's real status. Added a `vat_exempt` boolean column (new migration, default `false`) to `setup_company_profiles`, and seeded the default company's profile with `vat_exempt: true`/`vat_number: null` — reflecting a Madagascar SARL below the VAT-registration turnover threshold, which remains liable for IR (impôt sur les bénéfices, already modeled by the pre-existing `444` chart-of-accounts entry) without collecting or deducting TVA. **Deliberately scoped to configuration + documentation, not a new cross-module enforcement engine**: `InvoiceService::addItem()`/`InvoiceController` already default an invoice line's `tax_rate` to `0` when the caller doesn't supply one (confirmed via investigation — this was already the case before this chantier, both server-side and in `Form.vue`), so the practical default behavior for this SARL was already correct; retrofitting a `CompanyProfile.vat_exempt`-driven override across every tax-rate call site in Accounting/Sales/Achats would mean inventing a real per-company tax-enforcement architecture this codebase has never had, which is out of scope for "prepare defaults" — flagged here as a real gap for a future chantier if automatic per-company tax-rate enforcement is ever wanted, rather than silently guessed at.
  - **A confirmed, unrelated real bug found during the tax investigation, fixed alongside**: `Modules\Accounting\Http\Controllers\Api\TaxController::storeEntries()` validated `entries.*.tax_rule_id` against `exists:acc_tax_rules,id` — `acc_tax_rules` was dropped as dead scaffold in an earlier chantier (see above), and `tax_rule_id` was never even a real column on `TaxEntry` in the first place (the real column is `tax_rate_id`, confirmed via the model's `$fillable`) — every real call to this endpoint would have hit a "table not found" SQL error during Laravel's `exists:` validation. Fixed to `entries.*.tax_rate_id` / `exists:acc_tax_rates,id` (the real, live table).
  - New assertions added to `tests/Feature/DefaultSeedDataTest.php`: the 4 default products exist, the 3 compensation accounts exist, and the default company profile is `vat_exempt: true` with `vat_number: null`.

- **Chantier 14 — sauvegarde/restauration compressée avec réconciliation de schéma**: the user asked for a compressed backup/restore format that, when a restored backup's structure doesn't exactly match the live schema, can trace what changed and apply sensible defaults to conform to the new format — investigation confirmed `config/backup.php` had a full `BACKUP_*`-env-var-backed shape but was **dead config, never actually read anywhere**: `backup:database`/`backup:cleanup` hardcoded their own values instead, produced a bare `.sql.gz` with no manifest, and **no `backup:restore` command existed at all** — a backup could be taken but never programmatically restored, let alone reconciled against schema drift.
  - **New `app/Services/Backup/SchemaSnapshotService.php`**: captures a structural fingerprint (`Schema::getTables()`/`Schema::getColumns()`, per table → column → `{type, nullable, default}`), diffs two snapshots (`new_tables`/`dropped_tables`/`new_columns`/`dropped_columns`), and backfills newly-added columns that have no DB-level default (nullable no-default is the case Laravel's own `ALTER TABLE` never populates on existing rows) with a conservative type-based value (`''` for strings, `0` for numerics, `false` for booleans, `now()` for dates, `'{}'` for JSON, `null`/no-op for anything not confidently mappable — never a guessed business value).
  - **`backup:database` rewritten**: now produces a single `.zip` (`data.sql.gz` + `manifest.json` — created-at, db driver, app env, git commit, applied migrations list, and the full `SchemaSnapshotService::capture()` fingerprint) instead of a bare `.sql.gz` with nothing describing what it contains. Actually reads `config('backup.disk')`/`config('backup.retention_days')`/`config('backup.s3.*')` for the first time — `--s3` remains as an explicit CLI override of the configured disk, matching the existing daily-schedule intent ("backups always go off-box") without hardcoding it. Supports both drivers this app actually uses: `sqlite` (the dev/CI/this-sandbox default — `gzencode()` of the raw `.sqlite` file bytes) and `mysql` (`mysqldump`, unchanged from before). A real local-disk path bug was found and fixed before this landed: Laravel 12's `local` filesystem disk root is `storage_path('app/private')`, not the traditional `storage_path('app')` — writing a local backup via `Storage::disk('local')` would have put it in a directory neither `backup:cleanup` (raw `storage_path('backups')` glob) nor the new `backup:restore` (raw `storage_path("backups/{$file}")` lookup) ever look at; fixed by having `backup:database` write local backups directly to the same raw `storage_path('backups/...')` path the other two commands already use, rather than routing through a `Storage` disk whose root silently diverged.
  - **New `backup:restore {file} {--force}` command**: resolves the archive (literal path → `storage_path('backups/{file}')` → S3 key+download), extracts it, restores the raw data (sqlite: disconnect → overwrite the `.sqlite` file → reconnect; mysql: `mysql` pipe from the decompressed dump), then runs `migrate --force` to bring the schema up to what the **current codebase's** migrations define — this is the reconciliation step the user asked for: any migration that shipped after the backup was taken gets applied for real, using the DB engine's own defaults wherever a migration declared one. It then diffs the manifest's schema snapshot against the post-migrate live schema via `SchemaSnapshotService`, backfills any newly-added column with no DB-level default, and prints + saves (`storage/app/private/backups/reports/restore_<timestamp>.json` — via the `local` filesystem disk, whose root in Laravel 12 is `storage_path('app/private')`) a human-readable reconciliation report (new tables, dropped tables/columns, exactly which columns were backfilled and with what value). If `migrate --force` itself fails post-restore, the command stops and reports it as needing manual intervention rather than guessing — a partially-migrated DB is not something to paper over with a default value.
  - **`backup:cleanup`**: retention days now read from `config('backup.retention_days')` (`--days` CLI flag still overrides), and its local-file glob matches both `*.zip` (new format) and `*.sql.gz` (old format) so backups made before this chantier still get cleaned up on schedule.
  - **Verified with a real backup→restore→reconciliation round trip**, not just unit-level pieces: `tests/Feature/BackupRestoreTest.php` runs the actual `backup:database`/`backup:restore` Artisan commands as genuine `php artisan` **subprocesses** (via Laravel's `Process` facade) against a dedicated file-backed sqlite database, rather than in-process against this suite's shared `:memory:` connection. That wasn't a stylistic choice — an in-process attempt using a second named DB connection was tried first and found unsafe here: Telescope's own migration hardcodes `Schema::connection(env('DB_CONNECTION'))` regardless of whatever connection is "current," so it always targets the same `sqlite` connection the rest of the suite already migrated once at boot, colliding with an already-existing `telescope_entries` table the moment a second full `migrate` runs in-process. A real subprocess sidesteps this entirely — each boots fresh, so `sqlite` cleanly resolves to the test's own temp file every time, matching how a real restore actually runs. The test: migrates a real baseline schema to a temp file, creates a small probe table with 2 rows, takes a backup, adds a **real** migration (written into the app's actual `database/migrations/`, deleted in a `finally` block) that adds a nullable no-default column, applies it plus a 3rd row, then restores the earlier backup and asserts the 3rd (post-backup) row did not survive, the drift column exists again (`migrate --force` re-applied the real migration file), both restored rows were backfilled to `''` rather than left `NULL`, and the saved JSON report reflects it.
  - **Confirmed, not fixed — a real gap surfaced along the way**: `.env.example`/`config/backup.php` both default `BACKUP_DISK` to `s3` (deliberate production intent — daily backups going off-box), but this repo's `composer.json` has never included `league/flysystem-aws-s3-v3` — confirmed via `composer show`/`composer.lock`, no AWS S3 Flysystem adapter is installed anywhere, so **S3 storage cannot actually work today for backups or anything else that uses the `s3` disk** (`config/filesystems.php` configures one, but it has never been backed by a real adapter). This was invisible before this chantier because nothing ever actually called `Storage::disk('s3')` in a path anyone exercised — `backup:database` now genuinely tries to, and fails fast with a clear `Class "Aws\S3\S3Client" not found` rather than a silent no-op. Left as a documented gap rather than silently adding a new Composer dependency mid-chantier (this sandbox has no way to verify a fresh `composer require` resolves and locks cleanly against a real Packagist connection) — a real production deploy needs `composer require league/flysystem-aws-s3-v3` plus real AWS credentials before `BACKUP_DISK=s3`/the daily `backup:database --s3` schedule entry can actually succeed; until then, `BACKUP_DISK=local` is the only backup path that works.
  - **Not addressed, deliberately out of scope**: the pre-existing, already-commented-out `backup:binlog`/PITR (point-in-time recovery via MySQL binary logs) schedule entry in `app/Console/Kernel.php` — still just a comment, no command exists; this chantier's scope was the compressed backup/restore format and schema reconciliation, not building out binlog-based continuous recovery.

- **Chantier 15 — import d'opérations de caisse/banque avec templates comptables**: the user asked for a semi-automated posting process for imported cash-register operations (encaissement/décaissement) and bank statements, with default operation templates the user picks (or the app pre-selects) before validating — investigation confirmed this concept didn't exist anywhere: no `CashRegister`/`Caisse` domain model at all ("caisse" was only the `CAI` journal code + account `530`), no operation-template/auto-categorization-rule table anywhere in the repo, and — the blocking discovery — **the header+lines journal-entry creation path was entirely broken**: `JournalEntry` had no `lines()` relation despite `JournalEntryApiController::store()` already calling `$entry->lines()->create($line)` (a fatal `BadMethodCallException` on every real request), `reverse()` used a nonexistent `journal_entry_id` column and wrote to a nonexistent `reversed_from_id` column, and `store()`'s validated `lines.*.account_code` was never resolved to the `account_id` the line's FK actually needs. Confirmed via grep that `acc_journal_entry_lines` (and by extension the real 76-account seeded chart) had **zero real write path anywhere in the app** before this chantier — the only other journal-posting path, `AccountingService::postJournalEntry()`, writes single flat legs against the separate, never-seeded `GLAccount`/`acc_gl_accounts` table, not the real chart.
  - **Fixed the blocking bug first** (a prerequisite for building anything real on top of journal entries, not a side quest): added `JournalEntry::lines(): HasMany`, added `currency`/`posted_at`/`notes` to its `$fillable` (real columns, just never mass-assignable), and rewrote `JournalEntryApiController::store()`/`reverse()`/`index()` — `store()` now resolves each line's `account_code` to the real `ChartOfAccount` id and rejects an unbalanced entry (debit total ≠ credit total) with a 422 instead of silently persisting one; `reverse()` uses the real `entry_id` FK and links the reversal to its source via the already-real `reference_type`/`reference_id` morph columns instead of the nonexistent `reversed_from_id`; `index()`'s `account_code` filter now scopes via `whereHas('lines.account', ...)` instead of a bare-column filter that doesn't exist on `JournalEntry` at all.
  - **New `acc_operation_templates` catalogue** (migration + `OperationTemplate` model): 20 default templates seeded from `AccountingDatabaseSeeder` (French-labeled, split `encaissement`/`decaissement`, each carrying a fixed `counterpart_account_code` from the real seeded chart — e.g. `vente_comptant`→707, `reglement_fournisseur`→401, `loyer`→613, `charges_sociales`→645, `impots_taxes`→631 — plus two internal-transfer templates for caisse↔banque movements against 512) and a keyword list per template used for suggestion matching. Deliberately shared between cash and bank imports — a template only names its non-treasury counterpart leg, so the exact same catalogue serves "quel type d'opération comptable" for both, matching the user's own framing ("de la même manière").
  - **New `TreasuryImportService`**: `parseFile()` (CSV via `fgetcsv`, XLSX via the already-available `PhpOffice\PhpSpreadsheet`, header-detected date/libellé/montant columns — no dependency on Setup's unrelated `AiDataImportService`, which has no accounting entity at all), `suggest()` (deterministic French-keyword matching per row, nature inferred from amount sign, always returns a suggestion — falls back to a generic `autre_produit`/`autre_charge` template rather than leaving a row unclassified — no AI/network dependency, matching this app's fallback-first design), and `commit()` (validates the whole batch, then creates real balanced `JournalEntry`+2×`JournalEntryLine` per row inside one DB transaction — CAI journal for account `530`, BNQ for `512`/`531`/`532` — and, when a bank account is supplied, additionally creates a `BankStatement`+pre-matched `BankTransaction` via the already-real `BankReconciliationService::importStatement()`, so the new entries show up in the existing reconciliation screens rather than only living in the journal). A real bug was found and fixed while building the keyword matcher: naive `str_contains()` let the keyword "paie" (for `paiement_salaire`) falsely match inside the unrelated word "paiement" (e.g. "Paiement fournisseur..."), wrongly suggesting the salary template for a supplier payment — fixed with word-boundary regex matching (`\bpaie\b`), locked in by a regression test.
  - **New `TreasuryImportController`** (`POST accounting/treasury-imports/preview` — multipart upload, returns per-row suggestions without persisting anything; `POST accounting/treasury-imports/commit` — the only endpoint that writes) and **`OperationTemplateController`** (`GET accounting/operation-templates`), routed under the same `role:accountant,finance-manager,manager,admin` gate the rest of `Modules/Accounting/routes/api.php` already uses (this module doesn't use a `module:` gate anywhere — confirmed via grep, consistent with the rest of the file, not a gap introduced here).
  - **New `Accounting/TreasuryImport/Index.vue`** (root-level, matching this module's established root-first Inertia convention): pick a treasury account (Caisse/Banque/Mvola/Airtel Money, with an optional bank-account picker for auto-reconciliation) → upload a CSV/XLSX file → review a table of parsed rows, each with an editable template dropdown pre-filled with the suggested template and a confidence badge → validate. Reachable at `/accounting/treasury-import` (new thin `TreasuryImportWebController`) and linked from `BankReconciliation/Index.vue`'s page actions ("Importer un relevé") — the one page in the app that has nowhere else to point a "Caisse" equivalent link at, since no Caisse page exists at all yet.
  - **Verified end-to-end**, not just unit-tested: manually exercised via `php artisan tinker` (a real 4-row caisse CSV import produced 4 correctly-balanced entries in the CAI journal against the real chart of accounts; a bank-account-linked import produced a pre-matched `BankStatement`/`BankTransaction` pair and updated the bank account's `current_balance`), then locked in with `Modules/Accounting/tests/Feature/TreasuryImportTest.php` (9 tests: template listing, suggestion heuristic including the word-boundary regression, unsupported-treasury-account rejection, unknown-template-code rejection, balanced-entry commit in the right journal, bank-linked commit with pre-matching, plus 3 regression tests for the `JournalEntryApiController` fix itself — create-with-real-lines, reject-unbalanced, reverse-swaps-debit-credit).
  - **Deliberately out of scope**: no `CashRegister`/`Caisse` session/till domain model was built (the user's request was specifically about the *import/posting* workflow, not opening/closing a physical till with a float — building that would be inventing a new business concept, not wiring an existing one); no OFX/CAMT.053 bank-statement-format parsing (only CSV/XLSX, matching what a Malagasy SME's bank realistically exports and what this chantier's scope asked for); the operation-template catalogue has no CRUD UI yet (seeded defaults only, editable directly in `acc_operation_templates` for now) — a real gap if the user later wants to add/edit templates without touching the database, tracked here rather than silently built.
- **Chantier 16 — import de mouvements de stock (entrée/sortie) avec création automatique des produits inconnus**: same preview→commit pattern as Chantier 15's treasury import ("pareillement... de la même manière"), applied to warehouse stock movements — the user's explicit ask was that any product named in the import file but not yet in the catalogue gets created automatically rather than rejecting the row.
  - **Headline bug found while building the feature, fixed first**: `StockMovementController::store()` — the real, routed endpoint `Stock/Movements.vue` already posts to — called `InventoryService::recordStockMovement()`, which is a bare `StockMovement::create($data)` that only ever wrote the audit-trail row; the method that actually updates `inventory_stock.quantity` (with `lockForUpdate()` pessimistic locking and average-cost handling) is the separate `recordMovement()`, which only `BarcodeController::stockMovement()` was ever calling. Every stock movement recorded through the main UI has been silently leaving the product's real on-hand quantity untouched. Fixed by switching `store()` to call `recordMovement()` (wrapped in try/catch for the method's `\InvalidArgumentException` on negative-stock rejection → 422). Locked in with a regression test asserting a POST to `stock-movements` actually changes `inventory_stock.quantity`, not just that it returns 201.
  - **New `StockImportService`** (`Modules/Inventory/app/Services/`): `parseFile()`/`preview()` mirror `TreasuryImportService`'s CSV/XLSX parsing shape (SKU/nom/quantité/type/prix column auto-detection, French and English header variants); `commit()` resolves each row by SKU first, then case-insensitive exact name match, and — the user's explicit ask — auto-creates a `Product` for any row that matches neither, under the same `Category::firstOrCreate(['name' => 'Marchandises'])` / `Unit::firstOrCreate(['name' => 'Pièce'], ['symbol' => 'pc', 'type' => 'unit'])` defaults Chantier 13's `DefaultDataSeeder` already seeds (reused, not reinvented), with a slug-based generated SKU (collision-suffixed) when the row has none. Each row's actual stock update is delegated to the now-fixed `InventoryService::recordMovement()`, inside one `DB::transaction()` per commit so an insufficient-stock rejection on any row rolls back the whole batch rather than partially applying it.
  - **A real schema quirk found while writing the auto-creation code, not a bug but worth documenting**: `inventory_products` has both `category_id`/`unit_id` FK columns *and* separate plain-string `category`/`unit` display columns (confirmed via `Schema::getColumnListing()`) — `ProductResource` reads `$this->category` (the string) directly, falling back to the relation only if eager-loaded, and accessing `$product->category` via Eloquent's magic `__get` returns the string column, not the `category()` relation, even though a same-named relation method exists (the string column in `$attributes` wins). `Product` has no `unit()` relation at all, only the FK + string column. `createProduct()` populates all four (`category_id`+`category`, `unit_id`+`unit`) so the created product displays correctly everywhere the rest of the app already reads it, not just when explicitly eager-loaded.
  - **New `StockImportController`** (`preview()`/`commit()`, mirroring `TreasuryImportController`'s split) routed at `POST inventory/stock-imports/preview`/`commit`, inside the same `throttle:create_post` group as the existing `stock-movements` store route.
  - **New `Modules/Inventory/resources/js/Pages/Stock/Import.vue`** — placed at module level (not root), matching where the real `Stock/Movements.vue` page actually lives (confirmed via directory listing that no root-level `Inventory/Stock/` folder exists), and its Tailwind + per-component-PrimeVue-import convention rather than Accounting's root-level custom-CSS-class convention. 2-step UI: pick warehouse + upload file → review table (editable type/quantity/cost per row, a "Produit existant"/"Nouveau produit" tag per row) → commit → summary listing movements created and any auto-created products. Reachable at `/stock/import` (new `Route::get` closure in `routes/web.php`, matching the existing `stock-movements` closure pattern) and linked from a new "Importer" button on `Stock/Movements.vue` next to the existing "New Movement" button.
  - **Deliberately out of scope**: no multi-warehouse-per-file support (one warehouse chosen per import, matching the treasury import's one-treasury-account-per-import shape); no barcode-scanning integration beyond the pre-existing, unrelated `BarcodeController` path; quantities are normalized to unsigned magnitude at parse time (sign, when present, only breaks a type-inference tie) rather than accepting the more permissive signed-or-unsigned convention `RecordStockMovementRequest` still allows on the plain manual-entry endpoint — that pre-existing ambiguity on the manual endpoint was left alone (out of this chantier's scope) since fixing it doesn't affect the import feature, which never reads a signed quantity from a file.
- **Chantier 17 — catalogue de templates de produits (domaine vestimentaire) avec routage comptable suggéré, et journal de benchmarking des prix fournisseurs**: two requests handled together — (1) a reusable set of product templates covering the clothing domain the user described (matières premières: tissus, cuir, velcro, papier de doublure, tissus fonctionnels; accessoires: boutons, fermetures, fil, étiquettes; vêtements semi-finis; produits finis: t-shirt/sweatshirt/chemise + gsm thresholds), each proposing a chart-of-accounts routing by category; and (2) a price-observation log comparing the internal catalogue cost to prices seen at named China/Europe textile sourcing sites (XM Textiles, Klopman, Carrington Textiles, Marina Textil, TenCate Protective Fabrics), explicitly **not** a live scraper — the user's own framing ("cela donnerait idée sur la structure de coûts au fur et à mesure où nous utiliserons le système") describes an accumulating manual/import log, which is what this is; this app has no scraping infrastructure or API access to those sites, and building one would be fragile/ToS-risky for no real benefit over a log that sharpens with use. Verified against the user's own explicit 7-layer checklist (route, contrôleur, vue, modèle, format de données, sécurité, RBAC) for both features — see the breakdown below.
  - **Real pre-existing gap found and fixed as a prerequisite**: `shared_currencies` (`Modules\Shared\Models\Currency`, backing the already-real `CurrencyController::convert()`) had a real table and a real conversion endpoint but had never actually been seeded anywhere in the app (confirmed: 0 rows on a fresh install) — every currency lookup/conversion silently had nothing to work with. The new benchmarking feature genuinely needs MGA↔USD/EUR/CNY conversion (Africa First + Asia First: the same 11 countries `SmartDefaultsService::COUNTRIES` already supports, plus USD/EUR/CNY for China/Europe sourcing), so this was fixed rather than left as a second documented gap layered on an already-known one. `DefaultDataSeeder::seedCurrencies()` seeds all 13 with **illustrative, non-live exchange rates** (units of currency per 1 USD) — explicitly not a real FX feed (this app has none) — flagged here and in the seeder's own docblock as needing periodic admin review, the same "adaptation, not certified" caveat already used for the SYSCOHADA chart-of-accounts adaptation.
  - **Category-level chart-of-accounts routing (data format + Accounting integration)**: new nullable `default_stock_account_code`/`default_purchase_account_code`/`default_sale_account_code`/`default_variance_account_code` string columns on `inventory_categories` (codes, not FK ids — matches the existing `acc_operation_templates`/`OHADA_ACCOUNT_MAP` convention of routing by the real human-readable SYSCOHADA-adapted code rather than a foreign key into a chart that may not exist yet). Two categories the clothing domain needs and the app didn't have (Chantier 12/13 only seeded Matières premières/Produits finis/Marchandises/Services) — **Accessoires** and **Vêtements semi-finis** — were added, plus two new chart-of-accounts entries per class-6/3 SYSCOHADA-adaptation convention: `312` (Stocks d'accessoires et fournitures), `335` (Stocks en-cours — vêtements semi-finis), `602`/`6032`/`6035` (achat + compensation for the two new stock accounts). `DefaultDataSeeder` sets the routing on all 6 categories (Matières premières→310/601/6031, Accessoires→312/602/6032, Vêtements semi-finis→335/6035, Produits finis→355/701/7135, Marchandises→370/607/707/6037, Services→706 sale-only).
  - **New `ProductTemplate` model/migration/seeder** (`Modules/Inventory/database/seeders/ProductTemplateSeeder.php`): 20 templates across the 4 requested families (6 matières premières, 6 accessoires, 2 vêtements semi-finis, 6 produits finis — the last includes t-shirt/sweatshirt 250gsm+/chemise 200gsm+ exactly as named, plus polo/pantalon/veste as reasonable "etc." completions of a typical apparel lineup), each carrying a suggested `category_id`/`unit_id` and a `default_attributes` JSON placeholder (composition, gsm_min, largeur, etc. — left `null` for the user to fill in, not guessed). A new "Mètre carré" unit was added (cuir/tissus enduits are sold by m², not linear metre). Idempotent via `firstOrCreate` on `code`, run from `DatabaseSeeder` right after `DefaultDataSeeder` (depends on its categories/units existing).
  - **`Products/Form.vue` gained a template picker** (Simplicity First — smart defaults, same precedent as `SmartDefaultsService`): selecting a template pre-fills category/unit and shows the category's suggested stock/purchase/sale account codes as read-only info text, so the accounting routing is visible before the user finishes the rest of the form manually — it does not silently auto-post to Accounting, since account routing at the point of a manual product creation is advisory, not a transaction.
  - **New `SourcingBenchmark` model/migration/service/controller**: `inventory_sourcing_benchmarks` (product_id and/or product_template_id and/or a free-text `material_label`, one of the three required — enforced in `SourcingBenchmarkService::record()`; `source` constrained to the 5 named suppliers + `autre` with a required `source_name_other`; price/currency/unit/observed_at/notes). `SourcingBenchmarkService::compare(Product)` converts every observation to the product's own currency (reusing `CurrencyController::convert()`'s exact math directly against the `Currency` model rather than round-tripping through HTTP), groups by source (avg/min/max + variance % vs the real `cost_price`), and returns a monthly trend — verified end-to-end via `php artisan tinker` before writing tests (4.5 EUR/m → ~22 011 MGA, 3.8 USD/m → 17 100 MGA, both correct against the seeded illustrative rates). Missing exchange rates return `null` (not a guessed number) for that observation, matching the fallback-first design principle.
  - **New `Modules/Inventory/resources/js/Pages/Benchmark/Index.vue`** (module-level, PrimeVue+Tailwind — matching `Stock/Import.vue`'s established convention for this session's newer Inventory pages): product picker → comparison cards per source (avg/min/max, a colour-coded variance % Tag) → an SVG polyline monthly-trend sparkline (same lightweight inline-SVG approach as Strategy's `Ratios/Index.vue`, no charting library) → add-observation form → history table with delete. Reachable at `/inventory/benchmark`, linked from a new "Comparer les prix" button on `Products/Show.vue` (passes `?product_id=` for preselection).
  - **7-layer checklist, both features**: **route** — `GET inventory/product-templates`, `GET/POST inventory/sourcing-benchmarks`, `GET inventory/sourcing-benchmarks/compare/{product}`, `DELETE inventory/sourcing-benchmarks/{id}` (API) + `GET inventory/benchmark` (web); **contrôleur** — `ProductTemplateController`/`SourcingBenchmarkController` (API) + the existing web-route closure pattern; **vue** — `Products/Form.vue` picker + new `Benchmark/Index.vue`; **modèle** — `ProductTemplate`/`SourcingBenchmark` (both use `Modules\Core\Traits\RecordsActivity` for real audit-log coverage, Compliance First) + `Category`'s new columns; **format de données** — 3 new migrations (category columns, `inventory_product_templates`, `inventory_sourcing_benchmarks`), all additive; **sécurité** — server-side validation on every field (`source` constrained to a real enum, `required_if` on `source_name_other`, `before_or_equal:today` on `observed_at`, at least one of product/template/material_label enforced in the service layer, not just the request); **RBAC** — both resources added to `RolesAndPermissionsSeeder::MODULES['inventory']` (auto-seeds `view-any/view/create/update/delete` for admin/manager/employee/logistics-manager/purchasing-manager/inventory-analyst via their existing wildcard matches), `warehouse-operator` explicitly granted `product-template.view-any/view` only (useful when receiving stock, benchmarking pricing is a purchasing/analyst concern), new `ProductTemplatePolicy`/`SourcingBenchmarkPolicy` registered in `InventoryServiceProvider::registerPolicies()`, and both routes sit inside the same `module:Inventory`+`role:...` gate as the rest of this module — locked in by a regression test confirming a `sales-rep` (outside that role list) gets 403 on both.
  - **API First / Strategy First**: both features are real REST endpoints, built and verified via `php artisan tinker` before any Vue page existed. The accumulating benchmark log is deliberately designed so its `compare()` output (variance %, monthly trend) could later feed a Strategy-module cost-variance KPI without a schema change — not wired into `KPIRegistryService` in this chantier (no such ratio was requested), but the data shape supports it.
  - **Verified**: `php artisan migrate:fresh --seed` clean; `Modules/Inventory`/`Modules/Accounting`/`Modules/Shared` full suites green (376/409/74 passed, 1 pre-existing unrelated risky test) including 15 new tests covering the currency seed, the category account mapping, the seeded chart-of-accounts entries, template listing/filtering, benchmark CRUD, the RBAC role-gate denial, and the currency-conversion math end-to-end; `npm run type-check` and `npm run build` both clean; `php -l` swept across every new/changed PHP file.
  - **Deliberately out of scope**: no live scraping/API integration with the 5 named supplier sites (see above — the user's own phrasing describes an accumulating log, not a scraper); `default_attributes` values (composition, gsm, etc.) are left as `null` placeholders per template, not guessed per-material data; ~~no CRUD UI for the template catalogue itself~~ **closed in Chantier 17b below**; the benchmark comparison only targets a real catalogue `Product` (`compare()` takes a `Product`, not a template or free-text material) — comparing a not-yet-catalogued material's price history is possible via the history log (`product_template_id`/`material_label` observations are recorded) but has no dedicated comparison view yet, since there is no "internal cost" to compare against until the material becomes a real product.
- **Chantier 17b — product templates made fully editable/addable/removable**: direct follow-up to Chantier 17's own documented gap ("no CRUD UI for the template catalogue itself, seeded defaults only") — the user asked explicitly for full CRUD. Extended `ProductTemplateController` with `store()`/`update()`/`destroy()` (new `StoreProductTemplateRequest`/`UpdateProductTemplateRequest`, same validation shape as the seeder's own data — `code` unique, `family` constrained to `ProductTemplate::FAMILIES`, `category_id` required/real), all three gated by the already-registered `ProductTemplatePolicy` (Chantier 17 registered the Policy but never actually called `authorize()` from the controller since only `index()` existed at the time). `index()` gained an `include_inactive` query flag — the product-creation picker only ever wants active templates (unchanged default), while the new management page passes `include_inactive=1` so a deactivated-not-deleted template stays visible/editable. New `Modules/Inventory/resources/js/Pages/ProductTemplates/Index.vue` (module-level, PrimeVue+Tailwind, same list+modal-CRUD shape as `Categories/Index.vue` — family/category/unit selects, a raw-JSON textarea for `default_attributes` since its shape varies per family, an active/inactive toggle instead of hard-delete-only). Reachable at `/inventory/product-templates`, linked from both `Products/Form.vue`'s template picker ("Gérer les templates") and `Categories/Index.vue`'s header (a "Templates de produits" button) — the two places an admin would naturally look for it. Templates have no downstream FK from `Product` itself (a template only pre-fills the create form once, nothing persists a link afterward), so deletion is a real hard-delete, not a soft-delete — the only real dependency is `SourcingBenchmark.product_template_id`, already `nullOnDelete`. 7 new CRUD tests (create/duplicate-code-rejected/unknown-family-rejected/update-including-deactivate/inactive-excluded-from-default-listing/delete/RBAC-denial).
- **Chantier 17c — multi-warehouse inventory maturity assessment + the one real gap it found, fixed**: the user asked whether Inventory already follows multi-warehouse best practices. A dedicated read-only audit (10 dimensions: per-warehouse stock, transfers, reorder rules, bin/location granularity, cross-warehouse visibility, lot/serial tracking, warehouse hierarchy, RBAC, valuation, already-documented gaps) found the module **already covers the core of real multi-warehouse practice, not just superficially**: `inventory_stock` is keyed on `product_id+warehouse_id+location_id` (real per-site quantity/reservation/avg-cost, not a single global number); `TransferOrder` is a real, tested, routed inter-warehouse workflow with an actual in-transit state (`pending_approval→approved→in_transit→received`) plus `StockRedistributionService::autoSuggestTransfers()` for rebalancing; `Lot`/`LotTrackingController::transfer()` moves lots between warehouses for real; `ValuationService` computes FIFO/average-cost **per `product_id+warehouse_id`**, not globally — one of the module's strongest areas; `Location` (bin/emplacement) is a real, single-level granularity actually required by `PickingOrderController::store()`, not an orphaned model.
  - **The one real, worthwhile gap found and fixed**: `Modules\Inventory\Models\ReorderRule` (`product_id`+`warehouse_id`+`min_level`/`max_level`/`reorder_quantity`/`lead_time_days`) was a fully-written model with **zero consumers anywhere in the app** — the real, live reorder-automation UI (`ReorderAutomation/Index.vue`) calls `ProductController::lowStockReport()`, which compared every warehouse's stock row against the *same global* `Product.reorder_level`/`reorder_point`, so two warehouses with genuinely different sales velocity for the same product could never have their own reorder point in practice, despite the schema already modeling exactly that. **A separate, entirely dead subsystem was found and deliberately left alone rather than "fixed"**: `ReorderAutomationService` (tenant-wide auto-purchase-order generation, seasonal factors by country) is called from **no controller, route, console command, or job anywhere in the app** — improving its warehouse-awareness would have been effort spent on unreachable code, so only the live code path was touched. Fixed `lowStockReport()` to look up an active `ReorderRule` for the exact (product, warehouse) pair and prefer its `min_level` over the product-level default when one exists (response now also reports `from_warehouse_rule: bool` per line so the UI could surface which threshold applied). Also fixed, in the same file since it was the exact landmine this gap analysis was about: `ReorderRule::scopeNeedsReorder()` joined `inventory_products.quantity_on_hand` — a column that has never existed on that table (confirmed via `Schema::hasColumn`) — a guaranteed SQL error the moment this scope was ever called, which it never had been until this chantier's own new test exercised it; fixed to join the real per-(product,warehouse) `inventory_stock` row instead.
  - **Documented, not built**: two smaller gaps the same assessment surfaced were left as-is, since they're lower business value and weren't asked for — no automatic "best warehouse" allocation at order time (cross-warehouse stock visibility exists via `ProductController::stock()`, but nothing picks the optimal source warehouse for a customer order); `Warehouse.type` is free-text with no enum/hierarchy and no `company_id`/region structure (a flat list today, would matter more with virtual/3PL/multi-company warehouses).
  - **Verified**: 3 new regression tests (per-warehouse `ReorderRule` override wins over the product global, an inactive rule is correctly ignored, `scopeNeedsReorder()` no longer throws) plus the full `Modules/Inventory` suite (386 passed, 1 pre-existing unrelated risky test) and `migrate:fresh --seed` clean.

- **Chantier 20 — messagerie interne d'équipe en temps réel + extension de la notification à tous les modules**: two linked requests, both actually built (not just documented) after the user explicitly chose "développer réellement" via an `AskUserQuestion` checkpoint, with a binding constraint this chantier followed strictly: the 7 layers (route, contrôleur, vue, modèle, format de données, sécurité, RBAC) had to be empirically confirmed — real `tinker` calls and real HTTP Pest tests, not just code review — **before** touching this file or `docs/CATALOGUE-FONCTIONNALITES.md`. A 3-agent exploration up front established what already existed so this chantier wired real plumbing rather than building on a guess.
  - **Phase A — repaired existing, previously-dormant notification plumbing (a prerequisite, not new functionality)**: `app/Services/NotificationService.php::sendToUser()` was found writing `title`/`message` columns that have never existed on Laravel's real default `notifications` table (`id/type/notifiable/data/read_at`) — a guaranteed SQL error the first time any real caller reached it, dormant only because nothing did yet. Rewritten to write everything into `data` (`{title, body, meta}`), matching the one already-correct precedent in the app (`Modules\Core\Notifications\PushNotification::toDatabase()`), and to broadcast a real `App\Events\NotificationCreated` on `PrivateChannel("user.{id}")` on every send. `routes/channels.php`'s private channels (`App.Models.User.{id}`, `notifications.{tenantId}`, `dashboard.{tenantId}`, etc.) all gated on the well-documented phantom `users.tenant_id` column — fixed to `company_id`, the session-wide established fix pattern; added the missing `user.{id}` channel authorization `NotificationCreated` actually broadcasts on (distinct from the pre-existing `App.Models.User.{id}` channel, which nothing in this app subscribes to). `resources/js/Components/NotificationBell.vue` was **100% mock** (`DEMO_NOTIFICATIONS` hardcoded) despite the real `NotificationController` (`GET notifications`, `/unread-count`, `POST .../read-all`, `PATCH .../{id}/read`) already existing and working — rewritten to call the real endpoints and subscribe to `Echo.private('user.'+userId)` for a live unread-count bump. New `resources/js/Pages/Notifications/Index.vue` (`GET /notifications` web route, new) — the bell's "Voir toutes les notifications" link had nowhere real to go before this. `Modules\Workflow\Services\Actions\NotificationActionHandler::sendInAppNotification()` (the `notify.in_app` workflow-engine action) raw-inserted into `notifications` using columns that don't exist at all (`tenant_id`/`user_id`/`is_read`/`action_url`) — every dispatch of this action has always fatally errored; rewired onto the fixed `NotificationService`.
  - **Phase B — new `Modules\Core\Services\ParticipantNotificationService` + first wave of real wiring**: `notifyProcess(iterable $participants, ?User $owner, string $title, string $body, array $meta = [])` sends to every explicit participant the caller identifies (deduplicated) plus, separately, the owner's direct hierarchical superior — resolved via the real, already-existing HR reporting line (`$owner->employee?->manager?->user`, one hop, no new schema). Deliberately takes an explicit participant list rather than auto-detecting "who touched this" from the generic audit trail (`RecordsActivity`/`AuditableActions`) — a heuristic broad enough to cover every module's own notion of "participant" would drown real signal in noise; each caller (an `ApprovalRequest`'s requester+approver+history, a `Ticket`'s assignee+commenters, …) already knows its own real participants. The service does **not** auto-exclude the owner from a caller's participant list — that is documented as the caller's own responsibility, matching how all 4 real callers below already build their lists.
    - **Validation** (`Modules/Validation/app/Listeners/NotifyApprovalParticipants.php`, new): listens on the 4 real approval events (`ApprovalRequestCreated`/`ApprovalApproved`/`ApprovalRejected`/`ApprovalCompleted`) — all 4 already fired by `ApprovalRequestService` but into the void, since `Modules\Validation\Providers\EventServiceProvider` (a real class, a real populated `$listen` array) had **never actually been registered anywhere** (`module.json` only ever listed `ValidationServiceProvider`) — a pre-existing, previously-undocumented bug this chantier's own methodology caught and fixed, not something newly introduced. Fixed by registering it from `ValidationServiceProvider::register()`. Owner semantics: on creation, the owner is the *requester* (their action triggered the request), so it's the requester's manager who is kept informed — not the approver's, confirmed by an initial test-authoring mistake that assumed the opposite and was corrected once traced through the real code.
    - **Accounting**: `InvoiceApprovalService::notifyApprovers()` was a no-op with a commented-out TODO — wired for real (`User::role($role)->get()` as participants, invoice's `createdBy` as owner).
    - **HR**: `LeaveRequestObserver::created()`/`updated()` extended to notify (create: the employee's manager, via pure hierarchy resolution, zero explicit participants needed; approve/reject: the employee, owner = the approving manager).
    - **Helpdesk** (`Modules/Helpdesk/app/Observers/{Ticket,TicketComment}Observer.php`, new, registered in `HelpdeskServiceProvider::boot()`): `Ticket::created()` notifies a real assignee; `Ticket::updated()` notifies reporter+assignee on status change and the new assignee on reassignment; `TicketComment::created()` notifies reporter+assignee+prior commenters, excluding the comment's own author. **Two real bugs found and fixed empirically while building this, not caught by code review**: (1) `Ticket::booted()`'s `static::created()` hook calls `SlaService::apply()`, which itself calls `$ticket->update()` on the same in-flight instance — a nested save that runs *before* Eloquent's own `finishSave()`/`syncOriginal()` for the outer insert, so `wasChanged()` on that nested update spuriously reported every originally-set attribute (including `assignee_id`) as changed, firing a spurious second "Ticket réassigné" notification alongside the correct "Nouveau ticket assigné" one on every single ticket creation. The first fix attempt (`if ($ticket->wasRecentlyCreated) return;`) silenced the double-fire but broke a real, separate scenario — `wasRecentlyCreated` stays `true` for that PHP instance's entire lifetime, so it also wrongly suppressed a later, legitimate status-change `update()` call on the same instance. The correct fix, confirmed via a second empirical test after the first one caught the regression: `if ($ticket->exists && is_null($ticket->getOriginal('id'))) return;` — precisely targets only the nested-pre-syncOriginal case. (2) The action owner of a status-change was initially read as `$ticket->reporter` — backwards, since the reporter didn't perform the change; fixed to resolve the real acting agent via `Auth::user() ?? $ticket->assignee`, matching the same actor-resolution convention `RecordsActivity` already uses elsewhere in this app.
  - **Phase C — new `Modules/Messaging` module (real-time internal team discussion)**: followed the exact new-module registration checklist established by `Modules/Calendar` (`config/modules_statuses.json`, `composer.json` PSR-4 block, `module.json`+`MessagingServiceProvider`+`RouteServiceProvider`, `Gate::policy()` registration in `boot()` since Modules-namespaced policies never auto-discover, `RolesAndPermissionsSeeder::MODULES['messaging']`, an `AiAssistController` using `getRoleNames()->first()` never the phantom `users.role`, and — required for any Pest test targeting the module to work at all — an entry in `tests/Pest.php`'s discovery list and its `actingAsUser()` `$modules` array).
    - **Models/schema**: `msg_conversations` (`company_id`, `type` direct/group, `name` nullable, `created_by`), `msg_conversation_participants` (pivot, unique `[conversation_id, user_id]`, `last_read_at` for the unread counter), `msg_messages` (`conversation_id`, `sender_id`, `body`, `attachment_path` nullable) — 3 new tables, one migration, additive.
    - **API**: `ConversationController` (`index` — real per-conversation `unread_count` via `last_read_at`; `store` — reuses an existing direct 1:1 conversation between the same 2 participants rather than duplicating threads; `show`; `users` — a small, deliberately new company-scoped user-directory endpoint for the "new conversation" recipient picker, built rather than reused because `Modules\HR\Http\Resources\EmployeeResource` never exposes the linked `User` at all, confirmed by reading the resource before wiring the frontend to it) and `MessageController` (`index` — paginates + marks the thread read; `store` — validates `body` max 5000 chars, broadcasts `MessageSent`). New `ConversationPolicy`: `view`/`post` require real participant membership, checked via a real DB query, not a cached/derived flag.
    - **Real-time**: new `App\Events\MessageSent`... — actually `Modules\Messaging\Events\MessageSent` (`ShouldBroadcast` on `PrivateChannel("conversation.{id}")`), new `routes/channels.php` entry checking real `ConversationParticipant` membership (not a `tenant_id` shortcut). Frontend: `resources/js/Components/Messaging/MessagingLauncher.vue` (new, `QuickTicketButton.vue`'s established pattern — self-contained SFC, dropdown panel, unread badge summed across conversations, `Echo.private()` subscription per known conversation), mounted once in `AppLayout.vue`'s topbar next to `NotificationBell`; `resources/js/Pages/Messaging/Index.vue` (new, root-level matching `Notifications/Index.vue`'s convention — conversation list + active thread + a "nouvelle conversation" modal calling the new `users` endpoint), reachable at `GET /messaging`.
    - **A real, empirically-found bug in the direct-conversation-reuse query, fixed before this shipped**: `ConversationController::store()`'s duplicate-check originally used `->withCount('participants')->having('participants_count', 2)` — SQLite (this app's dev/test/CI driver) rejects a `HAVING` clause on a `withCount()` alias without an explicit `GROUP BY` ("General error: 1 HAVING clause on a non-aggregate query"), confirmed only by actually running the real Pest HTTP test for this endpoint, not by reading the code. Fixed by dropping the `HAVING` entirely and filtering the small candidate set in PHP (`->get()->firstWhere('participants_count', 2)`) — the same "SQLite is stricter than MySQL here" bug class already documented repeatedly elsewhere in this file.
  - **Phase D — the mandatory empirical 7-layer verification, run before any of Phase E below**: every layer confirmed by real execution, not re-reading code — `php artisan tinker` end-to-end (create conversation → post message → dispatch `MessageSent` → verify unread-count logic transitions 1→0 on read, all against the real schema via `Schema::getColumnListing()`), then locked in with real Pest HTTP tests: `Modules/Messaging/tests/Feature/MessagingApiTest.php` (10 tests — direct-conversation reuse, group creation, message posting + broadcast, unread-count transition, a non-participant correctly 403's on view/post to a conversation they don't belong to, unauthenticated 401, the web route renders the real Inertia component, AI-assist fallback, company-scoped user directory, message-body validation), `Modules/Core/tests/Feature/Chantier20NotificationExtensionTest.php` (11 tests covering `ParticipantNotificationService` directly plus all 4 first-wave wiring targets — Validation/HR/Helpdesk — including regression coverage for both real Helpdesk bugs found above), `Modules/Workflow/tests/Feature/Chantier20NotificationActionHandlerTest.php` (2 tests locking in the `notify.in_app` fix). The pre-existing `tests/Feature/Api/CoreNotificationsApiTest.php` (11 tests, already covering the real `NotificationController` against the real schema) re-confirmed still green, not re-written.
  - **Verified**: `php artisan migrate:fresh --seed` clean, including the new `msg_*` tables; every touched module's Pest suite re-run individually and matched its pre-chantier baseline plus exactly the new tests added here — Messaging 10 passed (new module), Validation 112 (baseline, unchanged), Accounting 428 (baseline, unchanged), HR 119 (baseline, unchanged), Helpdesk 282 (baseline, unchanged), Workflow 233 (231 baseline + 2 new), Core 389 (378 baseline + 11 new); `npm run type-check`/`npm run build` both clean; `php -l` swept across every changed/new PHP file.
  - **Deliberately scoped as a first wave, not total coverage**: the plan named 4 modules (Validation, Accounting, HR, Helpdesk) as the first wiring targets and explicitly did not claim to cover every process in every module — extending the same `ParticipantNotificationService::notifyProcess()` pattern to other modules' key processes (Achats PO approvals, Projects task reassignment, CRM opportunity stage changes, etc.) is a natural, low-risk follow-up using the exact same service, left for a future chantier rather than silently expanding this one's scope. No file-attachment upload flow was built for Messaging (`attachment_path` is a real, migrated column with no writer yet) — the user's request was for real-time team discussion + notifications, not a document-sharing feature; flagged here as the one genuinely deferred piece of the Messaging module itself.

- **Chantier 21 — nomenclature de coût (BOM devis chiffré) pour le circuit de vente à la commande**: premier chantier d'une feuille de route en 5 volets (A-E) mappant le vrai flux métier Life MDG (avant-vente → devis chiffré → acompte → achats matières → sous-traitance production → livraison → solde) sur des fonctionnalités d'application concrètes — cartographie détaillée établie avec l'utilisateur avant tout développement, confirmant explicitement ce qui existe déjà (Sales/Achats/Accounting/Inventory) vs ce qui manque (aucune notion de nomenclature de coût, de commande de production, ou d'acompte/solde structuré nulle part dans l'app). Ce chantier construit le socle (volet A) : le reste (B — cycle acompte/solde, C — commande de production simplifiée, D — traçabilité bout-en-bout, E — commandes récurrentes) est un travail futur explicitement scopé, pas encore commencé.
  - **Nouveau `Modules\Inventory\Models\{CostingSheet,CostingSheetLine}`** (tables `inventory_costing_sheets`/`inventory_costing_sheet_lines`, une migration additive) : reproduit numériquement le calcul déjà fait à la main dans les fichiers Excel de chiffrage transmis par l'utilisateur — matière + accessoires de montage + accessoires de finition + valeur ajoutée (PRINT/BRODERIE) + type lavage + main-d'œuvre (temps de gamme × coût minute) + coefficient de couverture (frais fixes) = coût de revient unitaire, plus un prix de vente suggéré basé sur une marge cible. `CostingSheet` porte un `opportunity_id` non contraint (lien souple vers `crm_opportunities`, volontairement sans FK stricte pour ne pas coupler le module Inventory au module CRM) et un système de versions/révisions (`version`/`parent_id`) pour qu'une demande de modification du client ne modifie jamais un devis déjà envoyé.
  - **`CostingSheetService`** : chaque ligne peut être libellée dans sa propre devise (fréquent pour le tissu importé) — convertie vers la devise de base de la fiche via les taux de change réels déjà seedés (`shared_currencies`, Chantier 17), avec la même logique de conversion que `SourcingBenchmarkService::convert()` (dupliquée plutôt qu'extraite, suivant le précédent déjà établi dans ce fichier — `CurrencyController::convert()` porte déjà la même logique une troisième fois). Les totaux (par section + coût de revient + prix suggéré) sont recalculés et persistés (snapshot) à chaque création/modification — jamais calculés à la volée, puisqu'un devis déjà envoyé au client doit rester figé même si les prix matière bougent ensuite. `duplicateAsRevision()` clone une fiche (nouvelle référence, version+1, statut brouillon) sans jamais modifier l'original.
  - **API** : `CostingSheetController` (`index`/`show`/`store`/`update`/`destroy`/`duplicate`), `CostingSheetPolicy` enregistrée dans `InventoryServiceProvider::registerPolicies()`, routes sous le même gate `module:Inventory`+`role:employee,logistics-manager,warehouse-operator,purchasing-manager,inventory-analyst,manager,admin` que le reste du module, `costing-sheet` ajouté à `RolesAndPermissionsSeeder::MODULES['inventory']` (auto-seed des permissions CRUD standard).
  - **Vue** : `Modules/Inventory/resources/js/Pages/CostingSheets/{Index,Form}.vue` (PrimeVue+Tailwind, même convention que les pages Inventory récentes) — liste filtrable par statut avec dupliquer/supprimer, formulaire d'édition avec lignes groupées par section et totaux affichés après chaque enregistrement (les totaux sont calculés côté serveur, pas dupliqués en JS — la fiche se recalcule via l'API à chaque sauvegarde plutôt que de réimplémenter le calcul côté client, une seule source de vérité). Routes web `/inventory/costing-sheets`, `/inventory/costing-sheets/create`, `/inventory/costing-sheets/{id}/edit`, liées depuis `Categories/Index.vue` et `Products/Show.vue` (même précédent de découvrabilité par lien croisé que `ProductTemplates`/`Benchmark`).
  - **Vérification empirique des 7 couches, faite avant toute mise à jour de documentation** (exigence explicite de l'utilisateur) : `php artisan tinker` de bout en bout (création d'une fiche avec les vraies valeurs de l'exemple "Pantalon EPI" fourni par l'utilisateur, conversion EUR→MGA confirmée, duplication en révision, recalcul après modification de ligne — tous les montants correspondent à l'ordre de grandeur des fichiers Excel joints), puis verrouillé par `Modules/Inventory/tests/Feature/CostingSheetTest.php` (9 tests : création+calcul des totaux, conversion de devise réelle, recalcul après modification, duplication en révision sans mutation de l'original, refus RBAC pour un rôle hors périmètre (`sales-rep`), rejet non authentifié, validation d'une section invalide, soft-delete, et les 3 pages web réellement atteignables avec le bon composant Inertia). Deux échecs de test réels trouvés et corrigés en cours de route n'étaient pas des bugs applicatifs mais des erreurs de test : `shared_currencies` n'est semé que par `DefaultDataSeeder`, pas par le seeder de base que `actingAsUser()` lance automatiquement (même précédent que `ProductTemplateAndBenchmarkTest`) ; `CostingSheet` utilise `SoftDeletes` (un devis déjà chiffré est une trace d'audit, pas une donnée jetable), donc une suppression ne déclenche pas la cascade FK au niveau base — comportement correct, l'assertion de test a été corrigée pour vérifier la sémantique réelle (exclusion du listing/find, pas suppression physique des lignes).
  - **Vérifié** : `php artisan migrate:fresh --seed` propre ; suite complète `Modules/Inventory` verte (397 passés = 388 de référence + 9 nouveaux, 1 test « risky » préexistant inchangé) ; `Modules/Achats` (114 passés, 4 skips préexistants) et `Modules/Shared` (78 passés) re-vérifiés sans régression malgré la nouvelle FK `inventory_costing_sheet_lines.supplier_id → achats_suppliers` ; `npm run type-check`/`npm run build` propres ; `php -l` balayé sur tous les fichiers PHP modifiés/créés.
  - **Volontairement hors périmètre de ce chantier** : aucune génération PDF/Excel de la fiche (mentionnée comme un plus dans le cadrage initial, mais pas demandée explicitement — laissé pour un futur ajout si besoin) ; aucune intégration avec `SalesQuotation` (volet D, pas encore commencé — un bouton "Générer devis" depuis une fiche approuvée est prévu mais pas construit) ; aucun champ `opportunity_id` réellement rempli automatiquement depuis une fiche CRM (le champ existe et est accepté par l'API, mais rien dans l'UI CRM ne le renseigne encore) ; les volets B (acompte/solde), C (commande de production simplifiée), D (traçabilité bout-en-bout) et E (commandes récurrentes) de la feuille de route restent des chantiers futurs distincts, chacun avec son propre modèle de données déjà détaillé avec l'utilisateur mais non implémenté.

- **Chantier 22 (volet B de la feuille de route Chantier 21) — cycle acompte/solde sur les commandes de vente et d'achat**: deuxième volet de la feuille de route Life MDG (avant-vente → devis chiffré → **acompte** → achats matières → sous-traitance production → livraison → **solde**), construit symétriquement sur `Modules\Sales\Models\SalesOrder` (côté client) et `Modules\Achats\Models\PurchaseOrder` (côté fournisseur), avec une vraie écriture comptable OHADA à chaque paiement — pas juste un pourcentage stocké.
